### Meta dev.meta.ai model catalog: Muse Glimmer, Muse Spark, and the developer API  [verifier reliability: high]

SUMMARY:
dev.meta.ai is Meta's developer portal for the hosted "Meta Model API" (base URL https://api.meta.ai/v1, Bearer key in env MODEL_API_KEY, drop-in compatible with the OpenAI SDK for Responses + Chat Completions and with the Anthropic Messages SDK; API key created in the dashboard after adding a card: https://dev.meta.ai/docs/quickstart, https://dev.meta.ai/help/accounts-and-login/sign-up). Catalog per https://dev.meta.ai/docs/models: hosted-only `muse-wonder-1.0` (flagship; $4/$0.20 cached/$20 per 1M; 1M ctx; computer use), `muse-spark-1.3` (+1.2, 1.1 and `-contributor` variants), `muse-image-1.0`, `muse-voice-transcribe-1.0`, `sam-3.1`; and two open-weight, self-host-only models: Muse Spark Open (816B/42B MoE) and Muse Glimmer (30B dense). Llama is not on the API: llama.developer.meta.com now 302-redirects to dev.meta.ai (verified) and secondary sources date the Llama API shutdown to July 6, 2026. There is no TTS model anywhere in the catalog.

Muse Spark 1.3 (closed, hosted): text+image+video+audio+PDF in, text out; 1,048,576-token context, 131,072 max output; tool calling (`tools`, parallel default true, `tool_choice` only "auto", streamed `response.function_call_arguments.delta`), structured output (Chat Completions `response_format:{type:"json_schema"}`, Responses `text.format`, strict-subset rules), streaming, search grounding, `previous_response_id` state (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/tool-calling, https://dev.meta.ai/docs/structured-output). Reasoning cannot be disabled: `reasoning_effort` in {minimal,low,medium,high,xhigh,max}; `none` returns 400; reasoning tokens billed as output (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/reasoning). Pricing $1.25 in / $0.15 cached / $4.25 out per 1M; Contributor mode $0.10/$0.002/$0.20 but Meta trains on your data and limits drop to 150 RPM; Standard limits per team 3,000 RPM / 4,000,000 TPM (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/pricing-rate-limits). A ~1280px image costs ~1,300-1,500 input tokens, max 50 images/request (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/image-understanding); video input is mp4 URL/base64/file_id, not a live stream (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/video-understanding). Third-party latency: 190-335 tok/s output, but TTFT 17-38 s at xhigh/max (Artificial Analysis) vs p95 4.5 s live traffic (LLM Stats) - reasoning-dominated, so use low effort for the intent parser. Audio understanding on 1.3 is "not fully supported" (use 1.2 or Muse Voice).

Muse Glimmer (open, Apache 2.0, released Aug 10 2026; https://research.meta.ai/blog/introducing-muse-glimmer-open-agentic-model): 29.6B dense decoder incl. a frozen 1.8B ViT-G/14 Perception Encoder; text + interleaved images in (<=4,096 visual tokens/image; video handled as frames; no audio), text out; 128K default / 131,072+ context; distilled from Muse Spark; knowledge cutoff Jan 4 2026; "Reasoning strength: low|medium|high|xhigh" set in the system prompt; tool calls in ATEM XML-ish format, no parallel tool calls (https://huggingface.co/meta-models/Muse-Glimmer-30B, https://dev.meta.ai/docs/muse-glimmer/prompting). It is NOT served on Meta Model API ("self-host only"). Hosted options: Fireworks `accounts/fireworks/models/muse-glimmer-30b` - serverless inference discontinued starting Sept 25 2026, on-demand GPUs only (H100 $8/hr), LoRA SFT free until Nov 30 2026 (rank 8, chat JSONL, trained LoRAs can only be served on on-demand deployments) (https://fireworks.ai/models/fireworks/muse-glimmer-30b, https://docs.fireworks.ai/fine-tuning/training-api/cookbook/muse-glimmer, https://fireworks.ai/pricing); OpenRouter routes base Glimmer to DeepInfra at $0.30/$1.20 (https://openrouter.ai/meta/muse-glimmer-30b); Baseten's library has no Glimmer entry (page 404s) - Glimmer on Baseten means a dedicated vLLM deployment or Baseten Training (TRL/PEFT on H100, then `baseten train checkpoint deploy` serves base+LoRA with vLLM) (https://docs.baseten.co/training/getting-started). Serving a tuned Glimmer needs vLLM >=0.28 with `--enable-auto-tool-choice --tool-call-parser muse_glimmer --reasoning-parser muse_glimmer` (https://recipes.vllm.ai/meta-models/Muse-Glimmer-30B). Local Mac: official GGUF K-Quant-17GB = 16.8 GB + 1.4 GB mmproj (19 GiB at 131k ctx) via llama.cpp b10353+ (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/muse-glimmer/quantization); Ollama `muse-glimmer:30b` (18 GB) and `30b-mlx` (19 GB) with image+tools+thinking (https://ollama.com/library/muse-glimmer); mlx-community 4/5/6/8-bit; Meta reports M5 Max 26.6->50.2 tok/s and M4 Max 23.7->37.8 tok/s with the DFlash drafter under ExecuTorch. Official post-training docs (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/muse-glimmer/fine-tuning): Axolotl QLoRA verified on 1xH100, full FSDP2 on 8xH100 (97 GB/GPU), TRL LoRA/QLoRA, TitanRL GRPO; no Apple-Silicon training path (mlx-lm merged text-only Glimmer support Aug 25 but LoRA is unverified; Unsloth claims a 24 GB NVIDIA card).

Muse Spark Open: 816B/42B MoE, text/image/audio/video in, 1M ctx, Muse Model License ("Built with Muse Spark Open" attribution; fine-tunes are Derivatives), >=700 GB VRAM NVFP4 (https://huggingface.co/meta-models/Muse-Spark-Open-816B-A42B). Hosted on Baseten Model APIs (`meta-models/muse-spark-open` at https://inference.baseten.co/v1; tool calling yes; structured outputs/JSON mode NOT supported for this model: https://docs.baseten.co/development/model-apis/overview) and Fireworks serverless (`accounts/fireworks/models/muse-spark-open`, $1.25/$0.15/$4.25). Fine-tuning recipes need 8x8 H100 - out of scope.

SAM 3.1 has two faces. (a) Hosted `sam-3.1` on POST https://api.meta.ai/v1/responses: one `input_text` short noun phrase ("person in blue shirt" style) plus one `input_image` (URL/base64) or `input_video` (URL or Files API `file_id`; MP4/MOV H.264/H.265; <=15,000 frames ~10 min per request; <=16 tracked objects/frame; `metadata.propagation_direction`), optional box/point prompts; output streams as special-token lines `<|box;x1..y2;w;h|>` + `<|mask;data|>` (`mask_encoding` lossless|one_bit, `include_confidence`), parsed by `meta-sam-parser` 0.1.0 (pure Python) / `@meta-sam/parser`; $2.50 per 1k images, $0.20 per 1k video frames (min 50 frames billed), 3,000 RPM; no published per-call latency and no live-stream mode (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/sam/segmenting, https://dev.meta.ai/docs/pricing-rate-limits). (b) Open weights facebook/sam3.1 (gated, SAM License; 848M params; released 03/27/2026; object multiplex tracks 16 objects per pass, 16->32 fps on an H100); the official repo requires Python >=3.12, PyTorch >=2.7, CUDA >=12.6 (https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam3, https://ai.meta.com/blog/segment-anything-model-3/). The HF card says SAM 3.1 has no transformers integration, but transformers ships Sam3Model/Sam3VideoModel for facebook/sam3 with a streaming per-frame API (https://huggingface.co/docs/transformers/main/en/model_doc/sam3_video); Apple-Silicon community numbers are ~1.34 s/frame (M4 Max) and 7-8 s/image on MPS, i.e. not real-time locally. Meta's cookbook includes `07_sam/*` and `03_use_cases/14_muse_robotics` (Muse Voice -> Muse Spark plan JSON -> MuJoCo; SAM with a 5-s timeout; "model-selected intents never directly control motors") (https://github.com/meta-models/meta-model-cookbook).

Muse Voice Transcribe (ASR only): `wss://api.meta.ai/v1/asr/realtime` (credential in the first JSON frame; PCM_24KHZ or PCM_16KHZ mono PCM16 ~80 ms frames; modes PUSH_TO_TALK / ENDPOINTING / DIARIZATION; events speechStart, transcript (partials), speechEnd, speechComplete; `keywords` biasing; 25 languages; 60-min sessions; 256 concurrent streams) and `POST /v1/asr/transcribe` (WAV <=32 MB / 10 min); $0.18 per audio hour (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/speech-to-text, https://dev.meta.ai/docs/api-reference/voice/realtime).

Other Meta vision models (download only, none on the API): DINOv3 (`facebook/dinov3-*`, gated DINOv3 License, transformers), V-JEPA 2 (`facebook/vjepa2-*`, MIT, transformers video encoder), Perception Encoder Core (`facebook/PE-Core-*`, Apache 2.0), CoTracker3 (`facebook/cotracker3`, CC-BY-NC-4.0, torch hub, online mode for real-time point tracking). Llama 4 weights remain downloadable but Meta no longer hosts them.

Reading "train an open Meta model and have it run on Muse Glimmer or Spark": Meta offers no hosted fine-tuning, so nothing custom can run "on" hosted Spark/Wonder. Feasible readings: (1) LoRA-fine-tune Muse Glimmer (the open model) - e.g. transcript -> strict JSON drone-command parsing - and serve it on Fireworks on-demand, Baseten dedicated, or locally (GGUF/Ollama on the M5 Pro), while hosted Muse Spark 1.3 remains the primary orchestrator; (2) skip training and use Spark zero-shot with strict JSON schema; (3) fine-tune Spark Open - infeasible. Hackathon (https://dev.meta.ai/events/global-hackathon, https://cerebralvalley.ai/e/meta-ai-developer-hackathon): build Oct 26-Nov 4, submissions due Nov 4 9:00 PM ET, judging Nov 4-8, winners Nov 9; themes include robotics; encourages Muse Spark series / Muse Image / SAM 3.1 via Model API plus "Meta open weights models"; $150 Model API credits per selected participant (Meta for Developers on X); Baseten $100 + 2M TPM, Modal $100, Fireworks credits for select participants. Judging weights (impact 30 / use of Meta tools 25 / demo 25 / technical 20) appear only in secondary sources; the Official Rules were not retrievable.

KEY CLAIMS:
- [high] Meta Model API base URL is https://api.meta.ai/v1 with Bearer auth via MODEL_API_KEY; it is drop-in compatible with the OpenAI SDK (Responses and Chat Completions) and the Anthropic Messages SDK; example model IDs muse-spark-1.3 (context 1,048,576, output 131,072). (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/quickstart)
- [high] Muse Spark 1.3 (muse-spark-1.3) is hosted-only (closed weights), accepts text, image, video, audio and PDF input, outputs text, 1,048,576-token context, supports tool calling, structured output, search grounding; Muse Glimmer and Muse Spark Open are the only open-weight entries and are 'self-host only' (not served on Meta Model API). (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/models)
- [high] Pricing: muse-spark-1.3 $1.25 input / $0.15 cached / $4.25 output per 1M tokens (contributor $0.10/$0.002/$0.20, data used for training); muse-wonder-1.0 $4/$0.20/$20; SAM 3.1 $2.50 per 1k images and $0.20 per 1k video frames (min 50 frames per request); Muse Voice Transcribe $0.18 per audio hour. Rate limits are per team: Spark/Wonder 3,000 RPM and 4M TPM, contributor 150 RPM / 3M TPM, sam-3.1 3,000 RPM, voice 256 concurrent streams. (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/pricing-rate-limits)
- [high] Reasoning cannot be turned off on Muse Spark/Wonder: reasoning_effort (Chat Completions) or reasoning.effort (Responses) accepts minimal, low, medium, high, xhigh, max; 'none' returns HTTP 400; reasoning tokens are billed as output and count against max_output_tokens; 'low' is the documented low-latency setting. (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/reasoning)
- [high] Tool calling uses the OpenAI-style tools array, parallel_tool_calls defaults to true, tool_choice supports only 'auto' (none/required/named return 400), and streamed tool-call arguments arrive as response.function_call_arguments.delta events; structured output uses response_format json_schema (Chat Completions) or text.format (Responses) with a strict-mode subset (additionalProperties:false, all properties required, no recursive schemas). (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/tool-calling)
- [high] Hosted SAM 3.1 is called as model 'sam-3.1' on POST /v1/responses with one input_text noun phrase plus one input_image (URL/base64) or input_video (URL or Files API file_id; MP4/MOV H.264/H.265); video is limited to 15,000 frames (~10 min) per request and 16 tracked objects per frame; results stream as special-token box/mask records (metadata.mask_encoding one_bit|lossless, include_confidence) parsed by meta-sam-parser / @meta-sam/parser; there is no live-stream mode and no published per-call latency. (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/sam/segmenting)
- [high] SAM 3.1 open weights exist at huggingface.co/facebook/sam3.1 (gated, SAM License, 848M params, released 03/27/2026, object multiplex tracks up to 16 objects per forward pass, throughput 16->32 fps on one H100), but the official facebookresearch/sam3 code requires Python >=3.12, PyTorch >=2.7 and a CUDA 12.6+ GPU; no CPU/MPS support is documented. (https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam3)
- [medium] Hugging Face transformers provides Sam3Model / Sam3VideoModel for facebook/sam3 (SAM 3, not 3.1) including a streaming per-frame video inference API (init_video_session without video, model(frame=...) per frame; native 1008px; streaming disables hotstart de-dup heuristics). Community reports put Apple Silicon at ~1.34 s/frame (M4 Max) and 7-8 s/image on MPS, so local SAM is not viable for a 5-10 Hz loop on the Mac. (https://huggingface.co/docs/transformers/main/en/model_doc/sam3_video)
- [high] Muse Glimmer is a 29.6B-parameter dense multimodal transformer (1.8B frozen ViT-G/14 Perception Encoder), Apache 2.0, text + interleaved image input (<=4,096 visual tokens per image; video processed as frames; no audio), text output, 131,072+ context, reasoning strength low/medium/high/xhigh set via 'Reasoning strength:' in the system prompt, recommended sampling temp 1.0 / top_p 0.95 / top_k 64, knowledge cutoff Jan 4 2026, released August 2026; DFlash drafter gives 1.8x on M5 Max and 1.5x on M4 Max. (https://huggingface.co/meta-models/Muse-Glimmer-30B)
- [high] Running Muse Glimmer locally on a Mac: official GGUF Muse-Glimmer-30B-KQuant-17GB-Q4_K_M.gguf is 16.8 GB plus a 1.4 GB mmproj for vision (19.0 GiB total at 131,072 context) on llama.cpp b10353+; Ollama offers muse-glimmer:30b (18 GB) and muse-glimmer:30b-mlx (19 GB) with image, tools and thinking support at 128K context; mlx-community publishes 4/5/6/8-bit MLX conversions. Meta measured 26.6->50.2 tok/s on M5 Max via ExecuTorch with the drafter. (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/muse-glimmer/quantization)
- [high] Muse Glimmer fine-tuning is documented only for your own GPUs: Axolotl QLoRA verified on 1xH100 (full-parameter FSDP2 on 8xH100 at ~97 GB/GPU), TRL LoRA/QLoRA, TitanRL GRPO; LoRA is recommended, merged weights serve on vLLM/llama.cpp. Meta Model API offers no hosted fine-tuning for any model, and no Apple Silicon/MLX training path is documented. (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/muse-glimmer/fine-tuning)
- [high] Fireworks: Muse Glimmer 30B (accounts/fireworks/models/muse-glimmer-30b) supports image input, tool calling and LoRA fine-tuning; serverless inference for it is discontinued starting Sept 25 2026 (on-demand dedicated GPUs only, H100 $8/hr); LoRA SFT is free via UI and Serverless Training API until Nov 30 2026 (default rank 8, chat JSONL, max-seq-len 2,048 default, vision SFT on Managed SFT) and trained LoRAs can only be served on on-demand deployments. Muse Spark Open is on Fireworks serverless at $1.25/$0.15/$4.25. (https://docs.fireworks.ai/fine-tuning/training-api/cookbook/muse-glimmer)
- [medium] Baseten Model APIs (https://inference.baseten.co/v1) serve Muse Spark Open as meta-models/muse-spark-open ($1.25/$0.15/$4.25) with tool calling and audio input but WITHOUT structured outputs/JSON mode; Baseten's library has no Muse Glimmer entry (family page returns 404), so Glimmer on Baseten means a dedicated vLLM deployment or Baseten Training (TRL/PEFT on H100 followed by 'baseten train checkpoint deploy', which serves base weights + LoRA via vLLM). (https://docs.baseten.co/development/model-apis/overview)
- [high] Serving Muse Glimmer (or a LoRA merge of it) on vLLM requires vLLM >=0.28 with --enable-auto-tool-choice --tool-call-parser muse_glimmer --reasoning-parser muse_glimmer (ATEM tool format, channel-scoped reasoning); the model does not support parallel tool calls; NVFP4 checkpoint uses 28.8 GB at 131k context on an RTX 5090 (68.8 tok/s; ~240 tok/s with DFlash on 2x5090). (https://recipes.vllm.ai/meta-models/Muse-Glimmer-30B)
- [high] Muse Voice Transcribe (muse-voice-transcribe-1.0) is Meta's only speech model and is ASR-only (no TTS exists in the catalog): realtime WebSocket wss://api.meta.ai/v1/asr/realtime with credential in the first JSON frame, PCM_24KHZ/PCM_16KHZ mono 16-bit PCM, modes PUSH_TO_TALK, ENDPOINTING, DIARIZATION, events speechStart/transcript/speechEnd/speechComplete, keyword biasing, 25 languages, 60-min sessions; file endpoint POST /v1/asr/transcribe (WAV <=32 MB, <=10 min); $0.18 per hour. (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/speech-to-text)
- [high] Meta Global AI Developer Hackathon: hackathon runs Oct 26-Nov 4 2026, project submissions due Nov 4 9:00 PM ET, judging Nov 4-8, winners Nov 9; themes include 'Push the Frontier' (robotics explicitly named); it encourages the Muse Spark series, Muse Image and SAM 3.1 via Meta Model API plus Meta open-weights models and Muse Code; $150 Model API credits per selected participant (Meta for Developers on X); Baseten $100 credits + 2M TPM, Modal $100, Fireworks credits for select participants (Cerebral Valley page). Official Rules and exact judging weights were not retrievable from primary pages. (https://dev.meta.ai/events/global-hackathon)

Imprecise: 848M is the SAM 3 parameter count (README line 'It has 848M parameters' describes SAM 3); no SAM 3.1 parameter count is published on the README or HF card. Additional: RELEASE_SAM3p1.md and HF card cite ~7x speedup at 128 objects on one H100 vs the Nov-2025 release; SAM 3 image latency is 30 ms with 100+ objects on an H200 (blog). Either way the conclusion holds: ~1 fps, not 5-10 Hz. (https://huggingface.co/docs/transformers/main/en/model_doc/sam3_video)

VERIFIER MISSED FINDINGS:
- Baseten DOES list Muse Glimmer 30B in its library as a dedicated deployment: 'runs the BF16 model on a single H100 through an OpenAI-compatible vLLM API ... pairs Muse Glimmer with its 2.56-billion-parameter DFlash assistant' (https://www.baseten.co/library/muse-glimmer-30b). Baseten Model API default limits are low (Basic unverified 15 RPM / 100k TPM; verified 120 RPM / 500k TPM; Pro 120 RPM / 1M TPM) so the hackathon's 2M TPM grant matters; use the x-session-affinity header (~60 req/min/session) for cache hits (https://docs.baseten.co/inference/model-apis/pricing-and-limits).
- Fine-tuning Muse Spark Open is NOT categorically infeasible: Fireworks' Serverless Training API trains LoRA adapters on accounts/fireworks/models/muse-spark-open-nvfp4 (train $18.58 per 1M tokens, prefill $6.19; managed UI fine-tuning not yet available) (https://fireworks.ai/models/fireworks/muse-spark-open, https://fireworks.ai/pricing). Post-promo Glimmer Serverless Training pricing is prefill $1.96 / cached $0.39 / sample $4.88 / train $5.86 per 1M tokens; Managed LoRA SFT for the 16.1B-80B tier is $3.00 per 1M training tokens.
- Data-use rules that affect using -contributor models in a demo: 'Meta does not use your Content from Standard Services to train Meta Models' but for Discounted Services 'Meta may use your Content, including Inputs and Outputs, to train' Meta's AI models (https://dev.meta.ai/legal/commitments); contributor variants are simply model IDs ending in -contributor and cannot be used by Zero-Data-Retention teams (https://dev.meta.ai/help/policies-and-privacy/contributor-tier); 'max' reasoning effort is unavailable on muse-spark-1.3-contributor (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/reasoning).
- Muse Glimmer on the exact dev machine (M5 Pro, 48 GB): the official deploy page verifies SGLang's MLX path on 'Apple silicon Mac (M5 Pro, 48 GB minimum)' with q4_k_m / q4 / q4k-dynamic (text-only, no speculative decoding, radix cache disabled), reports LM Studio Q4_K_M at ~24.1 GiB for one request at 16K context, and says ExecuTorch's MLX backend targets macOS while CPU export is unsupported (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/muse-glimmer/deploy). mlx-lm merged text-only Glimmer support on 2026-08-25 (https://github.com/ml-explore/mlx-lm/pull/1710); Unsloth claims fine-tuning 'on a 24GB card' with free Kaggle 2x T4 notebooks and lists Mac only for inference (32 GB for 4-bit) (https://unsloth.ai/docs/models/muse-glimmer).
- SAM 3/3.1 Apple-Silicon status is unmerged-PR territory: facebookresearch/sam3 PR #627 (2026-09-25, open) adds MPS/CPU with auto device selection (SAM3_DEVICE) and reports SAM 3.1 tracking 720p at ~1.3 fps, ~7 GB peak on an Apple M5 16 GB, CPU ~50x slower; PR #400 (open) adds image-only MPS and keeps video CUDA-only (https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam3/pull/627). transformers PR #45110 'Add SAM 3.1' (opened 2026-03-30) is still open, so SAM 3.1 in transformers is unavailable; SAM 3.1 Object Multiplex gives ~7x speedup at 128 objects on one H100 per RELEASE_SAM3p1.md. Practical implication: any on-Mac fallback tracker is SAM 3 (not 3.1) at ~1 fps; the 5-10 Hz loop must run on hosted SAM per-frame image calls or a cloud GPU.
- Hosted-API constraints the planner needs for a live video loop: Muse Spark video input accepts only video/mp4 via URL, base64 or file_id with no live-stream mode and no documented frame/duration cap, embedded audio read automatically (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/video-understanding); images cost ~1,300-1,500 tokens at ~1280px, 50 images/request max (>50 returns 400), 50 MB inline / 1 GiB via Files API, ~110-token request overhead, exact counts via POST /v1/responses/input_tokens (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/image-understanding). Meta's own robotics cookbook recipe keeps SAM outside the control loop with a 5-second timeout, retries disabled, fail-open, and 'The model never writes Python, emits joint commands, or controls the simulator directly' (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/meta-models/meta-model-cookbook/main/03_use_cases/14_muse_robotics/README.md). Artificial Analysis now shows Muse Spark 1.3 (Max) at 150.5 tok/s and 44.29 s TTFT and labels it deprecated in favor of Muse Wonder, so the summary's 190-335 tok/s / 17-38 s figures are stale (https://artificialanalysis.ai/models/muse-spark-1-3).

VERIFIER NOTES: Method: fetched ~35 distinct pages (dev.meta.ai docs/help/legal, HF cards and docs, GitHub READMEs/PRs via gh API, Fireworks, Baseten, OpenRouter, Ollama, vLLM recipes, Cerebral Valley, PyPI, Artificial Analysis). dev.meta.ai pages render a 'Please confirm your identity' SSO overlay but the doc text was readable; /legal index is a login wall, /legal/commitments is readable. The WebSearch budget was exhausted mid-task, so a few secondary claims (exact Apple-Silicon SAM timings, the $150 credit X post, the 'July 6 2026' Llama API shutdown date) could not be independently sourced; x.com returned HTTP 402. Verified by curl: llama.developer.meta.com and /docs 302 to dev.meta.ai; api.meta.ai/v1/models returns 401 without a key; the help article states 'Llama models aren't run for you through Model API' but gives no shutdown date. Scorecard: 12 confirmed, 4 partially correct (7: 848M is SAM 3's count; 8: Apple numbers unsourced; 13: Baseten Glimmer library entry exists, dedicated-only; 16: $150 unverified, Fireworks credit is $100). The only outright wrong statement is 'Baseten's library has no Glimmer entry (page 404s)'; its planning conclusion (dedicated vLLM deployment, not per-token API) still holds. All API-critical facts (endpoints, model IDs, prices, limits, parameter names, error codes, SAM output format, ASR protocol) matched primary docs verbatim. Note Baseten's site returns HTTP 200 for nonexistent library URLs, so existence must be judged by page content; the Glimmer page carries Glimmer-specific copy while a nonsense URL does not. Saved HTML used for that check: /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-russellvancuren-Projects-MetaHackathonDrone/a9780c4a-b149-4e3b-98c5-8dcb0c8772ee/scratchpad/baseten_check/.

RECOMMENDATIONS:
- Split the system into two loops. Slow loop (voice -> intent, <1 Hz): Muse Voice realtime (PUSH_TO_TALK via hotkey for demo reliability, ENDPOINTING for hands-free) -> muse-spark-1.3 with reasoning_effort 'low', stream:true, small max_output_tokens, and a strict json_schema (or a single `set_target` tool) producing {action: follow|stop|land|hover, target_noun_phrase, color, object_class, follow_distance}. Fast loop (5-10 Hz): SAM-derived bbox -> deterministic PID on bbox-center error (yaw/pitch), bbox-area error (throttle/pitch) -> turbodrone x69_lg RC packets. The LLM never emits RC values (mirror the Meta robotics cookbook's 'model-selected intents never directly control motors').
- Perception tier A (ship first): call hosted `sam-3.1` per frame on downscaled JPEG (e.g. 640x360 base64 data URL) at 1-3 Hz with metadata {mask_encoding:'one_bit', include_confidence:'true'}, parse with `meta-sam-parser`, and bridge gaps with a local OpenCV tracker (CSRT/KCF) or constant-velocity bbox prediction; re-detect on low tracker confidence. Cost at 2 Hz is ~$18/hr of flight; the 3,000 RPM team limit is not a constraint. Spike the round-trip latency on day one since Meta publishes none.
- Perception tier B (true real-time, satisfies 'open weights' judging): deploy facebookresearch/sam3 with the gated facebook/sam3.1 checkpoint on a Baseten or Modal GPU (hackathon credits) as a WebSocket frame-streaming service using the video predictor / transformers streaming session, returning identity-stable tracks at 10+ fps; keep tier A as fallback. Do not plan on running SAM 3/3.1 on the Mac GPU for flight (community numbers are ~1 s+/frame on MPS); use it only offline for the digital twin if the API is down.
- Target disambiguation: send the full attribute phrase to SAM ('person in blue shirt', 'green car'); if multiple instances return, apply a deterministic HSV color-histogram check on each mask region, and only at acquisition time (not in the loop) ask muse-spark-1.3 with the candidate crops (~1,400 tokens each) to pick the match. Lock onto the chosen object ID / bbox and track it.
- Interpret 'train an open Meta model' as a LoRA SFT of Muse Glimmer 30B for the transcript -> command JSON task: generate 2-5k synthetic noisy voice transcripts with labeled JSON, train on Fireworks (free LoRA SFT until Nov 30 2026, rank 8, chat JSONL, max-seq-len 2,048) or Baseten Training (TRL/PEFT on 1xH100), evaluate on a held-out set vs base Glimmer and vs Spark. Serve it either on Fireworks on-demand (H100 $8/hr, scale-to-zero) / Baseten dedicated with vLLM >=0.28 (`--tool-call-parser muse_glimmer --reasoning-parser muse_glimmer`, 'Reasoning strength: low' in the system prompt, no parallel tool calls), or merge+quantize to GGUF and run locally via llama.cpp/Ollama on the M5 Pro as the offline edge path. Make the intent-parser backend swappable (hosted Spark vs tuned Glimmer) and demo both.
- Do not attempt to fine-tune Muse Spark Open (816B, 8x8 H100 recipes, attribution license) or to run custom weights on hosted Muse Spark/Wonder (no hosted fine-tuning exists on dev.meta.ai). If a hosted Spark Open call is wanted for the 'open model' story, use Fireworks serverless or Baseten Model APIs, but note Baseten does not support structured outputs for it.
- Digital twin: build a Python simulator (MuJoCo is pip-installable, used by Meta's own robotics cookbook, and renders a camera; Blender headless is available for a photoreal variant) with a simple quadrotor model, a moving person-with-blue-shirt and green-car actors, and publish frames through the same interface turbodrone's video receiver uses (e.g. ffmpeg RTSP/HEVC or an in-process frame queue). Implement a `DroneBackend` interface with `X69Backend` (x69_lg protocol from turbodrone) and `SimBackend` so the identical perception/control stack runs in both; use simulator ground-truth to compute tracking error and to unit-test the controller without API calls.
- Safety and ops defaults: voice 'stop/land' handled by a local keyword check before any LLM call; lost-target -> hover after N frames; tilt/throttle clamps; watchdog on frame age; set `keywords` biasing in Muse Voice for 'follow', 'stop', 'land', colors and object words; use 24 kHz PCM16 mono 80 ms frames; use Responses API `previous_response_id` for clarification turns ('which person?').
- Budget/accounts checklist for the first hour: Meta Model API key with card on file (needed before requests), redeem the $150 participant credits, Fireworks account + FIREWORKS_API_KEY (for free Glimmer SFT), Baseten/Modal credits, Hugging Face gated access accepted for facebook/sam3.1 (and facebook/sam3 for the transformers path), `pip install openai meta-sam-parser websockets sounddevice`.
- Scope for the Nov 4 9:00 PM ET deadline (if submitting to the live event): day-1 = voice + Spark intent + hosted SAM tier A + sim + sim-based follow; day-2 = X69 flight test, demo video, README; stretch = Glimmer LoRA on Fireworks and tier B GPU SAM. Record the demo in the digital twin first so a flight failure does not sink the submission.

RISKS:
- Deadline: submissions for the live hackathon close Nov 4 2026 9:00 PM ET (tomorrow relative to 2026-11-03); the full scope (voice + LLM + SAM + controller + twin + flight code + fine-tune) is not achievable in that window without aggressive cuts.
- Hosted SAM 3.1 has no published per-call latency; a 720p HEVC decode + JPEG encode + HTTPS round-trip could exceed 300-500 ms, which makes the API unusable as the sole 5-10 Hz tracker; video mode is clip-upload only (min 50 frames billed) and not a live stream.
- SAM 3.1 open weights are gated and the official code is CUDA-only; the transformers path covers SAM 3 (not 3.1) and runs ~1 s+/frame on Apple Silicon, so real-time open-weight tracking requires a rented NVIDIA GPU (credits, deployment time, cold starts).
- Muse Spark reasoning cannot be disabled; third-party TTFT measurements range from ~4.5 s (p95 live) to 17-38 s at high effort, so even the 'slow' intent loop must use low effort, short schemas and streaming, and the UX must tolerate multi-second command latency.
- Muse Glimmer is not hosted by Meta; Fireworks has dropped serverless Glimmer inference (on-demand GPU at ~$8/hr), Baseten does not list it, and the cheapest hosted route (DeepInfra via OpenRouter) cannot serve a custom LoRA. A tuned Glimmer therefore costs GPU-hours or must run locally (18-20 GB RAM, ~15-30 tok/s on an M5 Pro, competing with the simulator for memory).
- Fine-tuning Glimmer on the Mac itself is unsupported (Axolotl/Unsloth/TRL recipes assume NVIDIA; mlx-lm support is text-only and LoRA for this architecture is unverified); Fireworks' free SFT window ends Nov 30 2026 and defaults to 2,048-token rows.
- Glimmer tool-calling (ATEM) needs matching parsers (vLLM >=0.28 'muse_glimmer'); community reports say Fireworks ignores chat_template_kwargs so reasoning strength must be passed differently; no parallel tool calls.
- Baseten Model APIs do not support structured outputs/JSON mode for Muse Spark Open; Contributor-mode Spark is cheap but trains on your data and caps at 150 RPM.
- Meta has no TTS model; any spoken feedback must come from a non-Meta engine (e.g. macOS `say`), which may matter for a 'Meta-models-only' narrative. CoTracker3 is CC-BY-NC-4.0 and DINOv3 has a custom gated license if used.
- Account/geo friction: Model API requires a card before requests, availability is limited to supported countries, and the $150 credits apply only to accepted participants; Official Rules and exact judging weights (30/25/25/20 per secondary sources) were not verifiable on primary pages.
- Hardware: the Loiley X69 is a brushed-motor toy quad with Wi-Fi RTSP/HEVC video (decode latency, drift, limited control authority); 'follow the green car' implies outdoor flight that this airframe handles poorly. Flight-ready code should be validated in the twin and with the drone tethered/low-altitude indoors first.

OPEN QUESTIONS FOR USER:
- Are you submitting to the live Meta Global AI Developer Hackathon (project due Nov 4 2026, 9:00 PM ET, i.e. tomorrow), or building this for a later deadline? This determines whether we cut scope to voice + Spark + hosted SAM + simulator first.
- Were you accepted as a participant (giving you the $150 Meta Model API credits and Baseten/Modal/Fireworks partner credits)? Do you already have a Meta Model API key with billing set up, and Fireworks / Baseten accounts?
- When you wrote 'train an open meta model and have it run [on] muse-glimmer or spark', do you mean (a) LoRA-fine-tune Muse Glimmer (the open model) for the voice-command parsing task and host it on Fireworks/Baseten, with hosted Muse Spark 1.3 as the main orchestrator, or (b) something else? Is a LoRA on the command-parsing task sufficient to count as 'training'?
- For live tracking, is the hosted SAM 3.1 API (1-3 Hz detections at roughly $9-27/hr plus a local OpenCV tracker in between) acceptable, or do you want true real-time open-weight SAM 3.1 on a rented GPU (needs HF gated access to facebook/sam3.1 and Baseten/Modal credits)?
- What is your cloud budget ceiling for GPU-hours (e.g. Fireworks on-demand H100 at $8/hr, Baseten dedicated per-minute billing) during development and the demo?
- How strict is 'all of the Meta models'? Is it OK to use OpenCV trackers/PID between SAM detections, macOS `say` for spoken feedback (Meta has no TTS), and MuJoCo/Blender for the twin?
- Digital twin fidelity: is a MuJoCo-rendered scene with simple textured actors (person in blue shirt, green car) enough, or do you want Blender-rendered photoreal frames (slower, more work)?
- Voice UX: push-to-talk hotkey (more reliable) or always-listening with Meta's ENDPOINTING mode?
- Flight environment: indoors following a person, or outdoors following a car? The X69 is an indoor brushed toy quad, and the demo plan changes significantly.
- Do you want the fine-tuned Glimmer to also run locally on your M5 Pro (18-20 GB RAM at 4-bit, alongside the simulator) as an offline mode, or only hosted?



### SAM 3.1: capabilities, APIs, hosting, and real-time tracking feasibility  [verifier reliability: high]

SUMMARY:
VERIFIED (fetched pages). SAM 3 (released 2025-11-19, arXiv 2511.16719) is an ~848M-param unified detector+tracker: Perception Encoder "L++" (vision ~450M, text ~300M), 1008x1008 input, DETR-style detector with presence token, SAM 2-style memory tracker (https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam3, https://arxiv.org/html/2511.16719v2). Prompts: short noun phrases (PCS, all instances), image exemplars, boxes/points/masks (PVS). SAM 3.1 shipped 2026-03-27 as a "drop-in replacement": Object Multiplex tracks up to 16 objects per forward pass via shared memory; Meta reports 16->32 FPS on one H100 for moderate object counts and ~7x at 128 objects; VOS gains on 6/7 benchmarks, text-prompt video metrics mixed (https://ai.meta.com/blog/segment-anything-model-3/, https://huggingface.co/facebook/sam3.1, https://learnopencv.com/sam-3-whats-new/). Checkpoint `sam3.1_multiplex.pt` is 3.5 GB, gated on HF; native repo needs Python 3.12+, PyTorch 2.7+, CUDA 12.6+ (triton kernels; no official MPS/CPU). Published speed: 30 ms/image with 100+ objects on H200; video "near real-time for ~5 concurrent objects" (SAM 3); Roboflow measured 251 ms/image on one L4 (https://docs.roboflow.com/models/supported-models/sam3).

Text/attribute prompting: paper and Appendix B say SAM 3 is "constrained to simple noun phrase prompts" with at most one or two attributes ("yellow school bus", "red apple") and does NOT support referring expressions or spatial relations; third-party RES papers confirm low recall/over-segmentation of the whole category for referring phrases. "person in blue shirt" and "green car" are within the 1-2 attribute envelope but have no color-specific benchmark; expect false positives on multiple people and threshold sensitivity. The paper's SAM 3 Agent (MLLM proposes noun phrases, inspects returned masks, iterates; tested with Gemini 2.5 Pro, Llama 4 Maverick, Qwen2.5-VL) is the sanctioned fix for complex phrases (https://arxiv.org/html/2511.16719v2).

HOSTED (Meta): dev.meta.ai exposes `sam-3.1` on the Meta Model API: `POST https://api.meta.ai/v1/responses`, OpenAI Responses-compatible, `Authorization: Bearer $MODEL_API_KEY`; one `input_text` noun phrase (one concept per request) + `input_image` (public URL or base64 data URL) or `input_video` (`video_url` or `file_id` from `POST /v1/files`; MP4/MOV with H.264/H.265/MPEG-4; no WebM/VP9). Box/point prompts are encoded as special tokens (`<0f>1<|box;x1=..;y1=..;x2=..;y2=..;w=..;h=..|>`), up to 16 objects. Output is special-token text: `<Nf>` frame marker, per-object id, box in source pixels, mask `data=H,W,~|!base85`; `metadata.mask_encoding="one_bit"` shrinks payloads, `include_confidence="true"` adds `c`. Video: one line per frame, identity preserved, up to 15,000 frames (~10 min) per request, 16 tracked objects/frame, requests synchronous with optional SSE streaming, no async jobs (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/sam/overview, https://dev.meta.ai/docs/sam/segmenting, https://dev.meta.ai/docs/sam/reading-segmentation). Parsers: `pip install meta-sam-parser` (0.1.0, py>=3.10; `decode_mask_to_raster`, `build_object_prompt`) and npm `@meta-sam/parser` (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/sam/client-libraries). Pricing: $2.50/1k images, $0.20/1k video frames, 50-frame minimum per video request; rate limit 3,000 RPM per team for sam-3.1; muse-spark-1.3 3,000 RPM/4M TPM, $1.25/$4.25 per M tokens; muse-voice-transcribe-1.0 $0.18/hr with streaming (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/pricing-rate-limits). No official API latency figures anywhere; one hackathon repo logged a single 1.8 s SAM 3.1 image grounding call from a phone (https://github.com/LincForge/nerve-muse-sam). Critically, the hosted API has no live/frame-by-frame session: video mode needs a finished clip. It can therefore serve as a periodic text-prompted DETECTOR, not as the 5-10 Hz tracker.

HOSTED (others): fal `fal-ai/sam-3/image` $0.005/request (PNG masks, optional boxes/scores); `fal-ai/sam-3/video` $0.005 per 16 frames; `fal-ai/sam-3-1/video` $0.01 per 16 frames, comma-separated multi-prompts, `max_num_objects` 16, clip-in/rendered-video-out (https://fal.ai/models/fal-ai/sam-3-1/video/api). Roboflow serverless `POST https://serverless.roboflow.com/sam3/concept_segment` (text/box prompts, RLE/polygon output) and `/sam3/visual_segment`; video tracking is local-GPU-only (https://docs.roboflow.com/models/supported-models/sam3). Replicate `visionaix/sam3` has no pushed versions; Baseten and Fireworks catalogs show no SAM 3 entry (custom Truss/self-host only). HF `facebook/sam3.1` is "not deployed by any Inference Provider".

APPLE SILICON: official PyTorch path is CUDA-gated; unmerged PRs #400/#627 add MPS/CPU; PR #627 reports SAM 3.1 720p tracking at ~1.3 FPS (0.77 s/frame, ~7 GB peak) on an M5 16 GB, CPU ~50x slower (https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam3/pull/627); `sam3-mac` shim: ~3 s/image CPU, 7-8 s MPS on M4 Pro, image only (https://github.com/benreichman/sam3-mac). The practical local route is the MLX port merged into upstream mlx-vlm (PR #880, 2026-03-30; current mlx-vlm 0.7.11, py>=3.10): weights `mlx-community/sam3.1-bf16` (3.49 GB) / `mlx-community/sam3-4bit` (0.62 GB); on an M3 Max, SAM 3.1 tracker propagation is ~190-230 ms/frame for 3-5 objects at 1008 px (SAM 3: 547-766 ms), and a "realtime" pipeline at 224 px with backbone caching hits 38-43 ms/frame (23-26 FPS) but with no accuracy evaluation at 224 px and stale (~1 s) backbone features; CLI `python -m mlx_vlm.models.sam3_1.generate --task realtime --video 0 --prompt "..." --resolution 1008 --detect-every 15 --memory-every 3`; Python `Sam3Predictor.predict`, `predict_multi`, `model.track_init/track_step` (https://huggingface.co/mlx-community/sam3.1-bf16, https://github.com/Blaizzy/mlx-vlm/pull/880). HF transformers (5.22) `Sam3VideoModel` supports true streaming (`model(inference_session=..., frame=pixel_values)`) with the documented caveat that hotstart heuristics are disabled (more false positives/duplicate tracks); `config.image_size=560` trades accuracy for speed; MPS support for the video model is unconfirmed (https://huggingface.co/docs/transformers/main/en/model_doc/sam3_video).

SMALLER META MODELS: no official SAM 3 "tiny" exists (the "sam3.1-tiny 60 fps on L4" blog claim is unsourced). EdgeTAM (Meta, Apache 2.0) is a distilled SAM 2 tracker with CoreML export for iOS/macOS, 16 FPS on iPhone 15 Pro Max, points/boxes only, no text (https://github.com/facebookresearch/EdgeTAM). SAM 2.1-tiny via MLX runs ~71-97 ms/step on M2 Max. CoTracker3 has no CPU/MPS benchmarks.

LICENSE: SAM License (non-OSI): royalty-free use/modify/distribute, you own derivatives, must ship the license text, no military/ITAR/trade-controlled uses, terminates if you sue Meta over the materials, Meta may modify terms with continued use as acceptance (https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam3/blob/main/LICENSE). `meta-sam-parser` is also under the SAM License.

FINE-TUNING: official `pip install -e ".[train]"` with Hydra configs for RF100-VL/ODinW (`python sam3/train/train.py -c <cfg> --use-cluster 0 --num-gpus 1`), CUDA only; paper few-shot recipe: 40 epochs, LR x0.1, batch 2; 10-shot lifts ODinW13 AP 61.0->71.8 (https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam3/blob/main/README_TRAIN.md). Roboflow offers hosted SAM 3 fine-tuning on all plans (1008x1008 polygon masks) but fine-tuned models cannot run on its serverless API.

LOCAL CONTEXT (read from repo): turbodrone's follow plugin runs YOLOv10n at FOLLOW_FPS=20, imgsz 320, bang-bang `FollowController` (yaw from x-offset, pitch from box width), emits `fc.set_axes_from("follow", throttle, yaw, pitch, roll)` in -1..1 and `send_overlay()`; X69 sticks are 0..128..255, RTSP 1280x720 HEVC, h265 path resamples to 15 fps. Muse Glimmer 30B (dense, 2B PE vision encoder, Apache 2.0, image+video input, tool calling, GGUF) and Muse Spark 1.x (hosted, multimodal) both qualify as the agent MLLM (https://huggingface.co/blog/muse-glimmer). Hackathon: submissions due Nov 4, 9:00 PM ET; $150 API credits; partner credits Baseten $100 / Modal $100 / Fireworks $25 (secondary source).

INFERRED: hosted image round-trip for a 640x360 JPEG should land ~0.5-2 s (L4 model time 0.25 s + upload + queue), i.e. 0.5-2 Hz detection cadence; 1008 px MLX detection on the M5 Pro is likely ~0.5-1.5 s/frame (unmeasured); cost at 1 Hz is $0.15/min ($9/hr), negligible against $150.

KEY CLAIMS:
- [high] SAM 3.1 was released 2026-03-27 as a drop-in replacement for SAM 3; Object Multiplex tracks up to 16 objects per forward pass, raising single-H100 throughput from 16 to 32 FPS at moderate object counts and ~7x at 128 objects. (https://ai.meta.com/blog/segment-anything-model-3/)
- [high] Meta Model API serves model id `sam-3.1` at POST https://api.meta.ai/v1/responses (OpenAI Responses-compatible, Bearer key); one short noun phrase per request plus input_image (URL or base64 data URL) or input_video (MP4/MOV H.264/H.265, via video_url or /v1/files file_id); box/point prompts via special tokens up to 16 objects; video up to 15,000 frames and 16 tracked objects per frame; requests are synchronous with optional SSE, no async job API. (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/sam/segmenting)
- [high] Hosted SAM 3.1 pricing is $2.50 per 1k images and $0.20 per 1k video frames with a 50-frame minimum per video request; rate limit is 3,000 RPM per team for sam-3.1; muse-spark-1.3 is 3,000 RPM / 4M TPM at $1.25 in / $4.25 out per M tokens; muse-voice-transcribe-1.0 is $0.18/hr with streaming. (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/pricing-rate-limits)
- [high] Hosted output is special-token text (<Nf> frame marker, object id, box in source pixels, mask data=H,W,~|! base85); request metadata mask_encoding="one_bit" and include_confidence="true"; official parsers are PyPI `meta-sam-parser` (0.1.0, py>=3.10, decode_mask_to_raster) and npm `@meta-sam/parser`. (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/sam/client-libraries)
- [high] SAM 3 is ~848M params (PE L++ vision ~450M + text ~300M, detector+tracker ~100M) at 1008x1008 input; the sam3.1_multiplex.pt checkpoint is 3.5 GB and gated; the official repo requires Python 3.12+, PyTorch 2.7+, and an NVIDIA GPU with CUDA 12.6+. (https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam3)
- [high] Published latency: 30 ms per image with 100+ objects on an H200; SAM 3 video latency scales with object count and is near real-time only for ~5 concurrent objects; Roboflow measured 251.4 ms per text-prompted image on one NVIDIA L4 (batch 1). (https://docs.roboflow.com/models/supported-models/sam3)
- [high] SAM 3 is designed for simple noun phrases with at most one or two attributes (e.g. 'yellow school bus') and does not support referring expressions or spatial relations; the paper's SAM 3 Agent pattern (an MLLM proposes noun phrases, inspects masks, iterates; tested with Gemini 2.5 Pro, Llama 4 Maverick, Qwen2.5-VL) is the recommended approach for complex queries. (https://arxiv.org/html/2511.16719v2)
- [medium] On Apple Silicon the official PyTorch repo is CUDA-gated; the unmerged PR #627 reports SAM 3.1 tracking 720p video at ~1.3 FPS (0.77 s/frame, ~7 GB peak) on an M5 16 GB via MPS, with CPU ~50x slower; the sam3-mac shim gets ~3 s/image on CPU and 7-8 s on MPS (M4 Pro), image only. (https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam3/pull/627)
- [high] An MLX port of SAM 3.1 is merged in upstream mlx-vlm (PR #880, 2026-03-30; weights mlx-community/sam3.1-bf16, 3.49 GB): on an M3 Max, tracker propagation is ~190-230 ms/frame for 3-5 objects at 1008 px, and a 224 px 'realtime' mode with backbone caching reaches 38-43 ms/frame (23-26 FPS) but has no accuracy evaluation at that resolution; API: Sam3Predictor.predict/predict_multi, model.track_init/track_step; CLI `python -m mlx_vlm.models.sam3_1.generate --task realtime`. (https://huggingface.co/mlx-community/sam3.1-bf16)
- [high] HF transformers Sam3VideoModel supports frame-by-frame streaming (model(inference_session=..., frame=pixel_values)) but the docs warn streaming disables hotstart heuristics, causing more false positives and duplicate tracks; custom resolution (config.image_size=560) degrades accuracy; the model is meant for 1008 px. (https://huggingface.co/docs/transformers/main/en/model_doc/sam3_video)
- [medium] Third-party hosting: fal fal-ai/sam-3/image is $0.005/request, fal-ai/sam-3/video $0.005 per 16 frames, fal-ai/sam-3-1/video $0.01 per 16 frames (clip in, rendered video out, max_num_objects 16); Roboflow serverless exposes /sam3/concept_segment for images only; Replicate visionaix/sam3 has no pushed versions; no SAM 3 entry was found in Baseten or Fireworks catalogs. (https://fal.ai/models/fal-ai/sam-3-1/video/api)
- [high] The SAM License is royalty-free and permits use, modification, distribution and commercial derivative works (you own derivatives) but requires shipping the license text, bans military/ITAR/trade-controlled uses, terminates on IP litigation against Meta, and lets Meta modify terms with continued use as acceptance. (https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam3/blob/main/LICENSE)
- [high] Fine-tuning is supported officially (pip install -e '.[train]', Hydra configs, `python sam3/train/train.py -c <cfg> --use-cluster 0 --num-gpus 1`, CUDA only); the paper's few-shot recipe is 40 epochs at 0.1x LR with batch 2 and lifts ODinW13 AP from 61.0 (zero-shot) to 71.8 (10-shot); Roboflow offers hosted SAM 3 fine-tuning on all plans but fine-tuned models cannot run on its serverless API. (https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam3/blob/main/README_TRAIN.md)
- [high] There is no official smaller SAM 3 variant (the 'sam3.1-tiny 60 fps on L4' claim is unsourced); Meta's on-device tracker is EdgeTAM (Apache 2.0, distilled SAM 2, CoreML export for iOS/macOS, 16 FPS on iPhone 15 Pro Max, points/boxes only, no text prompts). (https://github.com/facebookresearch/EdgeTAM)
- [medium] Hackathon submissions are due Nov 4, 9:00 PM ET (judging Nov 4-8, winners Nov 9); participants get $150 Meta Model API credits; secondary sources list partner credits of Baseten $100, Modal $100, Fireworks $25 and weight 'Use of Meta AI Tooling' at 25% of judging. (https://dev.meta.ai/events/global-hackathon)

VERIFIER MISSED FINDINGS:
- HF transformers has NO SAM 3.1 / Object Multiplex support: gh code search of huggingface/transformers returns 0 hits for 'multiplex' or 'sam3.1', the docs load facebook/sam3, and facebook/sam3.1 contains only sam3.1_multiplex.pt (3,502,755,717 B) + config/tokenizer (no safetensors). Community conversions exist if needed: unsloth/sam3.1 (library=transformers, model.safetensors 1.68 GB plus FP8/INT8 ~1.0 GB), Comfy-Org/sam3.1 fp16 1.75 GB, strangervisionhf/sam3.1-st-bf16 1.87 GB. Sources: https://huggingface.co/api/models/facebook/sam3.1?blobs=true, https://huggingface.co/api/models/unsloth/sam3.1
- Hosted-API prompt behavior documented by Meta that directly affects 'person in the blue shirt': comma lists ('bus, bicycle') return zero matches; a second input_text or 'and' still segments one concept; 'person on the left' is 'only partially honored'; negation/OR/spatial phrasing inconsistent; HTTP 400 is permanent (do not retry unchanged); metadata.propagation_direction both|forward|backward (backward needs a prompt on a later frame); frames with no visible objects emit no output line; object ids are stable only while the object stays in view (not across exit/occlusion/long clips); use `ffmpeg -movflags +faststart` to avoid server-side remux. Source: https://dev.meta.ai/docs/sam/segmenting and https://dev.meta.ai/docs/sam/reading-segmentation
- Other Apple-Silicon routes checked and found slow or incomplete: sam3.cpp (MIT, CPU+Metal, SAM 3 only with text prompts + video tracking, GGML q4/q8) benchmarks on an M4 Pro 24 GB at 1008 px: SAM 3 f16 7.7 s/frame Metal (23.8 s CPU), EdgeTAM f16 0.4 s/frame, SAM 2.1 tiny f16 0.8 s/frame; needs CMake (not installed). Ultralytics 8.4.202 (AGPL-3.0) loads sam3.1_multiplex.pt for image prediction only ('Object Multiplex video tracking is not supported yet'), text via SAM3SemanticPredictor; MPS support for SAM3 undocumented. CoreML SAM 3.1 exports exist (AllanVester/SAM3.1-CoreML-FP16, sdkv2/sam3.1-coreml, waynenilsen/vbt-sam3.1-ane) but ship no benchmarks or inference code. Sources: https://github.com/PABannier/sam3.cpp, https://docs.ultralytics.com/models/sam-3, https://huggingface.co/AllanVester/SAM3.1-CoreML-FP16
- MLX details: mlx-vlm now lives at Neywa-Labs/mlx-vlm (PR #880 merged 2026-03-30T17:04Z); mlx-community/sam3.1-bf16 metadata says F32 tensors and ~873M params (3.49 GB = 873M x 4 bytes, so weights are stored float32 despite the name; expect ~3.5 GB weights + activations in unified memory); card says mlx-vlm>=0.4.3; SAM 3 (not 3.1) MLX variants mlx-community/sam3-bf16 (1.72 GB) and sam3-4bit (0.62 GB) exist; the PR's own justification for 224 px realtime is 'scene changes slowly at 224px, so stale backbone for ~1s is fine', which does not hold for a moving drone camera. Sources: https://github.com/Blaizzy/mlx-vlm/pull/880, https://huggingface.co/api/models/mlx-community/sam3.1-bf16?blobs=true
- Pricing/limits the researcher omitted: muse-voice-transcribe-1.0 has no RPM/TPM limit but 256 concurrent streams and 16,000 streams/hour; muse-spark-1.3-contributor is $0.10 in / $0.20 out per M at 150 RPM / 3M TPM (12.5x cheaper, grants Meta training use of content); web search grounding costs $2.50/1k queries; muse-image-1.0 $0.01/image at 150 RPM; muse-wonder-1.0 $4/$20 per M. Baseten and Fireworks host the open Muse models (Baseten library: muse-spark-open, muse-glimmer-30b; Fireworks: muse-glimmer-30b, muse-spark-open, muse-spark-open-nvfp4), and a participant repo notes the hackathon requires Baseten model id `meta-models/muse-spark-open-1026`; neither hosts SAM. Muse Glimmer released 2026-08-10, samples video at 2 fps capped at 96 frames. Sources: https://dev.meta.ai/docs/pricing-rate-limits, https://www.baseten.co/sitemap.xml, https://fireworks.ai/sitemap.xml, https://huggingface.co/blog/muse-glimmer
- turbodrone specifics beyond the summary (verified in repo): follow_plugin always sends throttle=0 and roll=0 and divides controller output by 100 (FollowController returns +/-yaw_speed, +/-pitch_speed, defaults 20 -> +/-0.20 normalized, bang-bang with yaw_deadzone 0.15 and box-width band 0.30-0.80 +/-0.02); FOLLOW_FPS=20 is a frame-dropping rate limit, YOLO_CONFIDENCE default 0.65; the no-detection branch calls fc.set_axes (source 'unknown') rather than set_axes_from('follow'); FlightController.set_axes_from clamps to [-1,1] and a background loop calls model.update(dt, axes); X69LgRcModel STICK_RANGE=(0,128,255) and the adapter scales only roll/pitch by speed_scale_by_index {0:0.55,1:0.75,2:1.0} about center before clamping 0..255; h265 path output_fps default 15, RTSP JPEG quality default 85. Paths: backend/plugins/follow/follow_plugin.py, follow_controller.py, backend/services/flight_controller.py, backend/models/x69_lg_rc.py, backend/protocols/x69_lg_rc_protocol_adapter.py, x69_lg_video_protocol.py

VERIFIER NOTES: METHOD. Fetched ~45 distinct sources: ai.meta.com SAM 3 blog; dev.meta.ai docs (sam/overview, sam/segmenting, sam/reading-segmentation, sam/client-libraries, pricing-rate-limits, events/global-hackathon); github facebookresearch/sam3 (README, RELEASE_SAM3p1.md, README_TRAIN.md, LICENSE, pyproject, PRs #627/#400 via page + GitHub API), facebookresearch/EdgeTAM, benreichman/sam3-mac, PABannier/sam3.cpp, LincForge/nerve-muse-sam, meta-ai-hackathon/meta-hack (+ info/stack and credits.md, info/grading criteria.md); mlx-vlm PR #880; HF model cards/API (facebook/sam3, facebook/sam3.1, mlx-community/sam3.1-bf16, sam3-bf16, sam3-4bit, unsloth/sam3.1, strangervisionhf, Comfy-Org, AllanVester CoreML, muse-glimmer blog); HF transformers sam3_video docs + raw modeling file; arXiv 2511.16719v2 (downloaded 1.87 MB HTML and grepped locally) and 2501.07256; Roboflow docs; fal sam-3/image, sam-3/video, sam-3-1/video (+/api); Replicate visionaix/sam3; Ultralytics SAM 3 docs; learnopencv; PyPI JSON (mlx-vlm 0.7.11, transformers 5.22.1, meta-sam-parser 0.1.0, torch 2.15.0, ultralytics 8.4.202) and npm registry; Baseten/Fireworks/docs.baseten sitemaps; Cerebral Valley event page; developers.meta.com Connect recap; four hackathon secondary sources; and the turbodrone repo files. LIMITS. The shared WebSearch budget ran out after 5 searches, so three planned searches (official rules rubric; Baseten SAM; Fireworks SAM; transformers sam3.1) were replaced by direct fetches, sitemap greps, HF API queries, and gh code search. Baseten's library page is JS-rendered (only a title returned) and Fireworks' catalog shows 10 of 329 models, hence sitemap verification. facebook/sam3.1's model card is gated; file list/sizes came from the public HF API. dev.meta.ai pages loaded without login. OVERALL. The researcher's technical claims are accurate: 11 of 15 fully confirmed, 4 partially correct. Concrete errors: (a) Roboflow SAM 3 fine-tuning is on 'paid plans that include usage-based billing', not all plans; (b) 'spatial relations' limitation is from Meta's API docs, not the paper; (c) hackathon $150 credits and the 30/25/25/20 judging weights are not on any official page (official pages list only qualitative criteria and said Official Rules were not yet published), and the Fireworks partner credit is $100 per the Cerebral Valley organizer page vs $25 in secondary sources; (d) 'PE L++' naming is not in the paper (paper says ~850M, README 848M; 450M/300M/100M split confirmed). Inferred (not page-verified) items in the researcher summary remain inferences: 0.77 s/frame (=1/1.3 fps), 0.5-2 s hosted round-trip, 0.5-1.5 s/frame MLX detection on M5 Pro, $9/hr at 1 Hz. PLANNER-RELEVANT SYNTHESIS (verified facts only). The hosted sam-3.1 API is clip/image-in, synchronous/SSE-out with no live session, no latency SLA, 50-frame minimum per video request, 3,000 RPM; it is a periodic detector, not a 5-10 Hz tracker. Local SAM 3.1 on the M5 Pro has two measured routes: MLX (mlx-vlm 0.7.11; ~190-230 ms/frame multiplex tracking for 3-5 objects at 1008 px on M3 Max; 224 px realtime ~40 ms but no accuracy data and stale backbone) and PyTorch-MPS via unmerged PR #627 (~1.3 fps at 720p on M5 16 GB). HF transformers streaming is SAM 3 only (no multiplex). sam3.cpp Metal is ~7.7 s/frame for SAM 3 (not viable) but 0.4 s/frame for EdgeTAM. fal sam-3-1/video is $0.01 per 16 frames, clip-in/rendered-mp4-out, not frame streaming. Hackathon: submissions Nov 4 9:00 PM ET; credits must be claimed per person by the same deadline with card pre-auth (participant repo); projects must genuinely use Meta Model API and/or open-weight models (pass/fail gate per participant repo). Turbodrone follow plugin is a 20 Hz YOLOv10n bang-bang loop emitting normalized yaw/pitch only; X69 sticks are 0..128..255 with per-speed-index scaling on roll/pitch.

RECOMMENDATIONS:
- Architecture: split perception into (1) a text-prompted DETECTOR running at 0.5-2 Hz and (2) a local TRACKER + Kalman filter at 10-15 Hz, with a 10 Hz PID controller. The hosted Meta `sam-3.1` image endpoint is the detector (it cannot run a live session); the local tracker provides the 5-10 Hz loop. This mirrors the community 'SAMv3 detect + light tracker' hybrid and keeps the demo Meta-native.
- Detector call shape: downscale the 1280x720 RTSP frame to 640x360 JPEG (~40-60 KB), send as a base64 data URL to POST https://api.meta.ai/v1/responses with model="sam-3.1", a single noun phrase, metadata {"include_confidence":"true","mask_encoding":"one_bit"}, non-streaming; parse with `pip install meta-sam-parser` (decode_mask_to_raster). Run it in an asyncio worker that always sends the latest frame and tags each request with the capture timestamp. Cost at 1 Hz is $0.0025/call = $0.15/min; 3,000 RPM is not a constraint.
- Measure before committing: first task on the Mac is a 30-call latency benchmark (p50/p95) of the image endpoint at 640x360 and 1280x720, plus a 60-s benchmark of `python -m mlx_vlm.models.sam3_1.generate --task realtime --video 0 --prompt "person" --resolution 1008|560|224` on the M5 Pro. Choose hosted-detector if API p95 < 1.5 s; choose MLX-local if the Mac cannot keep internet while joined to the X69 Wi-Fi.
- Local tracker between detections: start with OpenCV CSRT (fallback KCF/MOSSE) initialised from the SAM box, plus a constant-velocity Kalman filter on (cx, cy, w, h). On each SAM result, match detections to the Kalman state predicted at the request's capture timestamp (keep a 3 s ring buffer), re-anchor the tracker on the best IoU match (>0.3), and declare 'lost' after 1.5 s without a match. Upgrade path if CSRT drifts: EdgeTAM (CoreML) or SAM 2.1-tiny via MLX as the propagation tracker, or SAM 3.1 MLX track_init/track_step at 1008 px (~4-5 FPS on M3 Max).
- Controller: replace turbodrone's bang-bang FollowController with PID + slew-rate limits; yaw from normalised x error, pitch from box width/area set-point (e.g. 0.30-0.45 of frame width), throttle (or camera tilt command) from y error; clamp outputs to +/-0.3 of stick range for the brushed X69, zero axes and hover on 'lost', slow yaw search after 1.5 s, hard stop after 5 s. Implement as a new plugin (e.g. backend/plugins/sam_follow) reusing `self.frames`, `send_overlay`, and `fc.set_axes_from("follow", throttle, yaw, pitch, roll)` so the same code runs against the digital twin's frame source.
- Intent + disambiguation: use Muse Spark 1.3 (hosted, multimodal) or Muse Glimmer 30B (Apache 2.0, image input, llama.cpp GGUF) to convert the transcript into JSON {noun_phrase (<= 2 attributes, SAM-friendly), action, constraints}. When SAM returns >1 instance or the phrase has spatial/relational parts, run the SAM 3 Agent step: send the frame with numbered boxes to the MLLM and ask it to pick the instance; lock onto that track id. Use `include_confidence` scores with a tuned threshold (start 0.5, lower to 0.3 if recall is poor).
- Voice: muse-voice-transcribe-1.0 (streaming, $0.18/hr) with push-to-talk on the Mac; keep a text fallback for the demo.
- Digital twin: feed the same plugin from a simulated camera (rendered frames with a moving 'person in blue shirt' and a 'green car') and a simulated X69 kinematic model; because the detector is image-based, the twin and the real drone share identical perception code. Use the hosted video mode offline ($0.20/1k frames, 50-frame minimum) on recorded twin/drone clips to produce reference tracks for regression tests.
- If time permits, true identity-preserving 10+ FPS tracking: self-host SAM 3.1 streaming (matteo-tafuro/sam3-realtime fork or HF transformers Sam3VideoModel streaming) on a Modal/Baseten L4 or H100 behind a WebSocket, sending 640x360 JPEGs; partner credits (Baseten $100, Modal $100) cover it. Do not rely on the hosted video API or fal video endpoints for live control (clip-based).
- Satisfy 'train an open Meta model' within the time budget by either (a) a 10-shot SAM 3 fine-tune on a small Roboflow dataset of your target objects via Roboflow hosted training (free tier) or the official trainer on a Modal GPU, or (b) a LoRA of Muse Glimmer for intent parsing; SAM 3 training needs CUDA and cannot run on the Mac.
- License hygiene: ship the SAM License text with any redistributed weights/derivatives, keep the project non-military, and note that meta-sam-parser is also SAM-licensed.

RISKS:
- Deadline: submissions close Nov 4, 9:00 PM ET and today is Nov 3; a hosted-detector + OpenCV tracker + PID is achievable, a self-hosted streaming server probably is not.
- Connectivity: the Mac must be joined to the X69 access point (172.16.11.1) for RC/RTSP and simultaneously reach api.meta.ai; a single Wi-Fi radio typically loses internet when on the drone AP, so the hosted path needs a second interface (USB-C Ethernet, second Wi-Fi adapter, or tethered phone). If that is impossible, only the MLX-local path works in flight.
- Unmeasured hosted latency: no official latency figures exist; the only observed data point is a 1.8 s single call from a phone. Detections may be 1-2 s stale, so latency compensation (timestamped matching, Kalman prediction) is mandatory, and detection cadence may be only ~0.5-1 Hz.
- Attribute prompts: SAM 3 supports 1-2 attributes but has no color-attribute benchmark; 'person in blue shirt' may match every person or none depending on lighting and distance; multiple candidates require the MLLM disambiguation step and tuned confidence thresholds.
- Hosted image mode has no identity across calls; identity must be maintained locally (IoU/Kalman association), with risk of ID switches between similar people.
- Local MLX SAM 3.1: the 23-26 FPS figure is at 224 px with ~1 s stale backbone features and no accuracy evaluation; drone targets are small in frame, so 224 px will likely miss them; 1008 px detection speed on the M5 Pro is unmeasured and may be ~0.5-1.5 s/frame.
- Official PyTorch SAM 3 does not run on Apple Silicon without unmerged patches (triton/CUDA autocast); HF transformers Sam3VideoModel on MPS is unconfirmed; budget time for environment failures (Python 3.13/3.15 on the Mac vs sam3's 3.12 pin; mlx-vlm requires >=3.10).
- Streaming-mode trackers (HF transformers streaming, sam3-realtime) produce more false positives and duplicate tracks because hotstart heuristics are disabled; memory grows without pruning on long sessions.
- Control: the X69 is a brushed-motor toy with Wi-Fi video at ~15 fps and unknown glass-to-glass latency; bang-bang control plus 1-2 s perception delay will oscillate; PID gains must be tuned in the twin first, and safety cutoffs (lost-target hover, manual override, max run time) are essential.
- Hosted video endpoints (Meta, fal) are clip-based with a 50-frame minimum and cannot drive a live loop; using them for live control would add seconds of latency.
- Licensing: SAM License is non-OSI, Meta can change terms, and meta-sam-parser inherits it; hackathon judging may still accept it, but redistribution must include the license text.
- Partner-credit amounts and the 25% 'Use of Meta AI Tooling' weighting come from secondary sources, not the official event page.

OPEN QUESTIONS FOR USER:
- Do you already have a Meta Model API key with the $150 hackathon credit active, and is the Nov 4, 9:00 PM ET submission deadline the hard target for this build (today is Nov 3)?
- Can your Mac keep internet access while joined to the X69's Wi-Fi (second Wi-Fi adapter, USB-C Ethernet, or phone tether)? If not, the in-flight perception must run locally (MLX SAM 3.1) and the hosted API can only be used in the digital twin and for offline evaluation.
- Which do you prefer if they conflict: lowest engineering risk (hosted sam-3.1 detector + OpenCV tracker, 0.5-2 Hz re-detection) or fully on-device (MLX SAM 3.1, no internet, lower resolution/accuracy, needs a benchmark on your M5 Pro first)?
- For the LLM/agent layer, hosted Muse Spark 1.3 (multimodal, trivial to call from the same base URL) or local Muse Glimmer 30B (Apache 2.0, ~18-20 GB in Q4 GGUF alongside a 3.5 GB SAM 3.1 MLX model on 48 GB)?
- How do you want to satisfy 'train an open Meta model' in the time available: a 10-shot SAM 3 fine-tune (Roboflow hosted or Modal GPU; cannot run on the Mac) or a small LoRA of Muse Glimmer for intent parsing?
- Typical target distance/size: will the person or car fill roughly 10-40% of the 1280x720 frame (indoor/close) or be small and far (outdoor)? This decides whether 560/224 px inference is acceptable or 1008 px is required.
- Do you have approved gated access on Hugging Face for facebook/sam3 and facebook/sam3.1 (needed for the official PyTorch/fine-tuning path; the mlx-community conversions are separate repos)?
- Is a cloud GPU (Modal/Baseten partner credits) acceptable for an optional identity-preserving streaming tracker, or must everything except Meta's own API run on the Mac?



### Meta speech models for voice commands on a Mac (ASR and optionally TTS)  [verifier reliability: high]

SUMMARY:
VERIFIED LANDSCAPE. dev.meta.ai lists exactly one speech model, Muse Voice Transcribe (`muse-voice-transcribe-1.0`, "Transcribe speech to text with speaker diarization and turn detection"), and no text-to-speech model (https://dev.meta.ai/models). The Meta Model API docs state it is "speech-to-text only; it does not synthesize speech or provide a speech-to-speech conversation API" (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/models). Muse Spark hosted chat models accept audio only as an `input_audio` content part (wav/mp3, base64 or file_id) on Chat Completions/Responses, and "Audio understanding in Muse Spark 1.3 is currently not fully supported" — the docs use `muse-spark-1.2` for audio and require `max_tokens>=4000` (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/video-understanding). Muse Glimmer is text+image only; Muse Spark Open (816B/42B-active MoE) has a 24 kHz audio encoder but needs multiple 512 GB Mac Studios or a GPU node (https://huggingface.co/blog/meta-muse-spark-open). Hosted providers: Fireworks serves muse-spark-open with "Inputs: text and images" only and its ASR catalog is Whisper (https://fireworks.ai/models/fireworks/muse-spark-open); Baseten serves muse-spark-open with audio via an `audio_url` part (WAV/MP3/FLAC/OGG/M4A, <=25 MB and <=5 min per file, billed as input tokens; hackathon slug `meta-models/muse-spark-open-1026`) (https://docs.baseten.co/inference/model-apis/audio). Neither hosts Omnilingual ASR, Seamless or MMS; HF cards say "not deployed by any Inference Provider"; the official HF Space is paused; an unofficial Replicate wrapper exists (~$0.0071/run, A100).

MUSE VOICE TRANSCRIBE (recommended primary ASR). Realtime WebSocket `wss://api.meta.ai/v1/asr/realtime` plus `POST https://api.meta.ai/v1/asr/transcribe` (multipart WAV, <=10 min/32 MB). First text frame within 10 s is the handshake: `authorization.accessToken:"Bearer <MODEL_API_KEY>"`, `audioEncoding: PCM_24KHZ|PCM_16KHZ` (s16le mono; 24 kHz native = 48,000 B/s), `model`, `mode: PUSH_TO_TALK|ENDPOINTING|DIARIZATION` (default PUSH_TO_TALK), `partialMode CUMULATIVE|DELTA`, `emitAudioProgress`, `keywords[]`, `languageBias[]`. Client sends binary PCM frames (cookbook uses 80 ms = 3,840 B at 24 kHz) and `{"type":"endStream"}`; server emits `transcript{final}`, `speechStart/speechEnd/speechComplete{turnId,transcript}`, `speaker`, `audioProgress`, `error`. Limits: 60-min session, 256 concurrent streams, 16,000 streams/hour, backlog <5 s (close 1008), rate-limit close 1013. 25 languages with code-switching; VAD/endpointing/diarization inside the model; turn-level timestamps only. Price $0.18/hour metered per second, no RPM/TPM (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/speech-to-text, https://dev.meta.ai/docs/api-reference/voice/schemas, https://dev.meta.ai/docs/pricing-rate-limits). Meta's cookbook `06_muse_voice/01_voice_api_fundamentals` captures the mic with `sounddevice.RawInputStream(samplerate=rate, channels=1, dtype="int16", blocksize=rate*80/1000)`, defaults mic mode to ENDPOINTING and disables pacing (https://github.com/meta-models/meta-model-cookbook/tree/main/06_muse_voice/01_voice_api_fundamentals). Accuracy: Meta says #1 by WER on the Artificial Analysis streaming leaderboard at launch (https://dev.meta.ai/resources/blog/meet-muse-voice-transcribe-streaming-speech-to-text/); secondary reporting gives 3.1% AA-WER streaming vs Cartesia 3.4%, ElevenLabs 3.6%, with 0.16 s final-transcript latency after end of speech; weights are closed (https://sqmagazine.co.uk/meta-muse-voice-transcribe-benchmark-closed-weights/).

OMNILINGUAL ASR (recommended offline fallback). Apache-2.0 suite: CTC and LLM variants at 300M/1B/3B/7B plus `_v2`, `LLM_Unlimited_*_v2`, and `LLM_7B_ZS`; CTC-300M = 325M params, 1.3 GiB FP32, ~2 GiB VRAM; LLM-300M is really 1.63B params/6.1 GiB. Hard cap `MAX_ALLOWED_AUDIO_SEC = 40` for CTC/LLM; Meta states "our inference pipeline does not currently support real-time / streaming applications" (https://github.com/facebookresearch/omnilingual-asr). Weights are on dl.fbaipublicfiles.com (HF cards exist but have no transformers path); the transformers port (huggingface/transformers PR #43265) was "ready to be merged" on 2026-10-23 but is unmerged (https://github.com/facebookresearch/omnilingual-asr/issues/5). Mac realities: PyPI 0.2.0 requires Python >=3.10,<=3.12 (https://pypi.org/project/omnilingual-asr/); fairseq2 ships macOS wheels only as CPU variants for torch 2.8.0/2.7.1 and warns of ABI crashes on version mismatch (https://facebookresearch.github.io/fairseq2/stable/getting_started/installation/index.html); fairseq2n dylib hard-links `/opt/homebrew/opt/libsndfile/lib/libsndfile.1.dylib` (verified with otool, so `brew install libsndfile` is mandatory); I also hit `pyarrow requires NumPy 2.0 or newer, found 1.26.4` and had to pin numpy>=2. The GitHub issue on Metal acceleration was closed with "we dont have any relevant experience with Mac" (issue #7), yet `ASRInferencePipeline(device="mps", dtype=torch.float16)` works. MEASURED on this M5 Pro (48 GB, macOS 26.6.2, uv Python 3.12.13, torch 2.8.0) on a 4.7 s `say`-generated command WAV: CTC_300M_v2 CPU fp32 0.18–0.19 s; MPS fp16 0.016–0.031 s after a 3.3 s first-call warm-up; MPS bf16 0.018 s after 2.1 s warm-up; CPU bf16 4.4 s (avoid, bf16 is the library default). Output: "drone can you follow the person in the blue shirt and keep about two metrs away" (lowercase, no punctuation, occasional misspellings). Alternative zero-fairseq2 route: `pip install sherpa-onnx` (2.0.1) with the int8 CTC export (292 MB tarball from the k2-fsa `asr-models` release): 0.68 s @1 thread, 0.19 s @4, 0.125 s @8 for the same clip; 1B int8 (787 MB) 0.27–0.41 s (https://k2-fsa.github.io/sherpa/onnx/omnilingual-asr/python.html). Soniqo's Swift/MLX port (Apache-2.0, no Python API) reports on M5 Pro: CTC-300M MLX 4-bit WER 4.26% LibriSpeech test-clean, RTF 0.005, 0.4 GB; vs Whisper large-v3-turbo 1.71%, Parakeet 2.37% (https://soniqo.audio/benchmarks). Other Meta ASR that runs on MPS today via transformers: wav2vec2-large-960h-lv60-self (Apache-2.0, 315M, English; measured 0.19 s CPU / 0.042 s MPS, uppercase output) and MMS-1B-all (CC-BY-NC 4.0, 1,162 languages; also supported by mlx-audio 0.6.4). Seamless: SeamlessM4T v2 (2.3B) and SeamlessStreaming (2.5B) are CC-BY-NC 4.0, ASR-capable, transformers-supported but heavy and unmaintained since 2023; SeamlessExpressive is speech-to-speech only under a gated license (https://github.com/facebookresearch/seamless_communication).

TTS. No Meta TTS exists on dev.meta.ai or as a 2026 open release (Spirit LM is FAIR-noncommercial and GPU-bound; Audiobox was never released). The only practical Meta TTS is MMS-TTS (facebook/mms-tts-eng: VITS, 36.3M params, CC-BY-NC 4.0, transformers `VitsModel`, 16 kHz; https://huggingface.co/facebook/mms-tts-eng). MEASURED on CPU: load 6.2 s, 0.22–0.31 s to synthesize 2.5–3.3 s sentences (RTF ~0.09), intelligible but robotic; macOS `say` is the non-Meta fallback.

HACKATHON. Organizer page: Oct 26 12:00 PM – Nov 4 9:00 PM ET submissions (https://cerebralvalley.ai/e/meta-ai-developer-hackathon.md); participant mirror of the rules: pass/fail gate "Genuinely uses Meta Model API and/or open-weight models", rubric Impact 30/Meta tooling 25/Demo 25/Depth 20, $10K bonus for open-weight builds, $150 Meta credits per person (claim by Nov 4 9 PM ET), Baseten $100 + 2M TPM, public repo + 3-minute video (https://github.com/meta-ai-hackathon/meta-hack). Rules text seen does not forbid non-Meta components. Side effects of this research: `brew install libsndfile` (1.2.2_1), 1.2 GB in ~/.cache/fairseq2/assets, HF cache downloads, 3.2 GB in the scratchpad bench dir; scripts/logs at /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-russellvancuren-Projects-MetaHackathonDrone/a9780c4a-b149-4e3b-98c5-8dcb0c8772ee/scratchpad/bench/logs/{omni,sherpa,w2v2}.log.

KEY CLAIMS:
- [high] dev.meta.ai lists exactly one speech model, Muse Voice Transcribe (speech-to-text with diarization and turn detection), and no text-to-speech model; Muse Spark/Glimmer pages do not advertise speech input. (https://dev.meta.ai/models)
- [high] Muse Voice Transcribe realtime endpoint is wss://api.meta.ai/v1/asr/realtime (file endpoint POST /v1/asr/transcribe, <=10 min/32 MB WAV); audio is raw s16le mono PCM at 24 kHz (PCM_24KHZ, 48,000 B/s) or 16 kHz; handshake JSON fields are authorization.accessToken ('Bearer <key>'), audioEncoding, model, mode (PUSH_TO_TALK default | ENDPOINTING | DIARIZATION), partialMode, emitAudioProgress, keywords[], languageBias[]; limits 60-min session, 256 concurrent streams, 16,000 streams/hour, <5 s backlog; price $0.18/hour. (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/speech-to-text)
- [high] Meta's docs state Muse Voice Transcribe is speech-to-text only ('does not synthesize speech or provide a speech-to-speech conversation API'), covers 25 evaluated languages with code-switching, has VAD/endpointing/diarization/keyword biasing built in, and returns turn-level (not word-level) timestamps. (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/models)
- [high] Muse Voice Transcribe has no RPM/TPM limit and bills $0.18/hour metered in whole seconds; muse-spark-1.3 costs $1.25/$4.25 per 1M in/out tokens (contributor $0.10/$0.20); sam-3.1 costs $2.50 per 1,000 images and $0.20 per 1,000 video frames (min 50 frames/request) at 3,000 RPM. - [high] Omnilingual ASR (Apache-2.0) ships CTC and LLM variants at 300M/1B/3B/7B plus _v2 and LLM_Unlimited_v2; CTC-300M is 325M params/1.3 GiB; the pipeline hard-caps input at 40 s (MAX_ALLOWED_AUDIO_SEC=40), Meta states it 'does not currently support real-time / streaming applications', device defaults to cuda-else-cpu but accepts 'mps', and weights are on dl.fbaipublicfiles.com with no merged transformers integration (PR #43265 pending as of 2026-10-23). (https://pypi.org/project/omnilingual-asr/)
