VERIFIER MISSED FINDINGS:
- PROJECT-FRAMING RISK (participant wiki, not official): github.com/meta-ai-hackathon/meta-hack info/restrictions.md quotes the official anti-project list incl. 'Anything political or involving war', then under 'Extrapolated — also banned in spirit' lists 'Drones, target recognition, ISR, anything defense'; gray zones: 'Face recognition / identity search' -> 'Search by attributes ("person in red hoodie"), never by identity ("find John")'; '"Suspicious behavior" scoring'; 'Public-space tracking, police, or cross-city person tracking = dead'. Wording implies the drone must be framed as consumer/creator/assistive (self-filming, follow-me sports cam, indoor helper), attribute-based only, never identity or security. Madrobot's rules summary adds 'entrants must own everything in their project'.
- Muse Spark Open audio/Mac facts (https://huggingface.co/blog/meta-muse-spark-open, Oct 15, 2026): inputs text+image+audio+video, audio mono 24 kHz, one audio token per 80 ms (12.5 tok/s); BF16 ~1.5 TB; a 2-bit GGUF runs on ONE 512 GB M3 Ultra at ~18 tok/s and the 3-bit MLX quant needs ~344 GB — so the researcher's 'multiple 512 GB Mac Studios' is wrong, though it remains impossible on 48 GB. Baseten is the only hosted path exposing its audio input (Fireworks = text+image). Per participant wiki, the $10K open-weight bonus counts only Glimmer/Spark Open 'on your own hardware or through partner inference' and 'SAM 3.1 through Meta's API doesn't qualify'.
- Muse Voice Transcribe protocol details not in the summary: audioEncoding is required (no default); partialMode DELTA only for file transcription via text/event-stream; omit languageBias to auto-detect; handshake ack has no `type` field; `error` carries optional errorType/errorCode/errorParam; server closes 1008 if audio arrives below real time for 10 s or backlog >5 s, and also on idle input/output; failed/429 requests are not billed; cookbook utils.py: CHUNK_MS=80 (3,840 B/frame @24 kHz, 2,560 B @16 kHz), DEFAULT_MODE=PUSH_TO_TALK (ENDPOINTING is the --mic default), emitAudioProgress False, absolute-schedule pacing (sleep only when behind). Muse Wonder (muse-wonder-1.0, $4/$20 per 1M) is a second hosted LLM option; muse-spark-1.3 is 3,000 RPM / 4M TPM.
- Fully-local no-fairseq2 voice loop is possible with sherpa-onnx: the same asr-models release ships silero_vad.onnx, and sherpa-onnx provides `sherpa-onnx-vad-microphone-offline-asr` / Python VAD + OfflineRecognizer so push-to-talk or endpointing can be done locally with the Omnilingual int8 CTC export. sherpa-onnx 2.0.1 (PyPI, Oct 26, 2026) ships macOS arm64 wheels for cp312 AND cp313, so it runs on the installed Python 3.13 (unlike omnilingual-asr 0.2.0). The v2 int8 exports (…-v2-int8-2026-02-05) exist only on the GitHub release, not on the sherpa docs models page.
- omnilingual-asr git main (not PyPI) has requires-python '>=3.10,<3.14', pins fairseq2[arrow]>=0.5.2,<=0.6.0 and pyarrow>=20; LLM_Unlimited_*_v2 variants process audio in 15 s segments (src/omnilingual_asr/models/README.md); HF facebook/omniASR-* has no v2/Unlimited repos and no transformers snippet; transformers PR #43265 now targets main with approvals and CI passing (Oct 21-27, 2026), so a `pip install git+…transformers` path (CTC v2 on MPS without fairseq2) may become available within days — worth re-checking before building on fairseq2.
- Soniqo speech-swift ships a Homebrew `speech` CLI and `speech-server` (HTTP/WebSocket, OpenAI-compatible endpoints; macOS 15+, Swift 6+, Xcode 16+), i.e. a Python-callable local route to the MLX 4-bit Omnilingual (193 MB) plus ANE Parakeet TDT/Whisper Turbo; its Omnilingual CoreML export uses fixed 5 s/10 s windows and has no streaming (#195). Artificial Analysis defines streaming latency as 'Time to Final Transcription' from SileroVAD-detected end of speech — use that definition when comparing against the hosted Muse Voice Transcribe.

VERIFIER NOTES: Method: fetched 30+ pages (dev.meta.ai models/docs/pricing/blog/video-understanding/voice schemas/muse-glimmer; GitHub omnilingual-asr README, models README, pipeline.py, pyproject, issues #5/#7, transformers PR #43265; PyPI omnilingual-asr and sherpa-onnx; fairseq2 install docs; sherpa docs + python example + release asset sizes via gh API; soniqo benchmarks + speech-swift repo + omnilingual doc; HF mms-tts-eng card + config.json, mms-1b-all, wav2vec2-large-960h-lv60-self, omniASR-LLM-7B, HF Muse Spark Open blog; seamless_communication README + commits API; Fireworks muse-spark-open page; Baseten audio + Model APIs overview; Cerebral Valley .md and HTML; meta-hack README/tree + info/grading criteria, restrictions, stack and credits, event guide; madrobot.blog; cookbook README/transcribe_stream.py/utils.py; sqmagazine; AA leaderboard pages). Local verification: read omni.log, sherpa.log, w2v2.log and all bench scripts; otool on libfairseq2n; ffprobe on inputs/outputs; printed installed versions; re-ran the MMS-TTS bench. WebSearch budget ran out after 7 searches; Fireworks ASR docs (3 URLs) and AA per-model pages returned 404, and Baseten's library page is JS-only, so those sub-claims stay unverified.

Scorecard: 9 confirmed (1,2,3,4,6,7,8,9,12), 6 partially correct (5,10,11,13,14,15), 0 refuted. No hallucinated endpoints, prices, model ids or limits were found; every dev.meta.ai number checked out verbatim (wss://api.meta.ai/v1/asr/realtime, POST /v1/asr/transcribe 10 min/32 MB, PCM_24KHZ=48,000 B/s, 60 min/256/16,000/5 s, $0.18/h rounded down to whole seconds, 25 languages, turn-level timestamps, muse-spark-1.3 $1.25/$4.25 with $0.15 cached and 3,000 RPM/4M TPM, sam-3.1 $2.50/1k images and $0.20/1k frames with 50-frame minimum). The measured Omnilingual/MPS, sherpa and wav2vec2 numbers reproduce exactly from the logs; MMS-TTS timings reproduced on re-run (0.27-0.33 s synth, RTF 0.09-0.11, 36.3M params, 16 kHz).

Errors a planner should absorb: (a) the 0.16 s Muse Voice Transcribe latency figure has no source — the cited sqmagazine article does not contain it; (b) Soniqo's '>40 s throws' is unsupported — its CoreML export uses fixed 5/10 s windows with pad-or-chunk, and the 4.26% WER is on only the first 200 test-clean utterances; (c) seamless_communication has 2024 and 2026 commits (#572, #579), it is merely low-activity; (d) sherpa 1B int8 is 0.27-0.42 s only at 4-8 threads, 1.46 s single-threaded; (e) the sherpa docs page cited does not contain the API name or v2 assets — those come from the python example file and the asr-models release (292,313,120 B and 787,296,506 B tarballs, 366 MB / 1.03 GB extracted .onnx); (f) Muse Spark Open runs on ONE 512 GB Mac Studio at 2-3-bit, not 'multiple'; (g) the meta-models/muse-spark-open-1026 slug, $150 credits, 30/25/25/20 rubric, $10K open-weight bonus, 3-minute video and public-repo rules all trace to the unofficial meta-hack wiki and secondary blogs — the official Cerebral Valley page only gives dates, $1M pool and partner $100 credits and says Official Rules would be published separately; (h) 'Fireworks ASR = Whisper' and 'nobody hosts Omnilingual/Seamless/MMS' remain unconfirmed from official catalogs (HF cards do say 'isn't deployed by any Inference Provider').

Planning implications verified from sources: Muse Voice Transcribe is the only Meta hosted STT and has no TTS; ENDPOINTING mode with 24 kHz s16le 80 ms frames is the documented realtime recipe; local fallback can be either the fairseq2 pipeline on MPS fp16 (16-31 ms/utterance after a ~3 s warm-up, Python 3.12 only, torch 2.8.0 pinned) or sherpa-onnx int8 CTC (125-190 ms at 4-8 threads, works on Python 3.13, pairs with silero VAD from the same release); Spark LLM audio input goes through muse-spark-1.2 with max_tokens>=4000, or through Baseten's audio_url on Muse Spark Open (counts toward the open-weight bonus; SAM 3.1 via Meta API does not). Biggest unflagged risk is eligibility framing: the participant wiki lists 'Drones, target recognition, ISR, anything defense' as banned in spirit and person-tracking by identity or in public space as a dead zone — the voice-follow drone must be positioned as attribute-based consumer/assistive use, and the team should confirm with organizers before the Nov 4 9 PM ET deadline. Timeline check: today is Nov 3; submissions and the $150 credit claim both close Nov 4 9:00 PM ET; judging Nov 5-8, live final Nov 9 12 PM ET.

RECOMMENDATIONS:
- Primary voice path (all-Meta, hosted): Muse Voice Transcribe realtime WebSocket. Capture with sounddevice.RawInputStream(samplerate=24000, channels=1, dtype='int16', blocksize=1920) (80 ms = 3,840 B frames), send handshake {authorization:{accessToken:'Bearer '+MODEL_API_KEY}, audioEncoding:'PCM_24KHZ', model:'muse-voice-transcribe-1.0', mode:'PUSH_TO_TALK', partialMode:'CUMULATIVE', emitAudioProgress:false, languageBias:['English'], keywords:[command/colour/object vocabulary]}, then endStream on key release and take the transcript frame with final:true. Expect ~0.15 s after end of speech; cost is negligible ($0.18/hour).
- Use push-to-talk (a held hotkey in the existing turbodrone React UI or a global key via pynput) rather than a wake word: it is deterministic around a flying drone, needs no extra non-Meta model (openWakeWord/Porcupine are not Meta and macOS support for openWakeWord is undocumented), and maps directly onto the API's PUSH_TO_TALK mode. Offer ENDPOINTING mode (handle speechComplete per turnId) as a hands-free option behind a flag.
- Offline/no-internet fallback (all-Meta, open weights): Omnilingual ASR CTC-300M-v2. Ship the sherpa-onnx int8 export (pip install sherpa-onnx, 292 MB, 0.13-0.19 s per 5 s utterance on CPU with 4-8 threads, zero fairseq2 dependencies) as the default local engine, and optionally the official omnilingual-asr pipeline with device='mps', dtype=torch.float16 (0.02-0.03 s after warm-up) in a dedicated uv venv pinned to Python 3.12, torch 2.8.0 and numpy>=2 with Homebrew libsndfile; never use the default bfloat16 on CPU (4.4 s). Do a warm-up transcription at startup.
- Local VAD/endpointing for the offline path: silero-vad (pip install silero-vad, MIT, <1 ms per 30 ms chunk at 16 kHz) to cut utterances, keeping each segment under the 40 s Omnilingual cap; for the hosted path rely on the model's built-in endpointing instead of a separate VAD.
- Normalize before intent parsing: CTC output is lowercase, unpunctuated and occasionally misspelled ('metrs'), wav2vec2 is uppercase; send the raw transcript plus a fixed vocabulary hint to muse-spark-1.3 with a strict JSON intent schema (action: follow|stop|hover|land|return; target noun phrase for SAM 3.1; optional distance/side), and add a cheap regex fast-path for safety words (stop, land) that bypasses the LLM.
- Keep the pipeline engine-agnostic behind one interface (transcribe(pcm16_24k|16k) -> text, with a hosted MuseVoiceTranscribe backend and a local OmnilingualSherpa backend) and a health check that falls back to local automatically when api.meta.ai is unreachable; the same interface serves the digital twin and the real drone.
- TTS: implement spoken confirmations with MMS-TTS (facebook/mms-tts-eng via transformers VitsModel; 0.22-0.31 s per sentence on CPU, 16 kHz, set_seed for determinism, pre-warm at startup, play via sounddevice). This is the only Meta TTS that is practical; document that it is CC-BY-NC 4.0 and keep macOS `say` as a one-line fallback flag. State clearly in the submission that Meta offers no TTS model on dev.meta.ai.
- Do not build on speech-in LLMs for the control loop: Muse Spark 1.3 audio is 'not fully supported', 1.2 requires max_tokens>=4000 and has unknown latency, Muse Spark Open is cloud-only (Baseten audio_url route, <=5 min clips). At most, use muse-spark-1.2 input_audio as an optional single-hop experiment or as a fallback intent parser when Voice Transcribe is down.
- Resolve the connectivity question first: the X69 is reached over its own Wi-Fi AP (172.16.11.1 RTSP), so the Mac may have no internet while flying; if there is no second network interface, the live-flight build must use local Omnilingual ASR (and local Glimmer/open-weight parsing), while hosted Muse Voice Transcribe/Muse Spark/SAM 3.1 can still be shown in the digital-twin demo.
- Given the Nov 4 9:00 PM ET submission deadline shown on the organizer page, scope voice to: hosted Voice Transcribe PTT + Spark intent JSON + MMS-TTS confirmation first (hours of work using the cookbook code), and add the local sherpa-onnx Omnilingual fallback second; record the 3-minute video with the digital twin if flight tests slip.

RISKS:
- Deadline: the organizer page lists submissions due Nov 4, 2026 9:00 PM ET, i.e., the day after this research (some third-party listings say Nov 9); scope must assume ~24 hours unless the user has different information.
- Internet while flying: the Mac must join the X69's Wi-Fi AP (172.16.11.1) to receive video and send RC; without a second interface, Muse Voice Transcribe, Muse Spark and hosted SAM 3.1 calls will fail mid-flight (inferred from the turbodrone README network addresses, not verified on the hardware).
- Muse Voice Transcribe is closed-weight and cloud-only; a service outage or a 1013 rate-limit close during the demo needs the local Omnilingual fallback to be genuinely wired in, not just planned.
- omnilingual-asr install fragility on macOS: Python <=3.12 only (user has 3.13/3.15), fairseq2<->torch ABI pin (torch 2.8.0), mandatory Homebrew libsndfile, and a pyarrow-vs-numpy<2 conflict observed in this session; bfloat16 default is 20x slower on CPU; 40 s input cap and no streaming support.
- Omnilingual CTC accuracy on English is worse than Whisper-class models (4.26% vs 1.7% WER per Soniqo's M5 Pro benchmark) and output lacks casing/punctuation; command parsing must tolerate misspellings ('metrs', 'dron').
- Licensing: MMS-TTS, MMS-1B-all and all Seamless models are CC-BY-NC 4.0 (Seamless Expressive is gated); fine for a hackathon demo but a problem if the startup track or later commercialization matters. Omnilingual ASR and wav2vec2-large-960h-lv60-self are Apache-2.0.
- No Meta TTS exists; using macOS `say` would weaken the 'all Meta models' story, while MMS-TTS sounds robotic and is English-per-checkpoint.
- Microphone acoustics: brushed-motor prop noise near the laptop will raise WER; mitigate with push-to-talk, keyword biasing and a close-talk mic.
- Hosted audio-in LLM routes are immature: Spark 1.3 audio 'not fully supported', Spark 1.2 needs max_tokens>=4000 and has unmeasured latency, Baseten Muse Spark Open audio is limited to <=5 min/25 MB files with no structured outputs.
- Hackathon rules were only read from a participant's mirror (official Cerebral Valley rules page is login-walled); whether third-party components (e.g., `say`, silero-vad, sherpa-onnx runtime) affect the 'Meta AI tooling' score is unconfirmed.
- Side effects left on this machine by the benchmarks: Homebrew libsndfile 1.2.2_1 installed, ~1.2 GB in ~/.cache/fairseq2/assets, new entries in ~/.cache/huggingface/hub, 3.2 GB under the scratchpad bench directory.

OPEN QUESTIONS FOR USER:
- When the Mac is joined to the X69's Wi-Fi, does it still have internet (second Wi-Fi adapter, Ethernet, or phone tether)? This decides whether hosted Muse Voice Transcribe / Muse Spark / SAM 3.1 can be used during real flights or only in the digital twin.
- Is the submission deadline really Nov 4, 2026 9:00 PM ET (organizer page) or Nov 9 (some listings)? How many hours do you actually have?
- Is push-to-talk (hold a key) acceptable, or is a hands-free wake word a hard requirement?
- Must confirmations be spoken, and is a CC-BY-NC Meta model (MMS-TTS) acceptable, or may macOS `say` be used as the fallback? Which track (Indie vs Startup) are you entering?
- Do you already have a Meta Model API key with the $150 credits claimed, and Baseten access for muse-spark-open-1026?
- English-only commands, or do you need other languages (affects Voice Transcribe languageBias and which Omnilingual/MMS checkpoints to bundle)?
- Is pinning the voice component to a separate Python 3.12 uv environment acceptable (omnilingual-asr does not support your installed 3.13/3.15), or should the local fallback be the sherpa-onnx route only?
- Should the plan include the optional single-hop experiment of sending audio directly to muse-spark-1.2 (input_audio) for intent JSON, or keep strictly to Voice Transcribe -> Spark text?



### Baseten and Fireworks: Meta model availability, fine-tuning, custom deployments  [verifier reliability: high]

SUMMARY:
VERIFIED STATE AS OF 2026-11-03 (hackathon submissions close Nov 4, 9:00 PM ET per https://cerebralvalley.ai/e/meta-ai-developer-hackathon).

FIREWORKS — Meta catalog. Muse Spark Open (`accounts/fireworks/models/muse-spark-open`, 816B/42B-active MoE, 1,040K ctx, text+image in, tool calling, reasoning, created 14 Oct 2026) is serverless on US-only capacity "at standard serverless pricing, with no US-only premium": $1.25 in / $0.15 cached / $4.25 out per 1M (https://fireworks.ai/models/fireworks/muse-spark-open, https://fireworks.ai/pricing). Zero data retention by default (https://fireworks.ai/blog/meta-muse-spark-open). Muse Glimmer 30B (`accounts/fireworks/models/muse-glimmer-30b`, 29.7B dense, 131K ctx, image+text, schema tool calling, Apache 2.0) was removed from serverless on Sept 25, 2026 and is now on-demand only (https://docs.fireworks.ai/updates/changelog, https://fireworks.ai/models/fireworks/muse-glimmer-30b). Llama 4 Maverick/Scout: serverless "Not Supported", on-demand only, image input + function calling, not tunable (https://fireworks.ai/models/fireworks/llama4-maverick-instruct-basic). Llama 3.3 70B left serverless 2026-05-14; 12 Llama IDs lost fine-tuning 2026-10-01. No SAM 3/3.1, DINOv3 or Omnilingual ASR anywhere in the catalog, and custom model upload accepts only listed LLM/VLM architectures (Llama 1-4, Qwen, Mistral, Gemma, GPT-OSS, DeepSeek, GLM, Kimi, LLaVA, Idefics3, Phi-3V...), so a SAM server cannot be hosted on Fireworks (https://docs.fireworks.ai/models/uploading-custom-models). Features: OpenAI-compatible chat at api.fireworks.ai/inference/v1; json_object / json_schema / grammar modes (json_schema disables reasoning output on reasoning models) (https://docs.fireworks.ai/structured-responses/structured-response-formatting); images by URL (<5 MB each) or base64 (<10 MB total), up to 120 per request (https://docs.fireworks.ai/guides/querying-vision-language-models); video input exists only for GLM 5.3 Flash / Qwen3-Omni / Molmo2, not for any Meta model (https://docs.fireworks.ai/guides/video-audio-inputs). Latency: Artificial Analysis measured Muse Spark Open on Fireworks at 59-64 tok/s with 0.85 s first chunk vs Baseten 220 tok/s / 0.25 s (https://artificialanalysis.ai/models/muse-spark-open/providers). No Glimmer provider benchmarks exist (serverless gone); Meta's card gives 74.9 tok/s baseline / 233 tok/s with DFlash on an RTX 5090 and 26.6 / 50.2 tok/s on an M5 Max (https://huggingface.co/meta-models/Muse-Glimmer-30B).

FIREWORKS — fine-tuning and on-demand. Dataset = OpenAI chat JSONL (3 to 3M lines, `messages` + optional `tools`, base64 JPEG/PNG image parts on user messages) (https://docs.fireworks.ai/fine-tuning/fine-tuning-models). Muse Glimmer 30B SFT is FREE via the Managed Training UI and the Serverless Training API until Nov 30 (jobs started before 2026-12-01 00:00 PT; no card needed); Managed SFT supports image examples (receipt-extraction cookbook), typically finishes "in a few minutes"; Serverless path defaults: LoRA rank 8, max-seq-len 2,048, batch 16, 1 epoch (https://docs.fireworks.ai/fine-tuning/training-api/cookbook/muse-glimmer, https://fireworks.ai/blog/train-muse-glimmer-for-free). Paid rates: Managed LoRA SFT $3/1M training tokens for 16-80B; Serverless Training Glimmer $5.86 train / $1.96 prefill / $4.88 sample per 1M; Muse Spark Open LoRA trains on base `muse-spark-open-nvfp4` at $18.58/1M (https://fireworks.ai/pricing, https://docs.fireworks.ai/fine-tuning/training-api/serverless). Trained LoRAs CANNOT be served serverless (serverless LoRA exists only for GLM 5.3 Flash); you must create an on-demand deployment: live-merge = base-model speed, one adapter; multi-LoRA `--enable-addons` needs a BF16 shape and adds 10-30% TTFT (https://docs.fireworks.ai/fine-tuning/deploying-loras, https://docs.fireworks.ai/serverless/specialized-model-inference/overview). On-demand GPUs: H100 80GB and H200 $8.00/hr ($0.134/min), B200 $13/hr, B300 $15/hr, GB300 $20/hr, region-restricted 1.5x (https://fireworks.ai/pricing); A100 exists only in US_ILLINOIS_2; new accounts get GLOBAL quota only (https://docs.fireworks.ai/deployments/regions). Defaults: min replicas 0, scale-to-zero after 1 h (min 5 m), scaled-to-zero requests get an immediate 503 `DEPLOYMENT_SCALING_UP` with no queueing, auto-delete after 7 idle days; no published cold-start figure beyond "up to a few minutes" (https://docs.fireworks.ai/deployments/autoscaling, https://docs.fireworks.ai/guides/ondemand-deployments). Billing is prepaid credits; FAQ still cites a $1 starter credit and account suspension once spent without a card (https://docs.fireworks.ai/faq-new/billing-pricing/what-happens-when-i-finish-my-1-dollar-credit); a third-party tracker claims the $1 credit was dropped Oct 22, 2026 (unverified). Serverless TPM limits are per model; Muse Spark Open sits in the "Medium" tier (https://docs.fireworks.ai/serverless/rate-limits).

BASETEN — Meta catalog. Model APIs (OpenAI-compatible at https://inference.baseten.co/v1, plus Responses and Anthropic Messages beta) serve Muse Spark Open as `meta-models/muse-spark-open` at $1.25/$0.15/$4.25, NVFP4, 1M ctx, tool calling + reasoning (https://www.baseten.co/library/muse-spark-open/, https://www.baseten.co/blog/muse-spark-open-launch/). It is the only Model API model accepting video AND audio: up to 8 images (10 MB each), 2 videos (<=300 s, <=200 MB, MP4/MOV/MKV/WebM, H.264/H.265/VP9/AV1, up to 4K60, 96 frames sampled, audio track ignored), 4 audio files (<=25 MB / 5 min; WAV/MP3/FLAC/OGG/M4A), 100 MiB body (https://docs.baseten.co/inference/model-apis/vision.md, https://docs.baseten.co/inference/model-apis/audio.md). Caveat: structured outputs are enforced on all Model API models EXCEPT Muse Spark Open (json_schema accepted, not enforced); `n>1` returns 400 (https://docs.baseten.co/inference/structured-outputs.md, https://docs.baseten.co/development/model-apis/overview). Default rate limits: Basic unverified 15 RPM / 100K TPM, verified 120 RPM / 500K TPM, Pro 120 RPM / 1M TPM (https://docs.baseten.co/inference/model-apis/pricing-and-limits.md); hackathon grants 2M TPM. Muse Glimmer 30B is dedicated-only (no per-token API): BF16 on a single H100 via vLLM with a 2.56B DFlash speculative drafter, slug `meta-models/Muse-Glimmer-30B`, endpoint `https://model-{id}.api.baseten.co/environments/production/sync/v1`, tool calling, reasoning strength via system prompt (https://www.baseten.co/library/muse-glimmer-30b/). Library also lists Llama 4 Scout/Maverick, Llama 3.3 70B, Llama 3.2 11B/90B Vision, Llama 3.1 8B/70B/405B as dedicated deployments (Llama 4 Scout example: vLLM, H100:4, FP8, 128K served, <=10 images/prompt, https://docs.baseten.co/examples/models/llm/llama-4.md). No SAM, DINOv3 or Omnilingual ASR in the library (ASR options are Whisper, Qwen3-ASR, Nemotron ASR).

BASETEN — training and custom deployments. Dedicated GPU per-minute: T4 $0.01052, L4 $0.01414, A10G $0.02012, A100 80GB $0.06667 ($4.00/hr), RTX-PRO-6000 96GB $0.06667, H100 MIG $0.0625, H100 $0.10833 ($6.50/hr), H200 $0.125, B200 $0.16633 ($9.98/hr) (https://docs.baseten.co/deployment/resources.md). Billing per minute (partial minutes round up), cold starts billed, scaled-to-zero and image pulls not billed, post-paid invoices at $50 or month-end, new workspaces get unspecified credits (third parties say $30) (https://docs.baseten.co/organization/billing.md). No published cold-start durations; four phases (acquire, pull image, pull weights via BDN, load) and "set min_replica >= 1" for latency-sensitive work (https://docs.baseten.co/deployment/autoscaling/startup-times.md). Any PyTorch model deploys as a Truss (`config.yaml` with `accelerator: H100`, Python `Model.load/predict`), including WebSocket transport (`runtime.transport.kind: websocket`, `wss://model-{MODEL_ID}.api.baseten.co/environments/{env}/websocket`, 100 MiB per message, connection pinned to one replica) (https://docs.baseten.co/development/model/websockets.md). Baseten Training = bring-your-own container on H100/H200 (`baseten train push --config config.py`, TRL/Axolotl/VeRL/MS-Swift), checkpoints auto-synced and deployable with `baseten train checkpoint deploy` (vLLM); quickstart LoRA run ~133 s (https://docs.baseten.co/training/overview, https://docs.baseten.co/training/getting-started.md). Baseten Loops (LoRA SDK) supports `meta-models/Muse-Spark-Open-816B-A42B(-NVFP4)` with vision but NOT Glimmer or Llama (https://docs.baseten.co/loops/supported-models.md); Meta's own Glimmer recipe (Axolotl QLoRA verified on 1xH100, 27-29 GB peak) fits a Baseten Training Job (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/muse-glimmer/fine-tuning).

CONTEXT NEEDED FOR THE PLAN. Meta Model API (https://api.meta.ai/v1) serves muse-spark-1.3 ($1.25/$4.25, 3,000 RPM/4M TPM, text/image/video/audio), sam-3.1 via streaming `POST /v1/responses` ($2.50/1k images, $0.20/1k frames with 50-frame minimum, <=15,000 frames, <=16 tracked objects, MP4/MOV H.264/H.265 only; images/clips must be uploaded or URL-hosted, so it is not a live-stream tracker) and muse-voice-transcribe-1.0 realtime WebSocket at $0.18/hr; Muse Glimmer and Muse Spark Open are NOT on Meta Model API (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/sam/segmenting, https://dev.meta.ai/docs/pricing-rate-limits, https://dev.meta.ai/docs/models). SAM 3.1 open weights: `facebook/sam3.1` (gated), SAM License (royalty-free, derivatives allowed, no military/espionage/weapons uses), 848M params, requires Python >=3.12, PyTorch >=2.7, CUDA >=12.6 with no MPS/CPU support claimed, 16->32 fps on one H100 at medium object counts, ~30 ms/image on H200 (https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam3, https://ai.meta.com/blog/segment-anything-model-3/). Hackathon partner credits: Baseten $100 + 2M TPM, Fireworks $100 (other write-ups say $25), Modal $100, all "select participants"; Meta $150 API credits per invite emails.

COMPARISON. (a) Muse orchestrator: both serve Muse Spark Open at identical token prices with tool calling; Baseten is ~3.5x faster with 0.25 s first chunk and uniquely accepts video+audio, but does not enforce JSON schema on this model; Fireworks enforces json_schema/grammar and is US-only, slower. Glimmer requires a dedicated H100 on either (Baseten $6.50/hr with DFlash drafter prebuilt; Fireworks $8/hr), or local Mac inference (Meta verifies SGLang MLX on M5 Pro with 48 GB minimum; 17-20 GB GGUF builds; https://dev.meta.ai/docs/muse-glimmer/deploy). (b) SAM 3.1 GPU service: only Baseten can host it (arbitrary Truss + WebSocket); Fireworks cannot. Within a 150-300 ms RTT budget: ~30 ms server inference + ~1-3 ms JPEG encode + US internet RTT (typ. 30-80 ms, region not selectable on Baseten self-serve) is feasible over a persistent WebSocket at 5-10 Hz with 640x360 JPEG frames, provided min_replica=1 during flights.

KEY CLAIMS:
- [high] Fireworks serves Muse Spark Open serverless (model id accounts/fireworks/models/muse-spark-open) on US-only capacity at standard pricing $1.25 input / $0.15 cached / $4.25 output per 1M tokens, 1,040K context, text+image input, tool calling; fine-tuning only via the Serverless Training API on base muse-spark-open-nvfp4 with on-demand serving. (https://fireworks.ai/models/fireworks/muse-spark-open)
- [high] Fireworks removed Muse Glimmer 30B from serverless on September 25, 2026 (announced 2026-09-12, effective per 2026-09-26 changelog); it remains available only as an on-demand (dedicated GPU) deployment; Llama 4 Maverick/Scout are likewise on-demand only and not tunable. (https://docs.fireworks.ai/updates/changelog)
- [high] Muse Glimmer 30B supervised fine-tuning on Fireworks is free (no credit card) through the Managed Training UI and Serverless Training API for jobs started before 2026-12-01 00:00 PT; Managed SFT accepts image examples; Serverless defaults are LoRA rank 8, max-seq-len 2,048, batch 16, 1 epoch; trained LoRAs can only be served on an on-demand deployment billed per GPU-hour. (https://docs.fireworks.ai/fine-tuning/training-api/cookbook/muse-glimmer)
- [high] Fireworks on-demand GPU pricing: H100 80GB and H200 141GB $8.00/hr ($0.134/min), B200 $13/hr, B300 $15/hr, GB300 $20/hr; region-restricted deployments cost 1.5x; idle deployments scale to zero after one hour by default. Managed LoRA SFT costs $3.00/1M training tokens for 16.1B-80B models; Serverless Training for Muse Glimmer 30B is $5.86/1M train tokens. (https://fireworks.ai/pricing)
- [high] Fireworks custom model upload supports only listed LLM/VLM architectures (Llama 1-4, Qwen, Mistral/Mixtral, Gemma, GPT-OSS, DeepSeek, GLM, Kimi, LLaVA, Idefics3, Phi-3V, etc.), not arbitrary PyTorch models such as SAM; uploaded LoRA adapters (rank 4-64) deploy only to on-demand deployments. No SAM, DINOv3 or Omnilingual ASR model exists in the Fireworks catalog. (https://docs.fireworks.ai/models/uploading-custom-models)
- [high] Fireworks on-demand deployments default to min replicas 0, scale-to-zero window 1h (minimum 5m), scale-down 10m; a request to a scaled-to-zero deployment returns HTTP 503 DEPLOYMENT_SCALING_UP immediately with no queueing, so clients must retry; deployments idle 7 days are auto-deleted; no specific cold-start duration is published. (https://docs.fireworks.ai/deployments/autoscaling)
- [high] Baseten Model APIs (OpenAI-compatible base URL https://inference.baseten.co/v1) serve Muse Spark Open as slug meta-models/muse-spark-open at $1.25/$0.15/$4.25 per 1M tokens; it is the only Model API model that accepts video (up to 2 clips <=300 s, 96 frames sampled, MP4/MOV/MKV/WebM) and audio (up to 4 files <=25 MB / 5 min) in addition to up to 8 images per request. (https://docs.baseten.co/inference/model-apis/vision.md)
- [high] On Baseten Model APIs, structured outputs (json_schema) are enforced for all language models except Muse Spark Open, which accepts json_schema but does not enforce it; Muse Spark Open also ignores logprobs and returns HTTP 400 for n>1. (https://docs.baseten.co/inference/structured-outputs.md)
- [high] Baseten offers Muse Glimmer 30B only as a dedicated deployment (no per-token Model API): BF16 weights on a single H100 served by vLLM with a 2.56B-parameter DFlash speculative-decoding drafter, slug meta-models/Muse-Glimmer-30B, endpoint https://model-{model_id}.api.baseten.co/environments/production/sync/v1, with tool calling and system-prompt reasoning strength. (https://www.baseten.co/library/muse-glimmer-30b/)
- [high] Baseten dedicated GPU prices per minute: T4 $0.01052, L4 $0.01414, A10G $0.02012, A100 80GB $0.06667 ($4.00/hr), RTX-PRO-6000 96GB $0.06667, H100 MIG 40GB $0.0625, H100 $0.10833 ($6.50/hr), H200 $0.125, B200 $0.16633; selected via config.yaml accelerator or instance_type; multi-node via node_count. (https://docs.baseten.co/deployment/resources.md)
- [high] Baseten bills per minute (partial minutes round up), including cold-start/model-loading time and warm idle replicas, but not scaled-to-zero time or image pulls; billing is post-paid (invoice at $50 or month end); new workspaces receive credits of an unstated amount; no published cold-start durations exist, and docs recommend min_replica >= 1 for latency-sensitive services (startups that exceed 30 minutes are marked Stalled). (https://docs.baseten.co/organization/billing.md)
- [high] Baseten dedicated deployments support a WebSocket transport (config runtime.transport.kind: websocket; async websocket() handler; URL wss://model-{MODEL_ID}.api.baseten.co/environments/{ENV}/websocket; 100 MiB per message; connections pinned to a replica), enabling a persistent low-latency frame-in/bbox-out SAM 3.1 tracking channel. (https://docs.baseten.co/development/model/websockets.md)
- [high] Baseten Training runs bring-your-own-container jobs on H100/H200 (baseten train push --config config.py; TRL, Axolotl, VeRL, MS-Swift) with checkpoints deployable via baseten train checkpoint deploy (vLLM); the quickstart LoRA job trains in ~133 s. Baseten Loops (LoRA SDK) supports meta-models/Muse-Spark-Open-816B-A42B and the NVFP4 vision variant but not Muse Glimmer 30B or Llama. (https://docs.baseten.co/loops/supported-models.md)
- [medium] Artificial Analysis measured Muse Spark Open at 220 tok/s output with 0.25 s first chunk on Baseten versus 59-64 tok/s and 0.85 s on Fireworks (Makora 320-356 tok/s, Nebius 197, Together 144), all at $1.25/$4.25; all five providers support function calling and JSON mode. (https://artificialanalysis.ai/models/muse-spark-open/providers)
- [high] Meta Global AI Developer Hackathon: submissions due Nov 4, 9:00 PM ET; partner credits for select participants are Baseten $100 + 2M TPM, Modal $100, Fireworks $100 (plus workshop); Meta Model API serves sam-3.1 via streaming POST https://api.meta.ai/v1/responses at $2.50/1k images and $0.20/1k video frames (50-frame minimum, <=15,000 frames, <=16 tracked objects, MP4/MOV H.264/H.265 only), while Muse Glimmer and Muse Spark Open are not served on Meta Model API. (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/sam/segmenting)

VERIFIER CORRECTIONS (non-confirmed claims):
- [partially_correct] 7. Baseten Model APIs (https://inference.baseten.co/v1) serve meta-models/muse-spark-open at $1.25/$0.15/$4.25; it is the only Model API model accepting video (2 clips <=300 s, 96 frames, MP4/MOV/MKV/WebM) and audio (4 files <=25 MB / 5 min) plus up to 8 images. -> Slug, price (confirmed on Baseten pricing page and library page), video limits (2 clips, 200 MB, 300 s, H.264/H.265/VP9/AV1, up to 4K60, 96 frames sampled, audio track ignored), audio limits (4 files, 25 MB, 5 min, WAV/MP3/FLAC/OGG/M4A, 8-48 kHz) and 8 images x 10 MB / 100 MiB body are all correct. But Muse Spark Open is NOT the only video-capable Model API model: moonshotai/Kimi-K3 also accepts video (up to 12 clips, each <=150 MB and <=30 s). It is the only Model API model that accepts audio, hence the only one accepting both video and audio. Also note a 200 MB combined media (images+video+audio) cap per request. (https://docs.baseten.co/inference/model-apis/vision.md)
- [partially_correct] 14. Artificial Analysis: Muse Spark Open 220 tok/s and 0.25 s first chunk on Baseten vs 59-64 tok/s and 0.85 s on Fireworks (Makora 320-356, Nebius 197, Together 144), all at $1.25/$4.25; all five support function calling and JSON mode. -> Speeds/first-chunk figures match the AA table (Baseten 222 t/s / 0.25 s; Fireworks 64 t/s / 0.85 s; Makora 356 table vs 320.2 headline / 0.65 s; Nebius 197 / 1.47 s; Together 144 / 0.68 s). But $1.25/$4.25 is shown only for Baseten, Fireworks and Nebius; Makora and Together prices are not stated on the page. Function calling/JSON mode 'all five' comes from the page FAQ, with no supporting table entry for Makora. Also 'first chunk' is not time-to-first-answer-token on this reasoning model (Makora: 0.65 s first chunk vs 6.92 s headline TTFT), so budget for reasoning tokens before the final answer. (https://artificialanalysis.ai/models/muse-spark-open/providers)
- [partially_correct] 15. Hackathon submissions due Nov 4, 9:00 PM ET; partner credits Baseten $100 + 2M TPM, Modal $100, Fireworks $100; Meta Model API serves sam-3.1 via streaming POST https://api.meta.ai/v1/responses at $2.50/1k images and $0.20/1k frames (50-frame minimum, <=15,000 frames, <=16 tracked objects, MP4/MOV H.264/H.265 only); Muse Glimmer and Muse Spark Open not on Meta Model API. -> Deadline, credits (official Cerebral Valley page; one team's GitHub issue confirms receiving $100 Fireworks and $100 Modal codes, and that Baseten granted them nothing), prices and limits all check out on dev.meta.ai/docs/pricing-rate-limits and /docs/models. Two precision fixes: (a) accepted video is 'MP4 or MOV containers with H.264, H.265, or MPEG-4 video' (MPEG-4 Part 2 also accepted; VP9/WebM rejected); (b) sam-3.1 inputs may be public URLs, base64 data URLs, or (video) a file_id from POST /v1/files, i.e. per-frame base64 image calls are possible; sam-3.1 rate limit is 3,000 RPM per team with no TPM. The $25 Fireworks figure appears only in a third-party write-up; the $150 Meta credit is sourced only to invite emails, not the public page. (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/sam/segmenting)

VERIFIER MISSED FINDINGS:
- SAM 3.1 on Meta Model API can be driven per frame: input_image accepts a base64 data URL (video_url accepts data URL or file_id from a synchronous POST /v1/files upload), the 50-frame minimum applies only to video requests, and the limit is 3,000 RPM per team with no TPM. At 5-10 Hz (300-600 RPM) per-frame image detection costs $2.50/1k images = $0.75-$1.50 per flight-minute; latency is unpublished and must be measured. So 'not a live-stream tracker' is true for the video-tracking mode but not for per-frame image calls. (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/sam/segmenting, https://dev.meta.ai/docs/pricing-rate-limits)
- Meta ASR specifics for the voice front-end: muse-voice-transcribe-1.0 realtime WebSocket is wss://api.meta.ai/v1/asr/realtime, file endpoint POST /v1/asr/transcribe, $0.18/audio-hour, limits 256 concurrent streams and 16,000 streams/hour. The models page also notes muse-spark-1.3 'audio input not fully supported', so do not route raw audio through Spark 1.3. (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/models, https://dev.meta.ai/docs/pricing-rate-limits)
- Running Muse Glimmer locally on the 48 GB M5 Pro: Meta verifies SGLang+MLX at 'M5 Pro (48 GB minimum)' but the MLX backend 'is text only and has no speculative decoding'; vision locally requires llama.cpp GGUF (Q4_K_M ~17 GB + 1.4 GB mmproj, DFlash draft ~1.6 GB) or LM Studio (Q4_K_M measured ~24.1 GiB at 16K context); Ollama path is `ollama run muse-glimmer:30b-mlx` and needs Ollama >=0.32.7; bf16 serving needs ~72 GB VRAM (vllm/vllm-openai:muse-glimmer image, CUDA 13.0). (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/muse-glimmer/deploy)
- Baseten Model API nuances: moonshotai/Kimi-K3 is a second video-capable model (12 clips <=30 s / 150 MB each, 96 images), useful as a fallback; Muse Spark Open has a 200 MB combined-media cap; Baseten's library page advertises 'Structured Outputs' for Spark Open while docs say the schema is not enforced, so validate JSON client-side and retry. Default account limits are 15 RPM/100K TPM unverified, 120 RPM/500K TPM verified, 120 RPM/1M TPM Pro (hackathon 2M TPM is a grant). Image builds after `baseten model push` ARE billed (only image pulls/scheduling are free); `--watch` dev deployments are not billed. (https://docs.baseten.co/inference/model-apis/vision.md, https://docs.baseten.co/organization/billing.md)
- Fireworks on-demand details a planner needs: 'No charge for On Demand start-up time'; default max replicas 1 and scale-up window 30 s; new accounts receive GPU quota for the GLOBAL multi-region only (single regions start at zero, so US-only pinning may need a quota grant and costs 1.5x); A100 80GB exists only in US_ILLINOIS_2 and is absent from the pricing page; AMD MI325X/MI350X are also offered; Muse Spark Open has a 'Priority' serverless tier at $1.875/$0.225/$6.375. The Glimmer free-SFT promotion covers LoRA SFT only (no DPO/RL/full-parameter) and the serverless path refuses rows >2,048 tokens rather than truncating. (https://fireworks.ai/pricing, https://docs.fireworks.ai/deployments/regions, https://docs.fireworks.ai/deployments/autoscaling)
- SAM 3.1 open weights: the GitHub README documents SAM 3.1 'Object Multiplex' (RELEASE_SAM3p1.md), requires Python >=3.12, PyTorch >=2.7, CUDA >=12.6 (install example pins torch==2.10.0 cu128), 848M params, SAM License; the HF repo facebook/sam3.1 is gated ('License: other') and claims '~7x faster inference at 128 objects on a single H100'. No MPS/CPU support is mentioned anywhere, and the only 'sam3-apple-silicon' GitHub repo found is an unmodified fork (6 stars, 5 commits, CUDA-only README), so treat Mac-local SAM 3.1 as unavailable and plan on Baseten (Truss + WebSocket) or Meta API. Baseten's library (245 entries) has no SAM/DINOv3/Omnilingual entries; its ASR options are whisper variants, qwen-3-asr-1-7b, nvidia-nemotron-3-asr-english, nvidia-nemotron-35-asr-multilingual, qwen3-omni, nemotron-3-nano-omni. (https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam3, https://huggingface.co/facebook/sam3.1)

VERIFIER NOTES: Method: 36 distinct pages fetched; every claim checked against its cited URL plus at least one independent page where one exists (Fireworks model pages + changelog + pricing; Baseten docs + pricing page + library payloads; dev.meta.ai pricing + models + SAM docs; Cerebral Valley page + two participant write-ups). Baseten marketing pages (library, pricing, blog) are fully client-rendered and return only a title via WebFetch; I read them by curling the HTML and searching the embedded Next.js payload, which is where the Glimmer 'BF16 model on a single H100 through an OpenAI-compatible vLLM API ... 2.56-billion-parameter DFlash assistant' text, the slug/endpoint code sample, and the $1.25/$0.15/$4.25 pricing rows live. The Glimmer library page exposes only a 'Dedicated deployment' action while the Spark Open page exposes both 'Dedicated deployment' and 'Model API', confirming dedicated-only. Fireworks' catalog (329 models) is lazy-loaded, so 'no SAM/DINOv3/Omnilingual on Fireworks' was verified by URL probes (fireworks.ai/models/fireworks/{sam3,sam-3,sam3p1,sam-3p1,segment-anything-3,dinov3,omnilingual-asr} all 404), catalog type filters (LLM/Router/Vision/Embeddings/Reranks, no audio), changelog (no SAM/DINO entries; audio inference deprecated 2026-06-10) and the VLM guide, not by exhaustive enumeration; the upload docs say 'most popular model architectures, including' the listed families and never promise arbitrary PyTorch models, so 'cannot host SAM' is a sound inference rather than a quoted statement. Minor internal inconsistencies worth knowing: the Fireworks Spark Open page's functionality table says Fine-tuning 'Not Supported' while its description documents LoRA via the Serverless Training API on muse-spark-open-nvfp4 (managed FT 'not available yet'); the 2026-10-01 changelog entry is headlined 'Managed RFT is paused' but states the 12 Llama IDs can no longer be fine-tuned at all. Baseten hourly figures ($4.00 A100, $3.75 H100 MIG, $6.50 H100, $9.98 B200) come from Baseten's own pricing page 'Approx / hour' column; H200 and RTX-PRO-6000 appear only in the docs table. Neither platform publishes cold-start durations (Fireworks: 'Cold start times vary depending on model size'; Baseten: four phases, 'Stalled' after 30 min, keep min_replica >= 1). WebSearch budget was exhausted after two queries; all later verification used direct fetches. The $150 Meta API credit is attested only by participant write-ups quoting invite emails; the Cerebral Valley page does not state it. Researcher's AA figures mixed table medians and headline values; the only materially misleading item found across all 15 claims is 'only Model API model that accepts video' (Kimi K3 also does).

RECOMMENDATIONS:
- Muse orchestrator PRIMARY = Baseten Model APIs with meta-models/muse-spark-open (0.25 s first chunk, ~220 tok/s, accepts audio and video clips directly, $100 hackathon credits + 2M TPM). Because Baseten does not enforce json_schema on this model, define the intent output as a tool call (tool_choice='required', e.g., follow_target{description, color, class}) and validate with Pydantic + one retry; FALLBACK = Fireworks serverless accounts/fireworks/models/muse-spark-open (same price, enforced json_schema/grammar, slower) or Meta Model API muse-spark-1.3 (same price, 3,000 RPM) behind the same OpenAI-client abstraction with a base_url switch.
- Satisfy the 'train an open Meta model' requirement with Muse Glimmer 30B LoRA SFT on Fireworks Managed Training (free until Nov 30, finishes in minutes, chat-JSONL with optional image parts). Train it on ~300-1000 synthetic voice-command -> structured follow-intent examples (plus a few hundred drone-frame image examples if time allows), then serve the live-merged LoRA on a Fireworks on-demand H100 ($8/hr) with --scale-to-zero-window 5m and --min-replica-count 1 only during demo recording. Keep the base Spark Open path as runtime fallback so the demo never depends on the on-demand cold start.
- If you prefer one vendor and downloadable weights, run the same LoRA on Baseten Training (BYO container, Meta's Axolotl QLoRA recipe verified on 1xH100 at 27-29 GB VRAM, $6.50/hr) and deploy the checkpoint with baseten train checkpoint deploy; checkpoint files are downloadable (7-day presigned URLs), which also lets you merge and run the tuned Glimmer locally on the M5 Pro for the digital twin (GGUF 17-20 GB; Meta verified SGLang MLX on an M5 Pro with 48 GB minimum). Do not plan on Baseten Loops for Glimmer (unsupported) or on serving any Fireworks LoRA serverlessly (only GLM 5.3 Flash qualifies).
- SAM 3.1 tracking service PRIMARY = Baseten custom Truss (Python 3.12 image, torch>=2.7/CUDA 12.6, pip install from github.com/facebookresearch/sam3, HF token as a Baseten secret for the gated facebook/sam3.1 checkpoint) with WebSocket transport: client streams 640x360 JPEG frames (~50-100 KB) at 5-10 Hz, server keeps a video-predictor session per connection, returns bbox/mask-centroid/confidence JSON per frame. Start on H100 ($6.50/hr) for the 32 fps headroom; benchmark A100 ($4.00/hr) or RTX-PRO-6000 ($4.00/hr) as cheaper options. Set min_replica=1 during flights and scale to zero otherwise (Baseten bills cold starts but not scaled-to-zero time). Fireworks is not an option for this component.
- SAM FALLBACKS: (1) Modal (hackathon partner, $100 credits; community sam3-on-modal repo on L40S/H100) with the same WebSocket/HTTP contract; (2) Meta Model API sam-3.1 for first-frame target acquisition and for offline digital-twin regression runs (upload a recorded clip; $0.20/1k frames with 50-frame minimum) — it is not usable as the live 5-10 Hz tracker because inputs must be URL/file-hosted clips and image calls would cost ~$90/hour at 10 Hz. Also build a local tracker bridge (OpenCV CSRT/KCF on the Mac re-seeded by cloud SAM detections at 2-5 Hz) so the control loop keeps running through RTT spikes or a lost replica.
- Voice path with Meta models only: use Meta Model API muse-voice-transcribe-1.0 realtime WebSocket (wss://api.meta.ai/v1/asr/realtime, $0.18/hr) for streaming ASR, or send the recorded WAV straight to Muse Spark Open on Baseten as an audio_url part (transcription + intent in one call). Neither Baseten nor Fireworks hosts Omnilingual ASR; if you want the open model, run omnilingual-asr CTC 300M/1B locally (Apache 2.0, pip install omnilingual-asr, <40 s clips) — verify CPU/MPS execution on the Mac early since the repo does not state device support.
- Control-loop architecture: keep the LLM out of the real-time loop (one call per command, re-planned at <=1 Hz), run SAM at 5-10 Hz over the persistent WebSocket, and run the PID/yaw-pitch-throttle controller at 20-50 Hz on the Mac with dead-reckoning between SAM updates. Add client-side retry/backoff for Fireworks 503 DEPLOYMENT_SCALING_UP and Baseten cold starts, and a health check that falls back to the local tracker if end-to-end latency exceeds 300 ms for 3 consecutive frames.
- Budget guidance for the hackathon window: Glimmer SFT $0 (promo); Glimmer on-demand serving ~4 h x $8 = $32 on Fireworks (or $26 on Baseten H100); SAM H100 on Baseten ~6 h x $6.50 = $39; Spark Open tokens negligible (<$5). This fits inside the $100 Baseten + $25-100 Fireworks + $150 Meta credits if granted; otherwise add a payment method on Baseten (post-paid) and prepay ~$20 on Fireworks.

RISKS:
- Hard deadline: submissions close Nov 4, 9:00 PM ET (tomorrow); any plan that depends on waiting for HF gated access to facebook/sam3.1, Fireworks regional quota, or credit approval can slip past it.
- Network topology: the X69 is a Wi-Fi access point (172.16.11.1) with no internet, so the Mac loses cloud access (Baseten/Fireworks/Meta) unless it has a second interface (Ethernet/USB tether/second Wi-Fi adapter) during flight; without this, the cloud SAM/LLM design cannot run in the real flight at all.
- Fireworks Muse Glimmer is no longer serverless and fine-tuned LoRAs cannot be served serverless; the tuned model therefore always needs an on-demand GPU ($8/hr) with a multi-minute cold start and 503-on-scale-up semantics, so a scaled-to-zero deployment can fail a live demo.
- Baseten publishes no cold-start durations and bills model-loading time; a SAM Truss with a multi-GB CUDA image could take several minutes to warm, so keep min_replica=1 for the demo window and budget for it.
- Baseten Model APIs do not enforce json_schema for Muse Spark Open; unvalidated intent JSON can break the follow controller unless tool-calling + Pydantic validation is used.
- Rate limits: Baseten Basic unverified accounts get only 15 RPM / 100K TPM until verified or hackathon limits apply; Fireworks accounts may be suspended after the $1 starter credit (and a third-party tracker claims that credit was dropped Oct 22, 2026), so add payment methods early.
- SAM 3.1 requires CUDA (Python >=3.12, torch >=2.7, CUDA >=12.6) with no stated MPS/CPU support, so the digital twin on the Mac must call the same cloud tracker or a mock; community CoreML conversions exist but are unofficial.
- Latency budget: Baseten self-serve does not expose region selection and Fireworks new accounts only have GLOBAL quota, so internet RTT to the GPU is not controllable; measure it before committing to a 10 Hz loop and keep the local-tracker fallback.
- Licensing/compliance: SAM License bars military, espionage and weapons-related use and Muse Spark Open uses the Muse Model License; frame the demo as hobby object/person following and keep weights redistribution within license terms (Glimmer is Apache 2.0).
- Serverless Training API default max-seq-len is 2,048 tokens and rows longer are refused; image-bearing examples must use the Managed SFT path, and in-session eval sampling is billed at normal rates even during the free promo.
- Credit amounts for Fireworks are inconsistently reported ($100 on the official event page vs $25 in participant write-ups) and are only for 'select participants'; do not assume them.

OPEN QUESTIONS FOR USER:
- Were you accepted to the hackathon, and which partner credits have you actually received (Baseten $100, Fireworks $25/$100, Modal $100, Meta $150)? Do you already have accounts with payment methods on Baseten and Fireworks, and have you requested/received access to the gated facebook/sam3.1 Hugging Face checkpoint?
- During a real flight the Mac must join the X69's Wi-Fi AP (no internet). Does your MacBook have a second network path (Ethernet adapter, USB/phone tether, second Wi-Fi dongle) so it can reach Baseten/Fireworks/Meta at the same time, or should the flight-ready build run fully offline (local Glimmer + local tracker) with cloud SAM used only in the twin?
- What does 'train an open Meta model' need to mean for your submission: a quick LoRA SFT of Muse Glimmer on synthetic command->intent data (feasible today, free on Fireworks), a vision fine-tune on drone frames, or is a documented training run enough even if the live demo uses the base model as fallback?
- Which hackathon track (indie vs startup) and what is your GPU spend ceiling for the next 48 hours (e.g., $50 vs $200) if partner credits do not land?
- Latency and safety tolerance: is a hybrid tracker (cloud SAM 3.1 re-detection at 2-5 Hz + local OpenCV tracker between updates) acceptable, and should the drone hold position/land automatically if end-to-end latency exceeds ~300 ms or the tracker loses the target?
- Do you want the Muse orchestrator to see video (Baseten Muse Spark Open accepts 300 s clips and audio) for richer scene reasoning, or only single frames plus text intent? This decides between Baseten (video+audio) and Fireworks/Meta (image-only or hosted Spark 1.3).



### Which open Meta model to train, and how, within a hackathon  [verifier reliability: high]

SUMMARY:
INVENTORY (verified Nov 3 2026). Meta's current open-weight LLMs are Muse, not Llama. Muse Glimmer 30B (released 2026-08-10) is Apache 2.0, dense ~29.6B (52 layers, GQA 32q/2kv, sliding-window 2048 with [L,L,L,G] pattern), text+image input via a ~1.8B ViT-G/14 perception encoder, 131k context, BF16 ~55-60 GB, official ~4-bit variants targeting 24/32 GB, with controllable "Reasoning strength: low|medium|high|xhigh" (https://huggingface.co/meta-models/Muse-Glimmer-30B, https://research.meta.ai/blog/introducing-muse-glimmer-open-agentic-model). Muse Spark Open (2026-10-15) is 816B/42B-active MoE, 1M context, Muse Model License (not OSI; secondary sources: $20M-revenue hosted-service threshold, "Built with Muse Spark Open" notice), needs two 512 GB M5 Ultra Mac Studios just to run — not trainable here (https://research.meta.ai/blog/muse-spark-open). No Llama weights newer than Llama 4 Scout/Maverick (Apr 2025); Scout is 109B total/17B active, gated, Llama 4 Community License — too big for a 48 GB Mac (https://huggingface.co/meta-llama/Llama-4-Scout-17B-16E-Instruct). Llama 3.2 3B Instruct (gated, Llama 3.2 Community License) remains the only tiny Llama (https://huggingface.co/meta-llama/Llama-3.2-3B-Instruct). Vision/other: SAM 3/3.1 (848M, SAM License: permissive, no MAU cap, no military; facebook/sam3.1 gated; SAM 3.1 released 2026-03-27 with Object Multiplex, ~7x faster at 128 objects, VOS +2.0 on MOSEv2) (https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam3, RELEASE_SAM3p1.md); DINOv3 ViT-S 21M…7B, gated, DINOv3 License requiring "Built with DINOv3" (https://huggingface.co/facebook/dinov3-vits16-pretrain-lvd1689m); PE-Core B/L/G Apache 2.0 (https://huggingface.co/facebook/PE-Core-L14-336); V-JEPA 2/2.1 MIT, V-JEPA 2-AC is a ViT-g predictor over 7-DoF arm actions (MLX port 2.63 GB) — manipulation-specific, no drone relevance (https://github.com/facebookresearch/vjepa2); CoTracker 3 CC-BY-NC (https://github.com/facebookresearch/co-tracker); Seamless M4T v2 CC-BY-NC-4.0 (https://huggingface.co/facebook/seamless-m4t-v2-large); Omnilingual ASR Apache 2.0, CTC 300M needs ~2 GiB, 96x real-time on A100, ≤40 s clips (https://huggingface.co/facebook/omniASR-LLM-300M).

META MODEL API (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/overview, https://dev.meta.ai/docs/pricing-rate-limits): base https://api.meta.ai/v1, OpenAI/Anthropic-SDK compatible. muse-spark-1.3 $1.25/$4.25 per M tokens (contributor SKU $0.10/$0.20, 150 RPM), 1M context, text+image+video+audio input. sam-3.1: $2.50/1k images, $0.20/1k video frames, min 50 frames per video request, 3,000 RPM. muse-voice-transcribe-1.0: $0.18/hr, realtime wss://api.meta.ai/v1/asr/realtime, 16-bit mono PCM 24 kHz, PUSH_TO_TALK mode, partial/final transcript events (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/api-reference/voice/realtime). Glimmer and Spark Open are NOT API model IDs (self-host or partners). SAM 3.1 API is clip-based: input_video as video_url/file_id (MP4/MOV, H.264/H.265), ≤15,000 frames, ≤16 tracked objects, ONE noun-phrase concept per request, streamed per-frame lines of box+mask tokens with optional confidence; no real-time session API (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/sam/segmenting). SAM 3 paper: "not designed for long referring expressions"; attributes subset cgF1 54.9; the SAM 3 Agent (MLLM proposes noun phrases, inspects masks, retries) reaches 68.5 gIoU on ReasonSeg with Llama 4 Maverick (https://arxiv.org/html/2511.16719v2). Local SAM on Mac: official repo is CUDA-only (Py≥3.12, torch≥2.7); unmerged PR #627 gets SAM 3.1 to ~1.3 fps at 720p (7 GB) on an M5 (https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam3/pull/627) — too slow to be the loop's detector.

FINE-TUNING PATHS. Mac: mlx-lm added muse_glimmer text-tower support (PR merged 2026-08-25; in v0.32.2), its LoRA tuner wraps any Linear/QuantizedLinear so QLoRA on mlx-community/Muse-Glimmer-30B-4bit (19.4 GB incl. vision; ~15 GB text-only; ~18 tok/s inference on an M5 Pro 24 GB) is plausible on 48 GB at seq ≤1024, but no public training throughput exists — smoke-test first (https://github.com/ml-explore/mlx-lm/pull/1710, https://huggingface.co/mlx-community/Muse-Glimmer-30B-4bit, https://github.com/nickwu00/muse-glimmer-mlx, https://github.com/ml-explore/mlx-lm/blob/main/mlx_lm/LORA.md). mlx-vlm also has a muse_glimmer module and a LoRA trainer. Cloud: Meta's official post-training page recommends QLoRA first (verified on 1xH100), LoRA/QLoRA SFT lr 1e-4–3e-4, rank 8–32, alpha 2x rank, effective batch 4–16, 2–5 epochs, ≥~500 examples, include reasoning_content so chain-of-thought is not suppressed (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/muse-glimmer/post-training/). Unsloth: Glimmer QLoRA 22.6 GiB peak at seq 1024/bs1/r16 (fits 24 GB), LoRA >40 GB, vision SFT supported with frozen perception encoder, free Kaggle 2xT4 notebooks, MLX training not yet (https://unsloth.ai/docs/models/muse-glimmer/train). Fireworks: muse-glimmer-30b is LoRA-tunable, SFT FREE until 2026-11-30 (else $5.86/M train tokens), but LoRA models deploy on-demand only ($8/hr H100/H200), no serverless, and Glimmer serverless was deprecated 2026-09-25 (https://fireworks.ai/pricing, https://docs.fireworks.ai/fine-tuning/deploying-loras, https://fireworks.ai/models/fireworks/muse-glimmer-30b). Baseten Training: BYO container on H100/H200/B200, per-minute (~$6.50/hr H100), TRL/Axolotl recipes, checkpoint→vLLM+LoRA deploy (https://docs.baseten.co/training/overview, https://docs.baseten.co/training/getting-started). Modal: H100 $3.95/hr, A100-80 $2.50/hr, L40S $1.95/hr (https://modal.com/pricing). SAM 3 fine-tune: sam3/train/train.py + sam3/train/configs/roboflow_v100/roboflow_v100_full_ft_100_images.yaml (res 1008, bs 1, 20 epochs x 1500 samples, lr_scale 0.1 → transformer 8e-5 / vision 2.5e-5 / language 5e-6, layer decay 0.9, AdamW, bf16 AMP, 2 GPUs/node); community reports ~18–20 GB at bs1 and that COCO_FROM_JSON uses category names as the text query; tracker training not provided (https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam3/issues/163). Paper: 10-shot ODinW13 71.8 AP vs 61.0 zero-shot, 40 epochs at 0.1x LR, bs 2, mask losses off.

HACKATHON: submissions due Nov 4 2026 9:00 PM ET (tomorrow), judging Nov 4–8, finals Nov 9; $1M pool; indie 1st $150K, startup 1st $250K (https://cerebralvalley.ai/e/meta-ai-developer-hackathon). Participant copy of the brief: pass/fail gate = genuine use of Meta Model API and/or Glimmer/Spark Open; rubric Impact 30% / Meta AI tooling 25% / Demo 25% / Technical depth 20%; 3-minute demo video is the primary artifact; public repo; team ≤4; "new work only"; $10K bonus "Best Build on Open Weight Models" (Glimmer or Spark Open on own hardware or partner); $150 Meta API credits (card required), Baseten $100 + 2M TPM, Modal $100, Fireworks $25 (https://github.com/meta-ai-hackathon/meta-hack).

VERDICT: train Muse Glimmer (the only open Meta LLM that fits the Mac and is explicitly rewarded), distilling Muse Spark 1.3 outputs into a LoRA that turns noisy voice transcripts into SAM-ready tracking intents + supervisory decisions; keep SAM 3.1 zero-shot via API (with Agent-style phrase rewriting) and treat a SAM 3.1 detector fine-tune as a GPU-gated stretch.

KEY CLAIMS:
- [high] Muse Glimmer 30B is Apache 2.0, dense ~29.6B params (52 layers) with a ~1.8B ViT-G/14 vision encoder, 131k context, text+image input; BF16 needs ~55-60 GB, official ~4-bit variants target 24 GB / 32 GB; reasoning effort set via 'Reasoning strength: low|medium|high|xhigh' in the system prompt; recommended sampling temp 1.0/top_p 0.95/top_k 64. (https://huggingface.co/meta-models/Muse-Glimmer-30B)
- [high] Muse Spark Open (released 2026-10-15) is 816B total / 42B active MoE with 1M context under the Muse Model License (limited commercial terms); Meta's reference desktop setup is two 512 GB M5 Ultra Mac Studios, so it cannot be run or fine-tuned on a 48 GB Mac; hosted on Baseten, Fireworks, Groq, Modal, Nebius, OpenRouter, Together. (https://research.meta.ai/blog/muse-spark-open)
- [medium] Llama 4 Scout is 109B total / 17B active (16 experts), gated, Llama 4 Community License (700M MAU clause, 'Built with Llama'), recommended inference is 1xH100 with int4; no newer Llama weights than Llama 4 Scout/Maverick (April 2025) were found, Meta's 2026 open releases are Muse Glimmer and Muse Spark Open. (https://huggingface.co/meta-llama/Llama-4-Scout-17B-16E-Instruct)
- [high] Meta Model API (base https://api.meta.ai/v1, OpenAI-SDK compatible) prices: muse-spark-1.3 $1.25 in / $0.15 cached / $4.25 out per 1M tokens (contributor SKU $0.10/$0.002/$0.20, 150 RPM); sam-3.1 $2.50 per 1k images and $0.20 per 1k video frames with a 50-frame minimum per video request and 3,000 RPM; muse-voice-transcribe-1.0 $0.18 per audio hour; Muse Glimmer and Muse Spark Open are not API model IDs. (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/pricing-rate-limits)
- [high] SAM 3.1 API is clip-based, not real-time: video via video_url or Files-API file_id (MP4/MOV; H.264/H.265/MPEG-4), up to 15,000 frames (~10 min) per request, up to 16 tracked objects, one short noun-phrase concept per request (lists/questions unreliable), results streamed as one special-token line per frame (frame marker, object ordinal, box in source pixels, mask; optional confidence via metadata.include_confidence), propagation_direction both/forward/backward; parsers @meta-sam/parser (JS) and meta-sam-parser (Python). (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/sam/segmenting)
- [high] SAM 3 is ~850M params (450M vision, 300M text, 100M detector+tracker), runs 30 ms/image on H200; it is 'not designed for long referring expressions or queries requiring reasoning' (attributes subset cgF1 54.9), and the SAM 3 Agent pattern (an MLLM proposes noun phrases to SAM 3, inspects masks, iterates) reaches 68.5/67.1 gIoU on ReasonSeg val/test with Llama 4 Maverick; 10-shot fine-tuning lifts ODinW13 from 61.0 to 71.8 AP using 40 epochs at 0.1x LR, batch 2, mask losses disabled. (https://arxiv.org/html/2511.16719v2)
- [high] Official SAM 3/3.1 repo requires Python >=3.12, PyTorch >=2.7 and CUDA >=12.6 (no CPU/MPS); SAM 3.1 (2026-03-27, checkpoints facebook/sam3.1 on HF, gated) adds Object Multiplex for ~7x speedup at 128 objects on one H100 and VOS gains on 6/7 benchmarks; fine-tuning entry is sam3/train/train.py with Hydra YAML configs; tracker training code is not provided; SAM License is royalty-free with no MAU cap, forbids military/espionage end uses, requires passing the license along with redistributed materials. (https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam3)
- [medium] The reference SAM 3 fine-tune config (sam3/train/configs/roboflow_v100/roboflow_v100_full_ft_100_images.yaml) uses resolution 1008, train batch 1, 20 epochs x 1500 target samples, lr_scale 0.1 (transformer 8e-4, vision 2.5e-4, language 5e-5 before scaling), vision layer-decay 0.9, AdamW with bf16 AMP, inverse-sqrt schedule, 2 GPUs/node, COCO JSON annotations; community reports ~18-20 GB VRAM at batch 1 and that the COCO_FROM_JSON loader builds the text query from the category name (so set category names to the target phrases). (https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam3/issues/163)
- [medium] SAM 3 on Apple Silicon is slow: an unmerged PR adds MPS/CPU support and measures SAM 3.1 video tracking at ~1.3 fps for 720p with ~7 GB peak on an M5 (CPU ~50x slower); a separate community package reports ~3 s per image on CPU (M4 Pro) with MPS slower than CPU for images, so local SAM cannot serve a 5-10 Hz control loop on the Mac. (https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam3/pull/627)
- [medium] mlx-lm supports Muse Glimmer's text tower (PR #1710 merged 2026-08-25; vision tensors dropped in sanitize(); loads mlx-community/Muse-Glimmer-30B-{bf16,8bit,4bit}), and its LoRA tuner converts any nn.Linear/QuantizedLinear in model.layers, so QLoRA via `mlx_lm.lora` on the 4-bit checkpoint (19.4 GB on disk incl. vision; ~15 GB text-only, ~16 GB peak and ~18 tok/s inference on an M5 Pro 24 GB) should fit a 48 GB M5 Pro at short sequence lengths; no published training tokens/sec for this model exists, so a smoke test is required. (https://github.com/ml-explore/mlx-lm/pull/1710)
- [high] Meta's official Muse Glimmer post-training guidance: start with QLoRA (verified on 1xH100; full FSDP2 needs 8xH100 and peaks ~97 GB/GPU), LoRA/QLoRA SFT lr 1e-4 to 3e-4, rank 8-32 with alpha 2x rank, effective batch 4-16, 2-5 epochs; JSONL `messages` format; at least ~500 high-quality examples for meaningful adaptation (5k-10k+ for strong domain performance); include reasoning traces via reasoning_content or chain-of-thought may be suppressed. (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/muse-glimmer/post-training/)
- [high] Unsloth measured Muse Glimmer 30B QLoRA at 18.22 GiB weights / 22.57 GiB peak (max_seq_length 1024, batch 1, r=16) so it fits 24 GB; plain LoRA needs >40 GB; recommended r=16/alpha=16/dropout 0 (r=32 for harder agentic tasks), lr 2e-4, 1-3 epochs; vision+text SFT supported with the perception encoder frozen; free Kaggle 2xT4 notebooks; MLX training not yet available. (https://unsloth.ai/docs/models/muse-glimmer/train)
- [high] Fireworks: accounts/fireworks/models/muse-glimmer-30b is LoRA-tunable and SFT is free through 2026-11-30 (otherwise $5.86 per 1M train tokens on the Serverless Training API; managed LoRA SFT $3.00/1M tokens for 16-80B models), but trained LoRA models can only be served on on-demand deployments (H100/H200 $8.00/hr, B200 $13.00/hr) — serverless LoRA is not supported and Glimmer serverless inference was deprecated 2026-09-25. (https://docs.fireworks.ai/fine-tuning/deploying-loras)
- [medium] Cheapest rented CUDA for a few-hour job: Modal H100 $3.95/hr, A100-80GB $2.50/hr, L40S $1.95/hr with $30/month free credits on the Starter plan; Baseten Training runs your own container on H100/H200/B200 billed per minute (~$6.50/hr H100) with TRL/Axolotl recipes and one-command checkpoint deployment to a vLLM+LoRA endpoint. (https://modal.com/pricing)
- [medium] Hackathon: submissions due Nov 4 2026 9:00 PM ET, judging Nov 4-8, finals Nov 9; participant copy of the brief lists a pass/fail gate (genuine use of Meta Model API and/or Muse Glimmer / Muse Spark Open), rubric Impact 30% / Meta AI tooling 25% / Demo 25% / Technical depth 20%, a 3-minute demo video as primary artifact, public repo, teams up to 4, 'new work only', $10K bonus for Best Build on Open Weight Models (Glimmer or Spark Open), $150 Meta API credits per person (card required), Baseten $100 + 2M TPM, Modal $100, Fireworks $25 for shortlisted teams. (https://github.com/meta-ai-hackathon/meta-hack/blob/main/info/grading%20criteria.md)
