VERIFIER MISSED FINDINGS:
- Disqualification rules in the participant copy of restrictions.md (https://github.com/meta-ai-hackathon/meta-hack/blob/main/info/restrictions.md) directly constrain a person-following drone: DQ for 'surveillance that moves into public-space tracking, police use, or cross-city person tracking', for 'face recognition by identity rather than by attributes', for 'machines judging people', for 'anything political or involving war', and for 'wrapper projects with one API call and a chat UI'. Attribute-based targeting ('person in the blue shirt') is allowed; identity recognition and public-surveillance framing are not. The SAM License separately bars military end uses. A $5K 'Best Agentic Use-Case' bonus (agent autonomy / multi-step workflows) is also in reach.
- Muse Voice Transcribe realtime has three modes, not just PUSH_TO_TALK (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/speech-to-text): ENDPOINTING ('Model-detected turn boundaries', events speechStart -> transcript... -> speechEnd -> speechComplete keyed by turnId; partials carry no turnId) is the documented choice for voice commands; DIARIZATION 'is not tuned for low-latency, voice-command use'. Sessions run up to 60 min; handshake within 10 s of connect; never buffer more than 5 s of audio ahead; PCM_16KHZ is accepted in addition to PCM_24KHZ; credentials go in the handshake frame, not the Authorization header; batch endpoint POST /v1/asr/transcribe (32 MB body, 10 min audio); 25 languages with code-switching.
- SAM 3.1 is invoked through the Responses protocol: POST https://api.meta.ai/v1/responses with model 'sam-3.1' and a user message of input_text + input_image (URL or base64 data URL) or input_video (video_url, or file_id from POST /v1/files purpose=user_data); non-streaming returns the whole result; export MP4 with '-movflags +faststart' to avoid server remux; concept absent -> response.completed with empty output_text; outputs are non-deterministic ('Results can vary slightly between runs'). The docs give no latency figures and no live/webcam mode, so the >=5-10 Hz loop needs a local tracker (SAM-seeded) between periodic clip re-anchors; the researcher's architecture never names that bridge.
- Tracker fine-tuning for SAM 3.1 exists outside the official repo: HeverLaw/sam3.1-training (announced in issue #163 on 2026-04-13) fine-tunes the SAM 3.1 'tracking (VOS) component' with the image encoder and parts of the detector frozen, merges back into a checkpoint that keeps open-vocabulary detection, supports PNG-mask datasets (EndoVis17/MOSE/DAVIS presets; SA-V untested), Linux CUDA only, not Meta-affiliated. Also from issue #163: several users got 0.000 AP from the Roboflow config and a Hydra "Key 'launcher' is not in struct" error with no fix posted; there are zero maintainer replies in the thread. Budget debugging time if the SAM detector fine-tune stretch is attempted.
- Official Glimmer quantized weights are GGUF only (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/muse-glimmer/quantization): Muse-Glimmer-30B-KQuant-17GB-Q4_K_M.gguf 16.8 GB ('Start here'), KQuant-Dynamic-Q4_K_XL 19.7 GB, plus a required 1.4 GB vision projector for image input and an optional 1.6 GB DFlash drafter; K-Quant-17GB + projector + 131k KV = 19.0 GiB; requires llama.cpp build b10353+ (older builds: 'unknown model architecture: muse-glimmer'). On the 48 GB Mac, llama.cpp/Metal with the official GGUF is the lowest-risk inference path (with vision), while MLX inference/LoRA relies on community conversions: mlx-community/Muse-Glimmer-30B-4bit (mlx-vlm 0.6.12) and mlx-vlm's muse_glimmer package (language.py + vision.py) with trainer/peft/lora.py, so vision-capable LoRA on Mac is plausible via mlx-vlm but equally unbenchmarked. Meta's hardware-platforms page covers only Intel Arc/Xeon and NVIDIA B300; no official Apple Silicon numbers.
- Hosted-model facts the planner may need: muse-wonder-1.0 ($4.00/$0.20 cached/$20.00 per M, 3,000 RPM) is a stronger teacher than muse-spark-1.3 for distillation data; contributor-mode SKUs ($0.10/$0.20) require granting Meta rights to train on your content; Baseten's hackathon credit must be used with model ID meta-models/muse-spark-open-1026 (the docs default meta-models/muse-spark-open is wrong per participant notes) and is gated by use-case review for the first 2,000 participants; Fireworks has no serverless Glimmer (deprecated 2026-09-25) but does serve Muse Spark Open serverless at $1.25/$4.25; mlx-lm v0.32.0 added Muse-Spark-Open-816B-A42B-NVFP4 support (irrelevant at 48 GB). SAM 3 Agent numbers show MLLM choice matters: Gemini 2.5 Pro 77.0/74.0 vs Llama 4 Maverick 68.5/67.1 gIoU; no published numbers for Muse Spark as the agent.

VERIFIER NOTES: Verification run 2026-11-03 against ~45 primary pages: dev.meta.ai (overview, pricing-rate-limits, sam/overview, sam/segmenting, media-segmentation, speech-to-text, api-reference/voice/realtime, muse-glimmer/post-training, muse-glimmer/quantization, muse-glimmer/hardware-platforms, open-weight-models), HF cards (meta-models/Muse-Glimmer-30B, meta-models/Muse-Spark-Open-816B-A42B, meta-llama/Llama-4-Scout, facebook/sam3.1, mlx-community/Muse-Glimmer-30B-4bit), research.meta.ai blogs (Glimmer, Spark Open), facebookresearch/sam3 (README, RELEASE_SAM3p1.md, LICENSE, README_TRAIN.md, roboflow config YAML, issue #163 via gh api, PR #627 via gh api), arXiv 2511.16719v2 (all three 100k-char segments), ml-explore/mlx-lm (PR #1710, tuner/utils.py, models/muse_glimmer.py, tags v0.31.3/v0.32.0/v0.32.2 via gh api), Blaizzy/mlx-vlm tree, nickwu00/muse-glimmer-mlx, benreichman/sam3-mac, HeverLaw/sam3.1-training, unsloth.ai Glimmer train page, fireworks.ai/pricing + model page + docs/deploying-loras, docs.baseten.co training overview/getting-started + baseten.co/pricing (JS-rendered; prices extracted from embedded JSON), modal.com/pricing, cerebralvalley.ai event page, and the meta-ai-hackathon/meta-hack participant repo (README, event guide, stack and credits, restrictions, grading criteria). The session's WebSearch budget was exhausted after four searches; everything after that is WebFetch/gh. Pages that returned nothing useful: baseten.co/pricing as rendered text (recovered from the HTML payload), docs.baseten.co/training/pricing (404), dev.meta.ai/docs/voice and /voice/overview (404; the real page is /docs/speech-to-text), HF Muse-Spark-Open short slug (401; the -816B-A42B slug works but is gated).

Result: 13 of 15 claims confirmed verbatim against the cited source and at least one independent page; 2 partially correct. No hallucinated model names, prices, endpoints, or dates were found. The researcher's inventory and verdict are sound.

Model facts that hold: Glimmer 30B = Apache-2.0, 29.6B incl. the frozen 1.8B ViT-G/14 PE, 52 layers, GQA 32q/2kv, [L,L,L,G] sliding window 2048, 131,072 ctx, 'Reasoning strength: low|medium|high|xhigh', temp 1.0/top_p 0.95/top_k 64, released 2026-08-10. The only official quantized artifacts are GGUF (K-Quant-17GB Q4_K_M 16.8 GB -> 24 GiB target; K-Quant-Dynamic Q4_K_XL 19.7 GB -> 32 GB; +1.4 GB vision projector, +1.6 GB drafter; llama.cpp >= b10353). Spark Open = 816B/42B-active MoE (256 experts, 8 active), 1M ctx, Muse Model License ($20M MaaS revenue trigger, 'Built with Muse Spark Open'), NVFP4 >= 700 GB VRAM; not a local option. No Llama weights after Llama 4 Scout/Maverick (2025-04-05).

API facts that hold: base https://api.meta.ai/v1, OpenAI and Anthropic SDK compatible; muse-spark-1.3 $1.25/$0.15/$4.25 (3,000 RPM, 4M TPM), contributor $0.10/$0.002/$0.20 at 150 RPM (grants training rights), muse-wonder-1.0 $4/$20 also available; sam-3.1 $2.50/1k images, $0.20/1k frames, 50-frame minimum, 3,000 RPM, invoked via POST /v1/responses, <= 15,000 frames and <= 16 tracked objects per clip, one noun phrase, no live mode, no published latency; muse-voice-transcribe-1.0 $0.18/hr at wss://api.meta.ai/v1/asr/realtime with PUSH_TO_TALK, ENDPOINTING (the documented voice-command mode) and DIARIZATION, 60-min sessions, PCM 16-bit mono 24 kHz or 16 kHz. Glimmer and Spark Open are explicitly not API models.

Training facts that hold: Meta's post-training page (QLoRA on 1xH100 ~27 GB peak at 4,096 tokens; full FSDP2 97,340 MiB/GPU on 8xH100; lr 1e-4-3e-4, r 8-32, alpha 2x, batch 4-16, 2-5 epochs, 500+/5-10k examples, keep reasoning_content). Unsloth 18.22/22.57 GiB at seq 1024 bs1 r16 (measured on a 180 GiB card; '24 GiB realistic minimum'); LoRA > 40 GB; MLX training 'coming this month'. mlx-lm: muse_glimmer landed in v0.32.0 (text tower, vision keys dropped in sanitize), the LoRA tuner is type-generic over nn.Linear/QuantizedLinear in model.layers, so mlx_lm.lora on the community 4-bit checkpoint should start; zero published training throughput, so the smoke test is mandatory. Fireworks: Glimmer SFT free through Nov 30 ($5.86/M after), LoRA serving on-demand only at $8/hr H100/H200, serverless Glimmer deprecated 2026-09-25. Baseten: H100 $0.10833/min ($6.50/hr), B200 $9.98/hr on the pricing page but Training docs list only H100/H200; checkpoint deploy = vLLM + LoRA adapter. Modal: H100 $3.95, A100-80 $2.50, L40S $1.95, $30/month Starter credit. SAM 3 fine-tune YAML matches line-by-line; community VRAM ~18-20 GB at bs1; COCO_FROM_JSON category-name behavior is an unanswered community report; the same thread has unresolved 0.000-AP and Hydra 'launcher' errors and no maintainer replies; a third-party VOS-tracker trainer (HeverLaw/sam3.1-training) exists but is unofficial.

Hackathon: Nov 4 9:00 PM ET deadline and Nov 9 finals are on the official page; judging is 'Nov 4 to Nov 8' officially vs 'Nov 5-8' in participant notes. The per-track prize split and the $25 Fireworks figure come only from the participant repo (official page: Fireworks '$100 in credits', no per-track amounts). The gate has two parts (problem-statement fit AND genuine Meta model use). restrictions.md DQ triggers (public-space/police surveillance, identity face recognition, machines judging people, war, one-call wrapper apps) must shape how the follow-me drone is framed: attribute-based target selection, private/consented setting, no identity.

Planning gap not addressed by the researcher: neither the hosted SAM 3.1 API (clip-based, no latency SLA) nor local SAM (1.3 fps on M5 via an unmerged PR) can close a 5-10 Hz loop; the plan needs an explicit local tracker (e.g., SAM-mask-seeded KCF/CSRT/optical-flow or a tiny detector) with periodic SAM re-anchoring, and the digital twin should exercise that bridge.

RECOMMENDATIONS:
- PRIMARY training deliverable (merges options 1 and 4): a 'Muse Spark -> Muse Glimmer' distilled LoRA that converts noisy voice transcripts into a strict JSON tracking intent (action in {follow, track, orbit, hold, takeoff, land, stop, clarify, refuse}; target.noun_phrase <= 4 words and SAM-compatible, target.attributes[], follow constraints like distance/altitude/speed, safety flags) plus a 'supervisor' head that maps a telemetry summary (bbox size/center trend, confidence, frames-since-lost, battery) to {keep, reacquire, hover, land}. This is the cheapest, most reliable, demo-visible training and directly hits the 'Meta AI tooling' 25% and the $10K open-weights bonus (Glimmer served on your own Mac).
- Data recipe (2-3 hours, < $5): write ~60 seed templates x object/attribute/constraint slot lists, hand-write ~200 gold examples (incl. 15% ambiguous -> clarify, 5% unsafe -> refuse, multi-step 'follow the red car then come back'), then have muse-spark-1.3-contributor ($0.10/$0.20 per 1M tokens, 150 RPM; or muse-spark-1.3 at 3,000 RPM) paraphrase and inject ASR noise (fillers, homophones, dropped words, Muse Voice Transcribe-style no punctuation) to reach 2,000-4,000 train / 300 held-out examples; have Spark also emit a 1-2 sentence reasoning_content so Glimmer's reasoning channel is trained, per Meta's guidance. Store as mlx-lm chat JSONL (train.jsonl/valid.jsonl) and also Fireworks/Unsloth `messages` JSONL from the same generator.
- Train on the Mac first: `pip install 'mlx-lm[train]'`; smoke test 20 iters of `mlx_lm.lora --model mlx-community/Muse-Glimmer-30B-4bit --train --data data/ --fine-tune-type lora --num-layers 16 --batch-size 1 --grad-accumulation-steps 8 --max-seq-length 1024 --grad-checkpoint --mask-prompt --learning-rate 1e-4 --iters 20` with a YAML `lora_parameters: {rank: 16, scale: 32, dropout: 0.0}`; if memory is tight raise the GPU wired limit (`sudo sysctl iogpu.wired_limit_mb=40000`) or drop to --num-layers 8 / seq 512. Full run: 600-1,000 iters (~2 epochs), evaluate with `--test`, then serve with `mlx_lm.server --model mlx-community/Muse-Glimmer-30B-4bit --adapter-path adapters/ --port 8080` (OpenAI-compatible) and call it with 'Reasoning strength: low' and max_tokens ~150 so each intent parse stays ~5-8 s at ~18 tok/s.
- Fallback training paths if MLX training is slow/OOM: (a) Fireworks free SFT (`firectl dataset create`, `firectl sftj create --base-model accounts/fireworks/models/muse-glimmer-30b --lora-rank 16 --epochs 2`), then an on-demand H100 deployment ($8/hr) only during demo recording; (b) Unsloth Glimmer QLoRA notebook on a Modal L40S/A100/H100 ($2-4/hr, ~30-60 min for ~3k examples), `merge_and_unload()` -> `mlx_lm.convert -q` -> serve locally. Decide by Nov 3 evening; do not run both.
- Evaluation to show in the README/video: on the 300 held-out examples report JSON-valid %, per-field exact match, noun-phrase SAM-compatibility (noun-headed, <= 4 words), clarify/refuse precision-recall, and median latency for (i) base Glimmer with a strong prompt, (ii) Glimmer+LoRA, (iii) muse-spark-1.3 teacher. Also run a closed-loop test in the digital twin: 50 scripted voice commands -> time-to-first-lock and mean tracking error.
- Keep SAM 3.1 zero-shot via the Meta API for the demo (no training): acquisition sends a single frame or a ~50-frame (1-2 s) clip with ONE noun phrase (16-object cap, 50-frame minimum billing = ~$0.01 per re-acquire); the Glimmer LoRA produces the phrase and, SAM 3 Agent-style, picks among candidates using attributes (e.g., ask Glimmer-vision 'which box is the person in the blue shirt?' or do a cheap HSV shirt-color check on each mask); between API calls run a local tracker at 10-20 Hz (OpenCV CSRT/KCF seeded by the SAM box; CoTracker3 only if a GPU endpoint exists) and re-acquire every 1-3 s or on drift. Do not try to run SAM 3.1 locally on the Mac in the loop (~1.3 fps).
- STRETCH (only if a CUDA GPU and >= 4 free hours exist): SAM 3.1 detector fine-tune for drone-view attribute concepts. Capture 300-600 frames from the twin and the real X69 (720p HEVC), pseudo-label with sam-3.1 ('person', 'car') and assign attribute phrases with Glimmer-vision, write COCO JSON where category names are the phrases ('person in blue shirt', 'green car'), copy roboflow_v100_full_ft_100_images.yaml (res 1008, bs 1, 20 epochs, lr_scale 0.1, bf16), run on 1xH100 on Modal (~1-2 h, ~$5-10) with `python sam3/train/train.py -c <cfg> --use-cluster 0 --num-gpus 1`; evaluate AP on held-out real frames vs zero-shot; deploy as a Modal endpoint used as the twin's detector. If time is short, skip and keep the Agent-style rewriting.
- Mac-only fallback stretch: behavior-clone the follow policy in the twin using DINOv3 ViT-S/16 (21M, request gated access now; add 'Built with DINOv3') or PE-Core B/16 (Apache 2.0) frozen features + target mask channel -> small MLP outputting yaw/pitch/throttle; ~20k frames from the PD expert, minutes to train on MPS, report tracking error vs the hand-tuned controller, and gate it behind the classical controller for real flights.
- Explicitly do NOT spend hackathon time on: Llama 4 Scout/Maverick or Muse Spark Open fine-tuning (memory), V-JEPA 2-AC (7-DoF arm-manipulation world model; no drone value), Seamless/CoTracker in anything meant to be commercial (CC-BY-NC), or training the SAM tracker (code not provided).
- Voice front end: use muse-voice-transcribe-1.0 realtime (wss://api.meta.ai/v1/asr/realtime, PCM16 mono 24 kHz, PUSH_TO_TALK, read `final: true` events) for the live demo and keep Omnilingual ASR CTC 300M (Apache 2.0, <40 s clips) as an offline fallback; both count as Meta tooling.
- Compliance/logistics today: request gated access to facebook/sam3.1, facebook/dinov3-*, meta-llama/* on Hugging Face; claim the $150 Meta API credits (card + spend limit) and Baseten/Modal credits; put the SAM License, Apache 2.0 (Glimmer), and any 'Built with' notices in the repo; make the repo public before submitting; produce the 3-minute video with twin + real flight footage.

RISKS:
- Deadline: submissions close Nov 4 2026 9:00 PM ET (about one day from now). Any training that cannot finish inside ~2-3 wall-clock hours, including data generation and eval, endangers the demo; the LoRA plan must be time-boxed with the prompt-only Glimmer path as the always-working fallback.
- mlx-lm QLoRA on Muse Glimmer has no public benchmark; risks are OOM on 48 GB at longer sequences, slow throughput (30B dense), or chat-template mismatch with Glimmer's harmony-style reasoning channel. Mitigate with a 20-iteration smoke test and the Fireworks free-SFT / Unsloth-on-Modal fallbacks.
- Training on final-answer-only data can suppress Glimmer's reasoning or tool-calling reliability (Meta warns about this); include short reasoning_content and evaluate general tool-calling after tuning.
- SAM 3.1 API is clip-based with unknown latency and a 50-frame billing minimum; a 5-10 Hz loop cannot call it per frame. Attribute phrases ('person in blue shirt') are a known weakness (attributes cgF1 54.9), so target disambiguation must happen outside SAM (Glimmer vision check or color heuristics).
- Local SAM on the Mac is ~1.3 fps via an unmerged PR; the official repo requires Linux/CUDA (Py >= 3.12, CUDA >= 12.6) for both inference and training, so the SAM fine-tune stretch depends on rented GPUs and on COCO/Hydra config friction reported by the community.
- Fireworks LoRA serving is on-demand only ($8/hr) and Glimmer serverless is deprecated; hackathon Fireworks credits are only $25 for shortlisted teams. Together/OpenRouter serve base Glimmer but LoRA support there is unverified.
- Rubric/submission details (30/25/25/20 weights, 3-minute video, public repo, team <= 4, 'new work only', anti-project list) come from a participant's copy of the brief, not an official page I could fetch; verify on the Cerebral Valley submission platform.
- Gated Hugging Face repos (facebook/sam3.1, dinov3, meta-llama) may take hours to approve; request immediately or rely on the Meta API for SAM.
- Pricing/limits can change (Fireworks free SFT ends Nov 30; Baseten per-minute rates were cross-checked only via aggregators and a dated pricing page); $150 Meta credits require a credit card, so set a spend limit before bulk data generation.
- Safety: a brushed-motor toy quad under autonomous control indoors can injure people; keep the classical controller, deadzones, a hard stop, and a human on the RC override during any flight footage.

OPEN QUESTIONS FOR USER:
- How many hours and people do you have before the Nov 4, 9:00 PM ET submission, and how much of that can go to training versus the digital twin and flight code?
- Do you have the hackathon acceptance, a Meta Model API key with the $150 credits claimed, and any Baseten/Modal/Fireworks credits granted? Is spending ~$10-40 on rented CUDA (Modal/Baseten) acceptable, or must all training run on the Mac?
- Must the trained model run live on your Mac during the demo (favoring mlx-lm QLoRA + local serving), or is a hosted endpoint (Fireworks on-demand, Baseten) acceptable for the video?
- Should the LoRA cover only voice-command parsing, or also the supervisory decisions (re-acquire/hover/land) from telemetry?
- Do you already have gated Hugging Face access for facebook/sam3.1, facebook/dinov3, or meta-llama models?
- Can you record 10-20 minutes of real X69 footage of people in distinctly colored shirts and a few cars (indoor/outdoor) today for evaluation and, if pursued, SAM fine-tuning data?
- Which ASR do you prefer for the live demo: Meta's realtime Muse Voice Transcribe (push-to-talk over WebSocket) or fully local Omnilingual ASR?
- Which track (indie vs startup) and theme (Build for Your World vs Push the Frontier) did you enter, since the robotics framing fits 'Push the Frontier'?



### Digital twin options on an Apple Silicon Mac  [verifier reliability: high]

SUMMARY:
Local facts verified by shell: macOS 26.6.2 arm64, ffmpeg 9.0.2 with hevc_videotoolbox/h264_videotoolbox encoders and hevc decoder, Chrome 157, Blender 5.2.0 LTS, Python 3.13.16/3.12.13/3.15.0, uv 0.13.1, Node 24.16, pip playwright 1.60 (npm latest 1.64.1). Not installed: Webots, MuJoCo, Unity, Godot.

Integration boundary (read from the turbodrone clone; same files at https://github.com/marshallrichards/turbodrone): RC goes to DRONE_IP:23458 (env DRONE_IP/CONTROL_PORT) at 25 Hz for x69_lg. Datagram = b"\xca\x47\xd5\x00" + u32le(20) + payload[20] = [0x66, 0x14, roll, pitch, throttle, yaw, flags1, flags2, 10 zero bytes, xor(bytes 2..17), 0x99]; axes 0-255 centre 128; roll/pitch pre-scaled 0.55/0.75/1.0 by speed_index; flags1 0x01 takeoff, 0x02 e-stop, 0x04 calibrate, 0x08 flip, 0x80 land (one-shot); flags2 0x08 tilt-up / 0x10 tilt-down held while camera_tilt_state is 2/1; keepalive b"\xca\x47\xd1\x00\x00\x00\x00\x00" every 1 s. Video default = RTSP rtsp://172.16.11.1/live/ch00_1 read by OpenCV/FFmpeg over TCP and re-emitted as JPEG VideoFrames; overridable via X69_LG_RTSP_URL. Alternate h265 mode: client sends OPEN_STREAM a8 8a 20 00 08 00 00 00 01 00 02 00 00 00 d2 04 (CLOSE a8 8a 21.., IFRAME a8 8a 24..) to :23459 and receives UDP :1234 packets with a 32-byte header (magic c6 6c a5 5a, u32le frame_len@4, frame_id@8, frame_type@17, u16le total_chunks@20/chunk_index@22, u32le payload_offset@24/payload_len@28) carrying Annex-B HEVC. Plugins (backend/plugins/base.py) iterate JPEG VideoFrames and call fc.set_axes_from(name, throttle, yaw, pitch, roll) in -1..1 under DirectStrategy; FollowPlugin runs at FOLLOW_FPS=20 with bang-bang yaw/pitch. Consequence: a twin that listens on UDP 23458/23459 and publishes 1280x720 HEVC RTSP at /live/ch00_1 is wire-identical with DRONE_IP=127.0.0.1 and zero turbodrone changes. mediamtx v1.21.3 ships mediamtx_v1.21.3_darwin_arm64.tar.gz (https://github.com/bluenviron/mediamtx/releases/latest), so bridge = sim frames -> ffmpeg -c:v hevc_videotoolbox -> RTSP publish. The cheaper seam is a Sim{Rc,Video}ProtocolAdapter pair modelled on DebugVideoProtocolAdapter (start/stop/is_running/get_frame); keep it for unit tests only.

Webots (primary). R2025a (2025-02-04) is the latest stable, one universal webots-R2025a.dmg "Apple Silicon & Intel"; nightly_3_11_2026 also publishes webots-R2025b.dmg; Apache-2.0 (https://github.com/cyberbotics/webots/releases). Issue #6976 (macOS 27 beta, M1 Max): R2025a aborted on a false TCP-port check plus Qt cocoa plugin path; fixed in PR #7032 and in nightlies since 2026-10-26; macOS 26 not discussed (https://github.com/cyberbotics/webots/issues/6976). Mavic2Pro.proto: body mass 0.4 kg, Camera default 400x240 in cameraSlot, gimbal motors "camera yaw/pitch/roll" (pitch -0.5..1.7 rad), Propeller thrustConstants +/-0.00026, GPS/InertialUnit/Gyro/Compass; the C sample controller gives a working loop (k_vertical_thrust 68.5, offset 0.6, k_vertical_p 3.0, k_roll_p 50, k_pitch_p 30, explicit mixing table) to re-tune into a brushed toy with injected drift (https://github.com/cyberbotics/webots/blob/master/projects/robots/dji/mavic/protos/Mavic2Pro.proto, https://github.com/cyberbotics/webots/blob/master/projects/robots/dji/mavic/controllers/mavic2pro/mavic2pro.c). People: Pedestrian.proto exposes shirtColor/pantsColor/shoesColor/skinColor (SFColor -> PBRAppearance baseColor); its Python Supervisor walks waypoints via --trajectory "[x1 y1, x2 y2, ...]" --speed (default 0.5 m/s) --step, animating 13 joints (https://github.com/cyberbotics/webots/blob/master/projects/humans/pedestrian/protos/Pedestrian.proto). Higher realism: CharacterSkin (Skin node, MakeHuman FBX for Anthony/Robert/Sandra/Sophia, per-character *_suit.jpg textures, BVH walk/run motions, C bvh_animation controller; Python Skin.setBoneOrientation exists) but the proto is "Licensed for use only with Webots" (https://github.com/cyberbotics/webots/blob/master/docs/guide/humans.md). Cars: TeslaModel3Simple has color SFColor (default 1 0 0) plus BMW/Citroen/Lincoln/Mercedes/Range Rover/Toyota protos; mavic_2_pro.wbt already contains a TeslaModel3Simple, Road, Pines, Forest, basicTimeStep 8 (https://github.com/cyberbotics/webots/blob/master/projects/vehicles/protos/tesla/TeslaModel3Simple.proto). Camera node: width/height, fieldOfView, noise (gaussian per RGB channel), motionBlur, exposure, lensFlare; each enabled camera renders via OpenGL and copies GPU->CPU every sampling period; getImage() is BGRA bytes (https://github.com/cyberbotics/webots/blob/master/docs/reference/camera.md). --no-rendering only blanks the 3D view: "Even if Webots is launched in no-rendering mode, the graphical scene will be computed. It is necessary for the camera" (https://github.com/cyberbotics/webots/blob/master/docs/guide/advanced-configuration.md). Python API supports >=3.8; extern controllers launch via $WEBOTS_HOME/Contents/MacOS/webots-controller (ipc default), so the firmware loop can live in a uv venv (https://github.com/cyberbotics/webots/blob/master/docs/guide/running-extern-robot-controllers.md). Unverified: sustained 1280x720 camera rate on M5 Pro (my estimate 20-30 Hz; measure day 1). Setup estimate 2-4 h.

Three.js/Rapier (fallback). three 0.187.0, @dimforge/rapier3d-compat 0.21.0, playwright 1.64.1. GPU WebGL in headless Chromium on Apple Silicon needs new headless (Playwright channel 'chromium' -> "ANGLE Metal"; the default headless shell -> SwiftShader CPU; --disable-gpu removes WebGL) (https://renderscreenshot.com/glossary/webgl-headless); on an M5 the fix was --use-angle=metal, renderer "ANGLE (Apple, ANGLE Metal Renderer: Apple M5)" (https://github.com/elvarofficial/teardown/issues/4). Frames: page.screencast.start({ onFrame: ({data}) => ..., size }) streams JPEG, fps option since 1.64 (https://playwright.dev/docs/release-notes); or WebSocket JPEG / WebCodecs HEVC (default-on since Chrome 130). Assets: Quaternius Universal Base Characters + Universal Animation Library (CC0, glTF), Kenney Car Kit (CC0), Mixamo (free, FBX only, no redistribution; convert in Blender 5.2); Ready Player Me shut down 2026-01-31. Setup 1-2 days; highest realism ceiling and total control of latency/noise; physics must be written (a Python 6-DoF model with a render-only browser is fine).

MuJoCo 3.17.0 (2026-10-22): arm64 wheels cp310-cp315; MUJOCO_GL defaults to cgl on macOS (glfw fallback); Renderer(model, height=240, width=320) needs <visual><global offwidth/offheight> >= target; mjpython only for launch_passive; Filament PBR renderer exposed as mujoco.rendering.filament/mjrf (https://mujoco.readthedocs.io/en/stable/python.html). Menagerie Skydio X2 (Apache-2.0) and Crazyflie 2 (MIT) carry only a third-person "track" camera; humanoids are robots, not people, so weak for "person in blue shirt". gym-pybullet-drones (MIT): tested on Apple Silicon/macOS 26 with CFLAGS="-Dfdopen=fdopen" pybullet build; camera default 64x48 at 24 FPS via p.getCameraImage TinyRenderer, fov 60 -> low realism. Genesis 1.4.5 (2026-10-22, Python 3.10-3.13) lists macOS/Apple Silicon GPU sim (gs.metal), viewer and headless rendering as supported; 2025 macOS drone-example breakage (#1125) and API churn make it risky. Unity ML-Agents: com.unity.ml-agents 4.1.0 (Jul 2026) needs Unity 6, PyPI mlagents 1.1.0 (Oct 2024, Python 3.10): heavy, RL-oriented, unnecessary. Godot 4.4+ has a native Metal driver (Apple Silicon only); --headless implies the headless display driver (no rendering), no Python API -> custom bridge. Isaac Sim 5.x/6.x: Ubuntu/Windows only. Colosseum: "THIS IS NOT CURRENTLY SUPPORTED WITH COLOSSEUM AND MAY NOT WORK" on macOS; Project AirSim has an unofficial UE 5.7/Metal macOS branch (0 stars, source build). PX4 SITL + Gazebo Harmonic: PX4 docs say it works on Apple Silicon (CI there), but gz-sim #2952 (server segfault on Apple Silicon, protobuf rebuild workaround) is open and PX4 dynamics do not resemble a brushed toy. Tello sims: tello-sim returns no frames; djitellopy-sim gives pygame frames; TelloEduLab is PyBullet; none match the X69 protocol. Blender: bpy 5.2.3 wheel requires Python 3.13 (available); use for FBX->glTF conversion and offline renders, not real-time.

Perception coupling: dev.meta.ai sam-3-1 takes "a short noun phrase naming one thing", images $2.50/1k, video $0.20/1k frames, login required (https://dev.meta.ai/models/sam-3-1); the official repo needs CUDA 12.6+, Python 3.12+, PyTorch 2.7+ (https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam3), so SAM runs in the cloud; plan a 1-5 Hz SAM cadence plus a 20 Hz local tracker, which the twin's latency knob (200-500 ms) must exercise.

KEY CLAIMS:
- [high] Webots R2025a (2025-02-04) is the latest stable release and ships one universal macOS installer webots-R2025a.dmg labelled 'Apple Silicon & Intel'; nightly builds (e.g. nightly_3_11_2026) also publish webots-R2025b.dmg; repo license Apache-2.0. Issue #6976 (macOS 27 beta on M1 Max) showed R2025a failing to start on a false TCP-port check and Qt cocoa plugin path; fixed in PR #7032 and present in nightlies since 2026-10-26; macOS 26 behaviour not covered. (https://github.com/cyberbotics/webots/issues/6976)
- [high] Webots Pedestrian.proto exposes shirtColor, pantsColor, shoesColor, skinColor (SFColor, bound to PBRAppearance baseColor) and its Python Supervisor controller walks a looping waypoint path via --trajectory '[x1 y1, x2 y2, ...]', --speed (default 0.5 m/s) and --step while animating 13 hinge joints. (https://github.com/cyberbotics/webots/blob/master/projects/humans/pedestrian/protos/Pedestrian.proto)
- [high] Webots TeslaModel3Simple has a 'color' SFColor field (default 1 0 0) and recognitionColors, with sibling car protos under projects/vehicles/protos (bmw, citroen, lincoln, mercedes_benz, range_rover, toyota, generic); the stock mavic_2_pro.wbt world already contains a TeslaModel3Simple, Road, Pine/Forest, Windmills and uses basicTimeStep 8. (https://github.com/cyberbotics/webots/blob/master/projects/vehicles/protos/tesla/TeslaModel3Simple.proto)
- [high] Mavic2Pro.proto: body mass 0.4 kg, cameraSlot default Camera 400x240, gimbal RotationalMotors 'camera yaw' (-1.7..1.7), 'camera pitch' (-0.5..1.7), 'camera roll' (-0.5..0.5), four Propeller nodes with thrustConstants +/-0.00026, GPS/InertialUnit/Gyro/Compass; the C sample controller uses k_vertical_thrust 68.5, k_vertical_offset 0.6, k_vertical_p 3.0, k_roll_p 50, k_pitch_p 30 and an explicit four-motor mixing table. (https://github.com/cyberbotics/webots/blob/master/projects/robots/dji/mavic/controllers/mavic2pro/mavic2pro.c)

'--no-rendering' only disables the main 3D view: 'Even if Webots is launched in no-rendering mode, the graphical scene will be computed. It is necessary for the camera to be able to see the scene'. (https://github.com/cyberbotics/webots/blob/master/docs/guide/advanced-configuration.md)

VERIFIER CORRECTIONS (non-confirmed claims):
- [partially_correct] 1. Webots R2025a (2025-02-04) latest stable, one universal webots-R2025a.dmg 'Apple Silicon & Intel'; nightly_3_11_2026 also publishes webots-R2025b.dmg; Apache-2.0; issue #6976 (macOS 27 beta, M1 Max) R2025a failed on false TCP-port check + Qt cocoa plugin path; fixed in PR #7032, in nightlies since 2026-10-26; macOS 26 not covered. -> Release facts confirmed via GitHub API (R2025a published 2025-02-04T12:36Z, license Apache-2.0, nightly_3_11_2026 assets include webots-R2025a.dmg AND webots-R2025b.dmg). Corrections: (a) the issue reports BOTH R2025a and R2025b failing (bundled Qt 6.5.3); (b) PR #7032 was merged to master on 2026-10-03 (base=master), is listed in changelog-r2025.md under R2025b, and has NOT been backported to the `released` branch, so the fix is in the nightly R2025b dmg, not R2025a; the 2026-10-26 comment merely confirmed 'latest nightly is patched'; (c) macOS 26 IS mentioned once: a commenter attributed the mechanism to a macOS '26.7+/27' socket behaviour change, so R2025a stable may be unaffected on this 26.6.2 machine (test first); (d) nightly macOS dmgs are intermittent: nightly_30_10_2026 had no dmg at all, nightly_2_11_2026 only R2025a.dmg. Also system-requirements.md officially lists only macOS 12/13/14. (https://github.com/cyberbotics/webots/issues/6976)

Isaac Sim 5.x/6.x requirements list only Ubuntu 22.04/24.04 and Windows 10/11 (container Linux-only, aarch64 only on DGX Spark); Colosseum macOS page says 'THIS IS NOT CURRENTLY SUPPORTED WITH COLOSSEUM AND MAY NOT WORK' (only Catalina tested); the only Apple Silicon Unreal path is an unofficial Project AirSim fork claiming UE 5.7/Metal with 0 stars. -> Isaac Sim page: x86_64 Ubuntu 22.04 (deprecated) / 24.04 and Windows 11 only; it states 'Windows 10 is not supported'; container Linux-only; aarch64 only DGX OS 7 on DGX Spark; GPUs without RT cores unsupported; no macOS. Colosseum quote and 'Only macOS Catalina (10.15) has currently been tested' confirmed (UE 5.3 recommended). (https://docs.isaacsim.omniverse.nvidia.com/latest/installation/requirements.html)
- [partially_correct] 14. dev.meta.ai sam-3-1 accepts 'a short noun phrase naming one thing', returns boxes/masks with identity across video frames; $2.50/1k images, $0.20/1k video frames; login required; facebookresearch/sam3 needs Python 3.12+, PyTorch 2.7+, CUDA 12.6+ (no MPS/CPU path documented); SAM 3.1 (Object Multiplex) released 2026-03-27 with gated HF weights. -> Pricing, prompt wording, login, repo requirements (Python 3.12+, PyTorch 2.7+, CUDA 12.6+, example pins torch==2.10.0 cu128), SAM 3.1 Object Multiplex 03/27/2026 and gated facebook/sam3.1 (license 'other') all confirmed. Corrections/additions: the API model id is 'sam-3.1' (the URL slug is sam-3-1); endpoint is POST https://api.meta.ai/v1/responses (OpenAI Responses-API compatible, base_url https://api.meta.ai/v1, Bearer MODEL_API_KEY), output is SSE special-token text (`<0f>0<|box;...|><|mask;...|>`) needing meta-sam-parser>=0.0.6 (Python); one noun phrase per request; video = whole clip per request (MP4/MOV, H.264/H.265/MPEG-4, up to 15,000 frames ~10 min, max 16 tracked objects/frame, minimum 50 frames billed), 3,000 RPM. 'No MPS/CPU path' is misleading: SAM 3 (facebook/sam3, gated) is integrated in HF transformers as Sam3Model/Sam3Processor and Sam3VideoModel/Sam3VideoProcessor with a device-agnostic API (device_map='auto', init_video_session inference_device default 'cpu') and a documented streaming per-frame mode; the facebook/sam3.1 card explicitly says there is no Transformers integration for 3.1. (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/sam/segmenting)

VERIFIER MISSED FINDINGS:
- dev.meta.ai SAM is a request/response API, not a live tracker: video requests take a whole clip (URL, base64 or Files API upload; MP4/MOV; <=15,000 frames; max 16 tracked objects per frame; billed min 50 frames) and stream per-frame results back; object identity persists only inside one request. For the live loop the practical pattern is per-image calls with model id 'sam-3.1' ($2.50/1k, 3,000 RPM) at 1-5 Hz plus a local tracker; parse responses with meta-sam-parser>=0.0.6. Same pricing page lists the other Meta Model API ids the pipeline needs: ASR muse-voice-transcribe-1.0 ($0.18/audio-hour, 256 concurrent streams) and LLMs muse-spark-1.3 ($1.25 in/$4.25 out per 1M tokens, 3,000 RPM) / muse-wonder-1.0 ($4/$20). (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/sam/segmenting, https://dev.meta.ai/docs/pricing-rate-limits)
- A local SAM 3 fallback exists on Apple Silicon: HF transformers ships Sam3Model/Sam3Processor and Sam3VideoModel/Sam3VideoProcessor (checkpoint facebook/sam3, gated) with device-agnostic APIs (device_map='auto'; init_video_session inference_device default 'cpu') and a documented streaming mode (model(inference_session=..., frame=pixel_values[0])) for text-prompted multi-object tracking; native res 1008 px, config.image_size=560 for speed. SAM 3.1 weights (facebook/sam3.1) have no transformers integration. MPS throughput is unmeasured. (https://huggingface.co/docs/transformers/main/en/model_doc/sam3_video)
- Webots macOS risk profile is narrower than stated: PR #7032 is only on master (R2025b nightly), not backported to `released`; the bug mechanism was attributed to macOS 26.7+/27 socket behaviour so R2025a stable may run fine on 26.6.2 (issue also says launching with a different --port range worked as a workaround); official system-requirements.md lists only macOS 12-14; nightly macOS DMGs are intermittently missing (none on 30 Oct, R2025a only on 2 Nov, both on 3 Nov). Plan: try R2025a.dmg first, keep the nightly R2025b.dmg URL as fallback, and budget a day-1 smoke test. (https://github.com/cyberbotics/webots/pull/7032, https://raw.githubusercontent.com/cyberbotics/webots/master/docs/guide/system-requirements.md)
- On this machine bare `python3` is Apple's /usr/bin/python3 3.9.6 (Homebrew 3.12/3.13/3.15 are only reachable as python3.1x). Webots' Preferences 'Python command' and any extern-controller shebang must point at /opt/homebrew/bin/python3.13 or the uv venv interpreter, otherwise controllers run on 3.9 without numpy/cv2. (local shell: `python3 --version` -> 3.9.6)
- MuJoCo is a verified headless-render option here, not just on paper: in a scratch uv venv (Python 3.13) mujoco 3.17.0 arm64 wheel loads mujoco.cgl automatically, imports `mjrf` and `mujoco.rendering.filament` (the wheel contains _render_filament.cpython-313-darwin.so), and the classic Renderer produces 1280x720 offscreen RGB at ~6.7 ms/frame on a trivial scene with offwidth/offheight set to 1280/720. Also note the Webots Pedestrian and Mavic2Pro protos are 'Licensed for use only with Webots', so their meshes cannot be ported into a three.js/MuJoCo twin. (local test; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/cyberbotics/webots/master/projects/humans/pedestrian/protos/Pedestrian.proto)
- Twin wire contract details from the turbodrone source the summary glossed over: in RTSP mode nothing is ever sent to 23459 (send_start_command/start_keepalive are no-ops), so an RTSP-mode twin needs only an RTSP server on 554 at /live/ch00_1 (OpenCV forces rtsp_transport=tcp, nobuffer, low_delay, CAP_PROP_BUFFERSIZE=1, re-encodes to JPEG q85) plus a UDP 23458 listener that must tolerate the separate 8-byte D1 keepalive (ca 47 d1 00 00 00 00 00) interleaved with 28-byte control datagrams; h265 mode would additionally need a UDP 23459 command listener (CLOSE a8 8a 21.. then OPEN a8 8a 20.. on start, OPEN re-sent as keepalive, IFRAME a8 8a 24..) emitting 32-byte-header chunks to UDP 1234, and turbodrone then transcodes to 640-wide 15 fps JPEG (q:v 12). FollowPlugin currently hard-depends on ultralytics YOLOv10n (person class only) and bang-bang yaw/pitch of +/-20%, so the SAM path replaces the plugin body, not the plugin contract. (local clone backend/protocols/x69_lg_rtsp_video_protocol.py, x69_lg_video_protocol.py, plugins/follow/follow_plugin.py)

VERIFIER NOTES: Method: 15 claims checked against primary sources (raw GitHub files, GitHub REST API via gh, PyPI JSON, npm registry, readthedocs, HF docs/model cards, dev.meta.ai docs/pricing pages, local shell on the target Mac, and the read-only turbodrone clone). 20+ distinct pages fetched. Result: 12 confirmed, 3 partially correct (claims 1, 12, 14), 0 refuted, 0 unverifiable. No hallucinated products or numbers found; errors are precision/omission errors.

Corrections that change planning:
- Claim 1 (Webots): #6976 affected R2025a AND R2025b; PR #7032 merged 2026-10-03 to master only (changelog lists it under R2025b; no backport to `released`), so the fix is in the nightly webots-R2025b.dmg, not the nightly R2025a dmg. The issue thread attributes the mechanism to a macOS 26.7+/27 socket change, so on this 26.6.2 machine R2025a stable may be unaffected; the issue also reports `--port <other range>` as a workaround. Nightly macOS DMGs are intermittently absent (30 Oct none; 2 Nov R2025a only; 3 Nov both). Official support list is macOS 12-14 only. Day-1 smoke test is mandatory, not optional.
- Claim 12: Isaac Sim lists Windows 11 only and says 'Windows 10 is not supported'; Ubuntu 22.04 is deprecated. AirSim fork is benreichman/ProjectAirSim-macOS (0 stars, pushed 2026-10-22, branch macos-unreal-support, UE 5.7/Metal); upstream PR #142 closed unmerged; issue #226 'Mac support' open. Still a non-option for a hackathon.
- Claim 14: API model id is 'sam-3.1' (URL slug sam-3-1). Endpoint POST https://api.meta.ai/v1/responses, OpenAI-SDK compatible, SSE special-token output parsed by meta-sam-parser>=0.0.6. Video is clip-per-request (MP4/MOV, <=15,000 frames, 16 objects/frame max, min 50 frames billed), identity only within a request, 3,000 RPM. So the 'SAM at 1-5 Hz + 20 Hz local tracker' architecture is right but must be built on per-image calls, not on the video endpoint. The 'no CPU/MPS path' statement is incomplete: SAM 3 (facebook/sam3, gated) is in HF transformers (Sam3Model, Sam3VideoModel with a documented streaming per-frame mode, device-agnostic), giving a local fallback; SAM 3.1 has no transformers integration per its model card.

Confirmed with added specifics:
- turbodrone (local clone): every byte constant, offset, flag, env var and the 25 Hz default (main.py default_rate=25.0; web_server.py default_control_rate=25.0) match. RTSP mode sends nothing to 23459 (no-op send_start_command), forces TCP transport + nobuffer/low_delay, re-encodes JPEG q85; '1280x720 HEVC' is README text, not enforced by code. h265 mode: CLOSE then OPEN on start, OPEN as keepalive, 32-byte chunk header offsets exactly as claimed, ffmpeg -f hevc -> mjpeg 640w/15fps/q12. set_axes_from clamps to [-1,1]; FollowPlugin FOLLOW_FPS=20, YOLOv10n person-only, bang-bang +/-20%.
- Webots protos/docs: Pedestrian fields/defaults, 13 joints (only instantiated when controllerArgs non-empty), --speed default 0.5, looping path; TeslaModel3Simple color 1 0 0 + recognitionColors (CC-BY-4.0); vehicles dirs abstract/bmw/citroen/generic/lincoln/mercedes_benz/range_rover/tesla/textures/toyota; mavic_2_pro.wbt basicTimeStep 8 with Road, 4 Windmill, 7 Pine, Forest, TeslaModel3Simple, Camera 400x240; Mavic2Pro mass 0.4, gimbal limits, thrustConstants +/-0.00026, torqueConstants 0.0000052; mavic2pro.c gains 68.5/0.6/3.0/50/30 and mixing table; camera.md noise/BGRA/GPU->CPU copy text; advanced-configuration.md --no-rendering quote; Python >=3.8; extern launcher ipc default, --port 1234. Pedestrian and Mavic2Pro protos are 'Licensed for use only with Webots'.
- MuJoCo: PyPI 3.17.0 (2026-10-22, >=3.10, cp310-cp315 + cp314t/cp315t macosx_11_0_arm64). Verified empirically in a scratch venv on this M5 Pro: GLContext -> mujoco.cgl, mjrf and mujoco.rendering.filament import (wheel includes _render_filament .so), classic Renderer 1280x720 offscreen at ~6.7 ms/frame (trivial scene), ValueError text as documented. Menagerie X2/Crazyflie licenses, single 'track' camera, ctrlrange 0-13 via default class, hover keyframe confirmed.
- gym-pybullet-drones README 'Tested on ... Apple Silicon/macOS 26', CFLAGS build, 64x48@24 fps, fov 60, 240 Hz confirmed; renderer arg not passed in _getDroneImages (TinyRenderer by default in DIRECT). Genesis 1.4.5 py3-none-any, <3.14, macOS all-supported table, #1125 history confirmed.
- Headless WebGL: teardown #4 (2026-11-02, M5, macOS 27.2, Playwright 1.58.2) fix --use-angle=metal confirmed; renderscreenshot measurements (M1 Pro) confirmed; sources disagree on whether Metal is automatic in new headless, so pass --use-angle=metal and assert the renderer string. Playwright screencast 1.59, fps 1.64; npm playwright 1.64.1 / three 0.187.0 / rapier3d-compat 0.21.0; local pip playwright is 1.60.0.
- Local: all versions confirmed; mediamtx v1.21.3 darwin_arm64 asset exists; bpy 5.2.3 cp313 arm64 wheel exists; Blender 5.2 is on blender.org's LTS list. Bare `python3` is Apple's 3.9.6 -> set Webots Python path explicitly.

Unverified/inferred: that the nightly webots-R2025a.dmg is built from the `released` branch (inferred from PR base/backport list); MPS throughput of transformers SAM 3; Webots camera FPS at 1280x720 on M5 Pro (researcher's 20-30 Hz estimate remains a day-1 measurement).

RECOMMENDATIONS:
- Primary: build the twin in Webots (install R2025a dmg; if it fails on macOS 26.6 use the webots-R2025b.dmg nightly). Start from mavic_2_pro.wbt, replace the Mavic physics with an 'X69' proto (mass ~0.1-0.2 kg, Camera 1280x720, fieldOfView ~1.2 rad, noise 0.02-0.05, gimbal 'camera pitch' motor reused for the servo tilt), keep the world's TeslaModel3Simple/Road/Forest, and add Pedestrian nodes with per-instance shirtColor and --trajectory/--speed args plus color-varied *Simple cars moved by a Supervisor.
- Run the 'firmware' as an extern Python Supervisor controller launched through $WEBOTS_HOME/Contents/MacOS/webots-controller from a uv venv on Python 3.13 (works for Webots >=3.8, MuJoCo wheels and bpy 5.2.3). It owns: UDP 23458 RC parser (exact turbodrone packet format, keepalive, one-shot flags, held tilt flags, speed-index scaling), the Mavic-derived altitude/attitude loop re-tuned with a 100-200 ms stick->motion lag, optical-flow-style position hold with random-walk drift, brushed-motor throttle sag, and camera frame export at 20-30 Hz.
- Make the twin wire-identical rather than patching turbodrone: pipe frames to ffmpeg (-c:v hevc_videotoolbox, 1280x720, ~2-4 Mbps, GOP ~30) publishing to mediamtx darwin_arm64 at rtsp://127.0.0.1:8554/live/ch00_1; run turbodrone with DRONE_TYPE=x69_lg DRONE_IP=127.0.0.1 X69_LG_RTSP_URL=rtsp://127.0.0.1:8554/live/ch00_1. Put the latency injector (200-500 ms ring buffer), frame drops and JPEG/HEVC artefacts in that bridge so perception/control see the same queueing as the real drone. Optionally emulate the UDP h265 path (23459 OPEN_STREAM handshake, 32-byte c6 6c a5 5a chunk header) later.
- Day-1 gates before committing: (a) Webots launches and renders on macOS 26.6; (b) measure 1280x720 Camera throughput with camera.enable(33) in --mode=realtime --minimize --batch; (c) upload ~20 rendered frames (Pedestrian flat-shaded vs CharacterSkin textured, Tesla color variants) to the dev.meta.ai segmentation playground with prompts 'person in blue shirt', 'green car', 'person' and pick the character type that SAM 3.1 detects reliably. If (c) fails for both, switch to the Three.js fallback with Mixamo/Quaternius textured humans.
- Fallback: a Three.js (0.187) render-only client driven by the same Python firmware over WebSocket (Python keeps physics so both paths share one dynamics model), launched by Playwright 1.64 with channel 'chromium' and --use-angle=metal, frames via page.screencast onFrame JPEG (fps option) into the identical ffmpeg/mediamtx bridge; assets: Quaternius Universal Base Characters + Universal Animation Library (CC0 glTF) or Mixamo FBX converted in Blender 5.2, Kenney Car Kit (CC0), Poly Haven HDRI for lighting; tint shirts/cars by material color at spawn.
- Add a second, in-process seam for CI: Sim{Rc,Video}ProtocolAdapter classes modelled on DebugVideoProtocolAdapter (start/stop/is_running/get_frame returning JPEG VideoFrames) selected by DRONE_TYPE=x69_sim, so perception/control unit tests run without RTSP or Webots; do not use it for the demo acceptance run.
- Define a scenario JSON (people: shirt/pants colors, waypoints, speed; cars: color, path, speed; light/time-of-day; wind/drift level; latency) that the Supervisor instantiates with importMFNodeFromString, and ship 5-6 canned scenarios (single person, two people different shirts, person + green and red car, occlusion crossing, target leaves frame, low light).
- Design the perception loop around cloud SAM 3.1 (CUDA-only locally): call sam-3-1 at 1-5 Hz on downscaled frames, run a local tracker (OpenCV CSRT or template/optical-flow) at 20 Hz, re-seed from SAM, and gate RC output on target-lost; the twin's latency knob must be exercised at 200, 350 and 500 ms before flying.
- Capture a real-drone calibration session as soon as possible with turbodrone's dump_frames / X69_LG_DUMP_H265 options and a pcap of RC packets: measure glass-to-glass latency, stick-step response, hover drift and camera FOV/tilt range, then back-fit the twin's parameters; also reuse the recordings as an offline replay harness for perception.
- Skip Isaac Sim (no macOS), Colosseum/AirSim (unsupported, UE source build), Unity ML-Agents (Unity 6 editor plus Python 3.10 mlagents 1.1.0, RL-oriented), PX4+Gazebo (open Apple Silicon segfault, non-toy dynamics), Genesis (fast-moving API, weak human assets) and gym-pybullet-drones (64x48 CPU camera) for this hackathon timeline.

RISKS:
- Webots R2025a on macOS 26.6 is unverified: the only recent macOS report (#6976) is a macOS 27 beta start-up failure fixed in nightlies; budget time to fall back to the R2025b nightly or to the Three.js path.
- Webots 1280x720 camera throughput on Apple Silicon is unmeasured; each enabled camera does an OpenGL render plus GPU->CPU copy per step, so 30 Hz at 720p may not hold and you may need 640x360 with upscaling in ffmpeg (which also better mimics the toy camera).
- SAM 3.1 detection of flat-shaded Webots Pedestrian mannequins with 'person in blue shirt' is unverified; textured CharacterSkin humans are limited to four fixed-outfit models whose proto is 'Licensed for use only with Webots', and recoloring shirts means editing *_suit.jpg textures.
- Cloud-only SAM 3.1 (CUDA required locally; per-image/per-frame pricing; login/API access) adds 200-1000 ms perception latency on top of 200-500 ms video latency; the control loop must tolerate roughly 1 s end-to-end or the real drone will overshoot.
- No public dynamics data for the X69 (mass, thrust, drift, latency); the twin's response and drift are guesses until calibrated against a recorded flight; the camera tilt flag semantics (step vs continuous while held) are unknown.
- Headless-browser fallback depends on Chromium GPU flags (--use-angle=metal, new headless, no --disable-gpu); misconfiguration silently drops to SwiftShader and the frame rate collapses.
- Asset licensing: Mixamo is FBX-only and forbids redistribution of raw files; Ready Player Me is gone (shut down 2026-01-31); verify Quaternius pack licenses (older packs CC0, newer under QAL).
- Python 3.15 is too new for most wheels (Webots, OpenCV, torch); standardize on 3.13 (mujoco cp313 and bpy 5.2.3 both available) to avoid build-from-source detours.
- Wire-level emulation of the UDP h265 path is reverse-engineered from turbodrone only (header fields at fixed offsets, unknown semantics of bytes 12-16 and frame_type); if the real firmware behaves differently the RTSP path remains the safe default.
- A single Webots instance supports one extern controller per robot and ipc only for same-user local processes; running perception in a separate process is fine, but do not put SAM calls inside the Webots step loop or the simulation stalls.

OPEN QUESTIONS FOR USER:
- Do you have the X69 on hand now and can you fly a 2-3 minute calibration session (record RTSP frames, H.265 dump and an RC pcap) before the twin is tuned? Without it the drift/latency model is a guess.
- On the real X69, does the camera tilt move in discrete steps per press or continuously while the tilt button is held (turbodrone sends the tilt flag continuously while the state is set)?
- Is cloud-hosted SAM 3.1 via dev.meta.ai (login, $2.50/1k images, $0.20/1k video frames) acceptable for both the twin and the live demo, or must perception run offline on the Mac?
- Are you willing to install a desktop simulator (Webots, ~1 GB app) or do you prefer an all-pip/npm stack (Three.js fallback) that can be committed and reproduced by judges?
- What does the real demo scene look like (indoor room with people and toy cars at tabletop scale, or outdoors with real cars)? This decides car scale, lighting presets and whether 'green car' means a toy.
- Should the twin's acceptance criterion be wire-level identity (turbodrone unchanged, RTSP + UDP) or is an in-process adapter acceptable for the hackathon submission?
- Which Python version do you want to standardize on (3.13 recommended; 3.15 lacks wheels), and is adding Node/Playwright acceptable if we need the browser fallback?
- Does the hackathon require the simulator itself to use Meta models (e.g. Meta-generated scenes/voices) or only the perception/LLM stack?



### Loiley X69 hardware, app, and flight characteristics  [verifier reliability: medium]

SUMMARY:
IDENTITY. Loiley X69 = Amazon ASIN B0F8QTBGNP ("Drone with Camera for Adults, 2K HD FPV ... 2 Batteries"), brand Loiley, 4.2 stars / 1,886 ratings, #1 Best Seller in UAVs, ~$34 (https://www.amazon.com/dp/B0F8QTBGNP, partial fetch). Manufacturer is Shantou Xintuo Intelligent Technology; FCC ID 2BF7B-X30-1 (granted 2025-03-24, 2440-2480 MHz, Part 15.249) lists "Quadcopters Model No.: X69" and a declaration covering X28/X29/X30/X31/X33/X50/X60/X61/X62/X65/X66/X69/X81/X82/X83/X85, so the X69 is one shell in a large white-label family (https://fccid.io/2BF7B-X30-1; page 403s to the fetcher, values from search snippet). Stock app is "X69 DRONE": Android com.lg.drone (APKPure: v1.0.13 2025-08-22, v1.0.14 2026-07-03, v1.0.15 2026-08-09, Android 5+, https://apkpure.com/x69-drone/com.lg.drone; Play rating 3.2/37 reviews, 10K+ installs, https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=com.lg.drone) and iOS id6738834352 (v1.0.1 2025-02-18, 3.2/31; advertised features only: joystick, gravity mode, trajectory flight, headless, 360-degree flip; "sends control data over UDP"; reviews: "many restarts of the application" to connect, https://apps.apple.com/us/app/x69-drone/id6738834352). Follow-me/gesture are NOT advertised for the X69; the decompiled app's ProtocolAnalysisUtil has native follow/circle/point and sendOpticalFlowDataCMD entry points but they are on inactive/alternate code paths (turbodrone docs/research/x69_lg.md).

PROTOCOL (verified in the local turbodrone clone, commits a334069/ada0e0e/b1ff297 of 2026-05-20..23). Drone is a Wi-Fi AP at 172.16.11.1. RC: 20-byte payload 66 14 RR PP TT YY F1 F2 00x10 XOR 99 (sticks 0..255, center 128) wrapped as ca 47 d5 00 + LE32 len -> UDP 23458; 1 Hz keepalive ca 47 d1 00 00 00 00 00. Byte 6: 0x01 takeoff, 0x02 emergency stop (motor cut), 0x04 gyro calibrate, 0x08 flip, 0x80 land; byte 7: 0x08 tilt up / 0x10 tilt down, momentary servo bits (held = moving). Three speed tiers scale roll/pitch only (0.55/0.75/1.0; stock default = middle). turbodrone sends at CONTROL_RATE 25 Hz for x69_lg (backend/web_server.py default_control_rate = 25.0; other drones 80 Hz). The research note warns the takeoff/land/flip flag table came from obfuscated JADX output and should be confirmed by packet capture (README nevertheless marks x69_lg "Tested"). No downlink telemetry (battery, altitude) is parsed. Video: default RTSP rtsp://172.16.11.1/live/ch00_1, HEVC 1280x720, OpenCV with rtsp_transport=tcp, nobuffer/low_delay, CAP_PROP_BUFFERSIZE=1, 5 s frame timeout then reconnect (2 s delay, 10 tries). Alternative native path: open-stream cmd a8 8a 20 ... d2 04 to UDP 23459, 32-byte c6 6c a5 5a headers on UDP 1234, I-frame request re-sent every 1 s, FFmpeg transcode to 640px/15fps JPEG; it required +genpts normal buffering (nobuffer produced only one frame). Legacy JPEG 7070/7080 "often unavailable on current firmware" (https://github.com/marshallrichards/turbodrone README). Existing follow plugin: YOLOv10n at FOLLOW_FPS=20, bang-bang yaw/pitch (deadzone 0.15 / 0.02, target box width 0.30-0.80, +/-20 of 100 stick) via DirectStrategy. No GitHub issue or PR mentions the X69 (issues #1-#34 reviewed via gh).

HARDWARE. Camera: "2K" marketing; device.report lists 1080p capture, CMOS, and an Amazon spec row shows "Effective Still Resolution 2.07 MP" (=1920x1080 sensor) while the live stream is 1280x720 HEVC -> 2K is upscaled (https://device.report/loiley/x69 snippet; https://www.amazon.com/dp/B0F8QTBGNP snippet). Lens FOV: not published anywhere found; calibrate it. Tilt: 90-degree remote-adjustable servo (https://deals.christianpost.com/deal/loiley-x69-fpv-drones-with-2k-hd-camera-and-altitude-hold.html; https://edwardsphotographypro.com/best-drones-under-50/). Digital stabilization only, no gimbal. Positioning: downward optical flow + barometric altitude hold ("optical flow positioning", protocol's "optical-flow packet"); no evidence of a ToF rangefinder, no GPS, no obstacle avoidance. Battery: 3.7 V 1000 mAh 1S LiPo, two included, USB-C charging (https://loileydrone.com/); marketing "26 min total"; owners report "Short 10-min battery life", "roughly 20 minutes total" (https://www.aerovisiondrones.com/best-drones-for-adults/), so plan 8-10 min usable, 6-7 min missions. Weight: listings say "Under 250g" but one reviewer calls it "a 1.1-pound plastic drone" and another "just over 250g with battery ... closer to 249g" (https://markus-hagner-photography.com/best-camera-drones-under-100/); device.report "1.21 pounds" is likely shipping weight. Weigh it. Radio: 2.4 GHz Wi-Fi only (no 5 GHz evidence); physical 2.4 GHz remote (2x AAA) included (FCC 2BF7B-P "remote control"). Range/latency anecdotes: FPV lag "about half a second" (https://mydroneauthority.com/best-micro-drones/), "stutters occasionally", "cuts out around 20 yards" of Wi-Fi video range, "about one in five flights required me to restart the app", fixes include disabling phone auto-join to home Wi-Fi. Hover: "drifts slightly near walls", "struggles ... uneven surfaces or poor lighting", wind: "a strong breeze will push it around", "calm-day-only". Features: 3 speed modes, headless, one-key takeoff/landing, emergency stop, gravity mode, 360 flips, trajectory drawing. Firmware variants: the app ships MainActivityUDP (active), RTSP, Trinity and legacy paths, so transport can differ by firmware; your unit should be probed with turbodrone before building on it. Reddit/RCGroups: no X69 threads found; one YouTube review exists (https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=dwDZk0kCNi8, description not retrievable). Max speed/tilt, charge time, failsafe-on-link-loss and low-battery behaviour are unpublished and must be measured.

CONTROL CONSTRAINTS (inferred). Treat the X69's own optical-flow/baro hold as the inner loop; the outer loop commands small stick offsets. Keep the 25 Hz RC stream continuous regardless of perception rate; run perception at 5-10 Hz. Total loop latency ~0.8-1.5 s (0.3-0.7 s video + decode + SAM 3.1 cloud round trip) forces low gains (bandwidth <0.3 Hz), deadbands, rate limits, and velocity extrapolation of the target. Use speed tier 0 (0.55) and cap pitch/roll at ~+/-20-25% of half-range, yaw ~+/-30%, throttle within +/-10% of center. Expect 0.2-0.5 m drift per 10 s indoors over textured floor; far worse over plain floors, low light, near walls. Watchdog: no fresh detection >0.5 s -> neutral sticks; >2 s -> land (0x80); reserve 0x02 (motor cut) for true emergencies. Indoor test: >=5x5 m, >=2.5 m ceiling, patterned mat, bright even light, prop guards, netting, physical remote in hand after verifying it can override the Wi-Fi stream. Mac must join the drone AP (loses internet) so a second NIC (USB-Ethernet/phone tether) is needed to reach Baseten/Fireworks. Outdoor "green car" is impractical: ~20-50 m Wi-Fi range, brushed-motor top speed far below a car, wind, and FAA recreational rules (TRUST required regardless of weight; sub-250 g needs no registration/Remote ID, but >=250 g would require both and the X69 has no Remote ID; no flight over moving vehicles/people, VLOS, 400 ft) (https://gigafact.org/fact-briefs/fact-brief-do-recreational-drones-need-to-be-faa-registered-if-they-exceed-a-certain-weight/; https://www.thedronegirl.com/2024/10/11/do-recreational-drone-pilots-need-to-register-and-pass-trust-even-if-their-drone-is-under-250-grams/).

KEY CLAIMS:
- [high] turbodrone x69_lg RC link: 20-byte 66 14 ... 99 payload (sticks 0-255, center 128) wrapped in ca 47 d5 00 + LE32 length, sent by UDP to 172.16.11.1:23458, with a 1 Hz ca 47 d1 keepalive; byte 6 flags 0x01 takeoff, 0x02 emergency stop, 0x04 calibrate, 0x08 flip, 0x80 land; byte 7 0x08/0x10 momentary camera tilt up/down. (https://github.com/marshallrichards/turbodrone/blob/main/backend/protocols/x69_lg_rc_protocol_adapter.py)
- [high] turbodrone sends X69 RC packets at a default CONTROL_RATE of 25 Hz for x69_lg (other drone types use 80 Hz); speed tiers scale roll/pitch only by 0.55/0.75/1.0 with the stock app defaulting to the middle tier. (https://github.com/marshallrichards/turbodrone/blob/main/backend/web_server.py)
- [high] Default X69 video is RTSP rtsp://172.16.11.1/live/ch00_1, HEVC 1280x720, read with OpenCV (TCP transport, nobuffer/low_delay, buffer size 1, reconnect after 5 s without frames); the alternate native path is H.265 over UDP 1234 with 32-byte c6 6c a5 5a headers and stream commands on UDP 23459, and it needed normal (+genpts) buffering because low-latency flags broke decoding; legacy JPEG 7070/7080 is often unavailable on current firmware. (https://github.com/marshallrichards/turbodrone/blob/main/docs/research/x69_lg.md)
- [high] The takeoff/land/flip flag table was recovered from heavily obfuscated JADX output and the research note says packet captures should confirm it before trusting a complete flag table; no downlink telemetry (battery, altitude, attitude) is parsed anywhere in the x69_lg implementation. (https://github.com/marshallrichards/turbodrone/blob/main/docs/research/x69_lg.md)
- [high] The stock app is 'X69 DRONE' (Android package com.lg.drone, v1.0.13 Aug 2025, v1.0.14 Jul 2026, v1.0.15 Aug 2026; iOS id6738834352 v1.0.1 Feb 2025). Advertised iOS features are joystick control, gravity mode, trajectory flight, headless mode and 360-degree flip; follow-me and gesture modes are not advertised. VERIFIER MISSED FINDINGS:
- Safety gap in turbodrone's control loop: backend/services/flight_controller.py `_control_loop` re-sends the last throttle/yaw/pitch/roll directions at the control rate indefinitely with no input-staleness check, so if the perception plugin thread dies or stalls the last yaw/pitch command keeps being transmitted; additionally `_clear_one_shot_flags` means takeoff/land/flip/calibrate are emitted in exactly one 25 Hz UDP datagram, which a lossy 2.4 GHz link can drop. A planner-side watchdog (neutral sticks after N ms without a fresh command) and repeated/acknowledged one-shot flags need to be added rather than assumed. (Verified in local clone, files backend/services/flight_controller.py lines 94-122 and backend/protocols/x69_lg_rc_protocol_adapter.py lines 79, 150-155.)
- Frame delivery to plugins is not low-latency by default: plugins only receive frames when PLUGINS_ENABLED=true; PLUGIN_FRAME_Q is a DroppingQueue(maxsize=100) that drops the OLDEST frame, so a slow consumer (cloud SAM 3.1 at 5-10 Hz) sees up to ~100 frames (~4 s at 25 fps) of backlog unless it drains to the newest frame; in RTSP mode each frame is a full 1280x720 JPEG re-encoded at q=85 by OpenCV, in h265 mode a 640px/15 fps JPEG at ffmpeg q:v=12 (low quality). For a SAM-based tracker it is simpler to open rtsp://172.16.11.1/live/ch00_1 directly (OpenCV/PyAV) in the perception process and only reuse turbodrone for RC. (Verified: backend/web_server.py 472-500, backend/utils/dropping_queue.py, backend/protocols/x69_lg_rtsp_video_protocol.py 198-200.)
- The App Store lists X69 DRONE as installable on Apple Silicon Macs ('Mac: macOS 11.0 or later with an Apple M1 chip or later', flagged 'Not verified for macOS'), so the M5 Pro MacBook may be able to run the stock iOS app natively and capture its UDP traffic with Wireshark on the same machine to confirm the takeoff/land/flip flag table and the 1 Hz keepalive -- no phone or Android emulator needed. (https://apps.apple.com/us/app/x69-drone/id6738834352; availability must be checked in the Mac App Store since developers can opt out.)
- turbodrone's protocol research was done on decompiled app build 1.0.13 (2025-08-22); the vendor has since shipped 1.0.14 (2026-07-03) and 1.0.15 (2026-08-09) (https://apkpure.com/x69-drone/com.lg.drone), and Play metadata was updated 2026-08-06 (https://appgoblin.info/apps/com.lg.drone). Newer app builds may pair with newer firmware or change the default video path (RTSP vs UDP H.265 vs Trinity), so the unit on hand should be probed with all three X69_LG_VIDEO_MODE values and, ideally, 1.0.15 re-decompiled before building on the protocol.
- FAA nuance the researcher missed: the sub-250 g no-registration/no-Remote-ID exemption applies only when flying 'strictly for recreational purposes' under 49 USC 44809(a)(1) (https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/49/44809); 14 CFR 48.15(b) exempts only aircraft 'operated exclusively in compliance with 49 U.S.C. 44809' (https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/14/48.15), and 14 CFR 89.101 ties Remote ID to registration (https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/14/89.101). A hackathon/company demonstration flight is arguably not recreational, in which case Part 107 applies and registration plus Remote ID are required regardless of weight -- another reason to keep real flights indoors.
- Source quality: every hardware anecdote in the summary (0.5 s latency, 20-yard cutout, 1-in-5 restarts, 10-min packs, drift near walls, wind behaviour) comes from templated/AI-assisted affiliate pages (aerovisiondrones, mydroneauthority, markus-hagner, edwardsphotographypro, actual360production, rcratings) whose titles auto-update to 'November 2026' while bylines read June-Oct 2026, which contradict themselves, and two of which admit in their methodology that no instrumented testing was done; the 'uneven surfaces or poor lighting' quote is about a different Loiley/Velcase 1080P drone. The only first-party sources found are the App Store page, APKPure, loileydrone.com (battery 3.7 V 1000 mAh LiPo; company address 5235 SW Landing Creek Dr, Palm City FL), the FCC filing snippets (model family also includes X80), and the turbodrone code. Latency, range, FOV, flying weight, battery endurance and failsafe behaviour must all be measured on the bench.
