Scorecard: confirmed 1, 3, 4, 13, 14; partially correct 2, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 11, 12, 15; refuted 0; none fully unverifiable, though 6 and 8 rest on indexed snippets only. The code-level research is accurate and precise. The hardware/behaviour research has a systematic flaw: quotes are attributed to the wrong pages (claims 10, 11, 12), one quote is about a different product (claim 11), and all 'measurements' originate from templated affiliate pages. The one hard factual error is claim 2's 'other drones use 80 Hz' (cooingdv 20 Hz, wifi_cam 25 Hz).

Planner-relevant corrections and specifics: (a) RC datagram = 8-byte wrapper ca 47 d5 00 14 00 00 00 + 20-byte payload; XOR over bytes 2-17; D1 keepalive ca 47 d1 00 00 00 00 00 at 1 Hz on the same socket; local port ephemeral by default (X69_LG_LOCAL_CONTROL_PORT). (b) Speed tier scales only roll/pitch; yaw and throttle always full range. (c) RTSP adapter: reconnect after 5 s without frames, 2 s delay, 10 attempts, then loops again; H.265 UDP adapter re-sends OPEN_STREAM every 1 s and I-frame request only when decoder not ready or >2 s since last decoded JPEG. (d) The x69_lg UI capability map enables takeoff/land/estop/camera_tilt/speed_control, no camera_switch. (e) x69_lg model uses IncrementalStrategy by default; the follow plugin swaps in DirectStrategy and restores on stop; normalized axes are clamped to [-1,1] in set_axes_from. (f) No telemetry: link loss can only be inferred from video frame age. (g) iOS app says it receives 'flight data' from the drone, hinting at a downlink the app parses but turbodrone ignores; unconfirmed. (h) The research note names alternate app paths (MainActivityRTSP, TrinityActivity with trinityprotocolparse, ProtocolAnalysisUtil with sendOpticalFlowDataCMD and follow/circle/point natives) -- consistent with the researcher's statement that follow-me exists in code but is not advertised.

Hardware facts that survive scrutiny: battery 3.7 V 1000 mAh LiPo (vendor site); two batteries bundled (Amazon title, vendor site for the unnamed 2K model); 90-degree remotely adjustable camera tilt (three independent pages, matches tilt bits in protocol); digital-only stabilization; optical flow + altitude hold advertised; headless, one-key takeoff/landing, speed adjustment, gravity mode, trajectory flight, 360 flip (app store description + Amazon title); 2.4 GHz band for the quad's radio (FCC 2440-2480 MHz snippet); app ratings ~3.1-3.2 with connection complaints. Everything else -- flying weight (listings range 1.0-1.21 lb boxed vs 'under 250 g'), FOV, real latency, Wi-Fi range, endurance, failsafe on link loss, whether the physical remote overrides the app -- is unmeasured and should be bench-tested on day one.

RECOMMENDATIONS:
- Day-0 hardware validation before any model work: run turbodrone DRONE_TYPE=x69_lg against the real X69 (props off / guards on) and confirm: RC packets accepted at 25 Hz, takeoff/land/stop flags (0x01/0x80/0x02), tilt bits, RTSP stream up time, and what the drone does when RC packets stop (hover vs drift vs land) - that failsafe behavior defines the watchdog design.
- Measure, do not assume: weigh the drone with battery (sub-250 g decides FAA registration/Remote ID); measure RTSP glass-to-glass latency with a phone stopwatch in frame; measure stick-to-velocity response (step inputs at speed tier 0) and tilt-servo slew rate; calibrate camera FOV with a checkerboard. Feed all of these into the digital twin as parameters rather than guessing.
- Architecture: cascade control. Let the X69's optical-flow/baro hold be the inner loop; the outer loop outputs small stick offsets (speed tier 0; pitch/roll capped at ~20-25% of half-range, yaw ~30%, throttle +/-10% around 128) from a SAM 3.1 bbox/mask error, with deadbands, slew limits, and constant-velocity target prediction to cover ~1 s of loop latency. Keep the 25 Hz RC thread independent of perception (5-10 Hz) and reuse turbodrone's DirectStrategy/FlightController.set_axes_from.
- Perception pipeline: do not call a cloud model on every frame. Run SAM 3.1 text-prompted detection once per command (and on re-acquire), then track locally at 10-15 Hz on the 640px JPEG frames (SAM 3.1 video tracking if it can run on MPS/CPU, else a lightweight local tracker re-seeded by SAM every N seconds). Budget the cloud round trip (Baseten/Fireworks) at 300-800 ms and design the loop so a slow response degrades gracefully to hover.
- Safety state machine with hard timeouts: IDLE -> ARMED -> TAKEOFF -> HOVER -> FOLLOW -> (LOST>0.5 s -> HOVER, LOST>2 s -> LAND); any voice/keyboard 'stop' sends neutral sticks then land (0x80); reserve emergency stop (0x02, motor cut) for a dedicated hotkey. Enforce a wall-clock battery timer (6-7 min per pack) because no battery telemetry exists; log every session.
- Networking: the Mac must join the drone's 2.4 GHz AP (172.16.11.x) and loses internet; use a second interface (USB-Ethernet, USB Wi-Fi dongle, or iPhone USB tethering) with explicit route metrics so 172.16.11.0/24 goes to the drone and everything else to the internet, otherwise cloud-hosted Meta models are unreachable mid-flight. Disable macOS auto-join to other Wi-Fi during sessions.
- Indoor test range: >=5x5 m, >=2.5 m ceiling, patterned non-reflective mat (optical flow needs texture), bright even light, prop guards, netting or a tether for first autonomous runs, and the stock 2.4 GHz remote in hand (verify it can override app control). Use a person in a distinctive shirt at 2-4 m as the first target; test 'green car' with a toy RC car indoors or a parked object in a calm, empty field instead of a moving car on a road.
- Digital twin scope: a kinematic quadrotor with first-order velocity response to stick offsets, optical-flow hold modeled as position damping plus random-walk drift (0.2-0.5 m/10 s, larger when 'texture' is low), baro altitude noise (+/-0.2 m), 720p 16:9 camera at 15 fps with the measured FOV, a 0.5-1.0 s video delay buffer, 1 Hz keepalive and 25 Hz RC packet interface identical to the real adapter (same bytes to a fake 172.16.11.1 UDP server) so the exact flight code runs unmodified against the sim.
- Treat 'follow' and 'gesture' as this project's features, not the stock app's: the X69 app does not advertise follow-me, and its native follow/circle/point hooks are on inactive code paths, so do not plan on firmware-side tracking. Keep all autonomy on the Mac.
- Mitigate video flakiness: keep RTSP as default but implement automatic fallback to the UDP H.265 path, send the drone CLOSE then OPEN stream commands on reconnect (as the Android app does), and feed the control loop a 'frame age' value so stale frames freeze the sticks rather than steering on old data.

RISKS:
- Link-loss failsafe is unknown: if the X69 keeps flying its last stick command when UDP packets stop, a Wi-Fi hiccup (reported 'disconnects during flight', ~20-50 m range) becomes a flyaway indoors; must be tested with props guarded before autonomy.
- End-to-end latency ~1 s (0.5 s video + decode + cloud SAM round trip) can make a naive proportional follow loop oscillate or chase; low gains and prediction reduce responsiveness, so 'follow the green car' at real car speeds is infeasible with this airframe.
- RC flag table (takeoff/land/flip) partially unverified from obfuscated decompile; a wrong bit could trigger a flip or motor cut. No telemetry means no battery/altitude awareness - sessions must be timer-bounded.
- Weight ambiguity: if the X69 with battery is >=250 g, outdoor recreational flight legally requires FAA registration and Remote ID broadcast, which the drone cannot do; sub-250 g still requires a TRUST certificate and prohibits flight over people/moving vehicles - following a real car on a road is effectively off-limits.
- Optical-flow hover degrades on plain floors, low light, near walls, and in any breeze; expect drift and altitude bobbing that the outer loop must tolerate; outdoor wind above a light breeze overwhelms a brushed sub-250 g toy.
- Firmware/app variants (MainActivityUDP vs RTSP vs Trinity paths; app bumped to 1.0.15 in Aug 2026; JPEG path already dead on current firmware) mean the user's unit may differ from the May-2026 turbodrone capture; protocol may need re-probing.
- The Mac loses internet while attached to the drone AP; without a second NIC the cloud-hosted Meta models cannot be reached during flight.
- Realistic flight time is ~8-10 min per 1000 mAh pack with two packs and USB-C charging, so each test day yields perhaps 15-20 min of airtime unless extra packs are bought.
- Video quality: 1280x720 HEVC with digital stabilization, grainy in low light, 15 fps after transcoding - small or distant targets (a 'green car' at 20 m) will be only a few pixels wide for SAM 3.1.
- No community debugging history exists for the X69 (zero GitHub issues, no Reddit/RCGroups threads), so quirks will have to be discovered first-hand.

OPEN QUESTIONS FOR USER:
- Have you already flown the X69 with turbodrone (DRONE_TYPE=x69_lg) on your Mac, and did RC, takeoff/land, camera tilt and the RTSP video all work? Which app version/firmware does your unit pair with (X69 DRONE 1.0.13 vs 1.0.15)?
- What does your X69 weigh with battery on a kitchen scale (under or over 250 g)? Are you planning any outdoor flights, and do you hold an FAA TRUST certificate?
- Do you have a second network interface for the Mac (USB-Ethernet, USB Wi-Fi dongle, or iPhone USB tethering) so it can stay on the drone's Wi-Fi and reach cloud-hosted Meta models at the same time? If not, must the detector/tracker run fully local on the M5 Pro?
- Describe the indoor test space (floor pattern, size, ceiling height, lighting) and whether you have prop guards/netting; is a person in a colored shirt an acceptable first target instead of a car?
- Does the stock physical remote override the Wi-Fi app when both are active (useful as a manual kill switch), and are you willing to keep it in hand during autonomous tests?
- Is 'green car' a hard demo requirement (then a toy RC car indoors) or can the hackathon demo be person-following indoors with the sim showing the car case?
- How many spare 1000 mAh batteries do you have or are willing to buy (each gives ~8-10 min of flight)?



### Meta Global AI Developer Hackathon rules and judging  [verifier reliability: high]

SUMMARY:
IDENTITY AND STATUS. There is exactly one matching event this season: the "Meta Global AI Developer Hackathon", co-run by Meta (Meta Superintelligence Labs) and Cerebral Valley Enterprises, Inc. Official pages: https://dev.meta.ai/events/global-hackathon and https://cerebralvalley.ai/e/meta-ai-developer-hackathon; the "Hackathon Terms & Conditions ('Official Rules') and Event Waiver" (dated Oct 25, 2026) is embedded in https://cerebralvalley.ai/e/meta-ai-developer-hackathon/waiver (JS-rendered; I extracted the full text to /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-russellvancuren-Projects-MetaHackathonDrone/a9780c4a-b149-4e3b-98c5-8dcb0c8772ee/scratchpad/dl_rules/waiver.txt). No Devpost/lablab listing exists. The participant guide (https://cerebralvalley.ai/e/meta-ai-developer-hackathon/details), Discord invite, credit-claim form, and submission portal (https://cerebralvalley.ai/e/meta-ai-developer-hackathon/hackathon/submit) are login-walled; the "Credit Terms" PDF is on a signed Facebook CDN URL I could not retrieve. Submission-form details below therefore come from two independent participant transcriptions of the guide (https://github.com/JannetEkka/DSProjects/issues/40 and https://raw.githubusercontent.com/meta-ai-hackathon/meta-hack/main/info/event%20guide.md), which agree with each other.

DATES (ET, verified on the official page/waiver). Applications Oct 13–Oct 25 11:59 PM, cap 20,000 — CLOSED; you must already be an accepted participant to submit. Kickoff Oct 26 12 PM; workshops Oct 27–Nov 4; PROJECT SUBMISSIONS DUE NOV 4, 9:00 PM ET (about 36 hours from today, Nov 3); judging Nov 4–8; closing ceremony with live top-5-per-track finals Nov 9; bonus prizes announced on/about Nov 10; community-vote winner Nov 16 (participant copy). Late submissions are not considered. Format: virtual only, worldwide, free.

ELIGIBILITY/TEAMS (waiver). 18+; sanctions/country exclusions; Meta/CV staff excluded; one track per participant and per project; Indie Developer track = individuals/students, teams of up to 4 (solo allowed); Startup track = technical founders, Representative must be an active employee. A Representative submits once per team and lists all members.

REQUIRED META MODELS. Waiver: "Participants build with Meta AI products, including Muse Spark, Glimmer, SAM 3, and any other Meta AI products identified on the Event page." Event page list: Muse Spark series, Muse Wonder, Muse Image, SAM 3.1 via Meta Model API; Meta open-weights models; Muse Code. Participant guide adds Muse Voice Transcribe and names the open weights as Muse Glimmer and Muse Spark Open; judging stage 1 is a pass/fail gate: fits a theme AND "genuinely uses Meta Model API and/or open-weight models". Llama is not named anywhere. Third-party hosts: the official page says Baseten/Modal/Fireworks credits are "to use Meta's open-weights models", and the $10K "Best Build on Open Weight Models" bonus explicitly counts Glimmer/Spark Open "on own hardware or partner inference"; an organizer ruling relayed from Meta (https://github.com/Tways-study/Basa/pull/28) confirmed unsuffixed meta-models/muse-spark-open on a team's own Baseten account counts. So Fireworks/Baseten-hosted Glimmer or Spark Open count; SAM 3.1 via the API counts for the gate but not for the open-weights bonus (https://github.com/seanudeke/Nightshift/pull/45).

JUDGING (waiver, verbatim weights): Impact 30% ("real-world potential... whether it fits one of the problem statements"); Use of Meta AI Tooling 25% ("beyond a basic integration... surfaced unexpected capabilities"); Technical Depth & Execution 20%; Demo 25% ("working, impressive demo, whether the product holds up live, and the quality of the demo video"). Scored 0–10 per criterion; top 5 per track go to a live final on Nov 9 (same rubric). Track lenses: Indie = creativity, technical ambition, whether it works; Startup = technical execution, production readiness. Themes: "Build for Your World" and "Push the Frontier" — the latter literally says "robotics... Give a machine a new capability... unlock a better way to understand and control a physical system. Show us what becomes possible when these models meet the real world."

PRIZES (waiver): Indie $400K = 150/100/75/50/25K; Startup $550K = 250/150/75/50/25K; bonus up to $50K (guide: Most Viral $20K by X engagement on the demo post through Nov 8 — post link must be on the form; Best Build on Open Weight Models $10K; Community Vote $10K; Best Agentic Use-Case $5K; Best Creative Build $5K for Muse Image/Wonder). USD via Tremendous within 30 days; W-9/W-8BEN; winners pay taxes/fees.

SUBMISSION ARTIFACTS (guide, via participants): one submission per team on the CV portal; PUBLIC repo; 3-MINUTE demo video showing only what was built during the hackathon; all contributors listed; a deployed/live product (judges use video + code + live product); form fields: name, tagline, description, models used, optional X post. NEW WORK ONLY: "demo shows only what you built during the event — unclear contribution = immediate disqualification"; one transcription says only startups may build on an existing codebase, and reuse of ideas must be stated in the README. The waiver itself permits reuse: "Participants may also include open source software or hardware, provided applicable open source licenses are followed and the Submission enhances or builds upon the underlying open source product" — turbodrone is Apache-2.0 (LICENSE/NOTICE in the ref clone), so forking it is allowed if disclosed and the new work is clearly delineated. IP: entrant keeps ownership; grants Organizers a perpetual, irrevocable, royalty-free, non-exclusive license incl. marketing; nothing is confidential; California law.

SAFETY/HARDWARE: no drone- or hardware-specific rules; the waiver's risk clause covers only virtual risks. Anti-projects list (guide, "verbatim"): medical advice, basic RAG, simple image analyzers, simple education chatbots, job screeners, nutrition coaches, personality analyzers, sports analyzers/coaches, "anything political or involving war". One participant's own (unofficial) extrapolation lists "drones, target recognition, ISR, anything defense" as banned in spirit — a positioning risk, not a rule. Model licenses add constraints: SAM License bans ITAR/military/warfare/espionage/weapons uses (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/facebookresearch/sam3/main/LICENSE); Muse Glimmer is Apache-2.0 + Usage Policy (https://huggingface.co/meta-models/Muse-Glimmer-30B); Muse Spark Open uses the "Muse Model License" requiring "Built with Muse Spark Open" display (https://huggingface.co/meta-models/Muse-Spark-Open-816B-A42B).

CREDITS: $150 Meta Model API credits per selected participant, "valid for a 10-day development window" (waiver); claimed individually via a form in the guide by Nov 4 9 PM ET; a credit card with a temporary pre-auth hold is required; set a spend limit on dev.meta.ai/billing. Partners (official page): Baseten $100 + 2M TPM (guide: up to 2,000 participants, use-case review, hackathon model ID meta-models/muse-spark-open-1026), Modal $100 (first 1,000), Fireworks $100 on the official page but $25/team "shortlisted only" in the guide copies. All discretionary.

PLAN-RELEVANT API FACTS (verified on dev.meta.ai docs): base URL https://api.meta.ai/v1, OpenAI-compatible Responses/Chat Completions. Per-team limits (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/pricing-rate-limits): sam-3.1 3,000 RPM, $2.50/1k images, $0.20/1k video frames (50-frame minimum per video request, ≤15,000 frames, MP4/MOV H.264/H.265, ≤16 objects, per-frame streamed deltas, box in source pixels + mask; parsers @meta-sam/parser and meta-sam-parser; concept prompt = one short noun phrase, lists/negation unreliable) (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/sam/segmenting); muse-spark-1.3 $1.25/$4.25 per M, 3,000 RPM/4M TPM (contributor $0.10/$0.20 but 150 RPM and data used for training), 1M context, image input up to 50 images/request, structured output via text.format json_schema strict (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/structured-output), and "perception grounding" returning points/boxes on a 0–1000 grid (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/image-understanding); muse-voice-transcribe-1.0 $0.18/hour, realtime WebSocket wss://api.meta.ai/v1/asr/realtime, 16/24 kHz PCM, PUSH_TO_TALK/ENDPOINTING modes, keyword biasing, 25 languages (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/speech-to-text/). SAM 3.1 open checkpoints (facebook/sam3.1, gated, 848M params, released Mar 27, 2026) require CUDA ≥12.6 with no MPS support stated and no transformers integration (https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam3) — on the M5 Pro use the API or a Modal GPU. Muse Glimmer 30B (dense 29.6B incl. 1.8B vision encoder, 131K ctx) runs locally via llama.cpp b10353+ with Q4_K_M ~17 GB + 1.4 GB mmproj (vision and tool calling work; ~24 GiB resident at 16K ctx) (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/muse-glimmer/llama-cpp); Fireworks offers free LoRA SFT of Glimmer through Nov 30 but serving the LoRA needs a paid on-demand deployment (https://docs.fireworks.ai/fine-tuning/training-api/cookbook/muse-glimmer); Baseten serves Spark Open on Model APIs (inference.baseten.co/v1) and Glimmer on dedicated H100 (https://www.baseten.co/library/muse-spark-open/).
