Pure PyTorch with an inference_device parameter, so MPS/CPU on the M5 Pro is possible in principle (my inference; no Apple benchmarks found; default 1008 px, image_size=560 is documented as a faster lower-accuracy option; streaming mode disables hotstart dedupe heuristics). Correction 2: Fireworks serverless Glimmer was deprecated Sep 25, 2026 ('Serverless: Not Supported'), so Fireworks Glimmer inference means a paid on-demand deployment too. Also Core ML ports of SAM 3.1 exist (AllanVester/SAM3.1-CoreML, dustbeast/SAM3.1-CoreML-FP16) with no usage docs or benchmarks. (https://huggingface.co/docs/transformers/en/model_doc/sam3_video)

VERIFIER MISSED FINDINGS:
- Local Mac SAM path the researcher missed: transformers v5.22.1 ships SAM 3 (not 3.1) end-to-end — Sam3Model (text concept -> all instances), Sam3VideoModel streaming mode (per-frame model(inference_session, frame=...) with persistent object_ids, boxes in absolute XYXY pixels, masks at source resolution, multi-prompt in one pass via prompt_to_obj_ids), Sam3TrackerModel (point/box re-prompting). Checkpoint facebook/sam3 is gated under the same SAM License (~0.9B params). Device-agnostic PyTorch, so it can be tried on MPS/CPU on the M5 Pro as an offline/digital-twin fallback; no Apple FPS data exists, and streaming disables hotstart heuristics (more false positives/duplicates). Sources: https://huggingface.co/docs/transformers/en/model_doc/sam3_video , https://huggingface.co/facebook/sam3 , https://huggingface.co/facebook/sam3.1 (explicitly 'no Hugging Face Transformers integration').
- SAM 3.1 API cost/latency shape for a live control loop (my arithmetic from https://dev.meta.ai/docs/pricing-rate-limits and /docs/sam/segmenting): no live-camera input mode exists, so a 5-10 Hz loop must either POST single frames as images ($2.50/1k = $0.0025/frame -> ~$45/h at 5 Hz, ~$90/h at 10 Hz; the $150 credit buys ~3.3 h at 5 Hz) or POST short clips as video ($0.20/1k frames, billed min 50 frames = $0.01/request minimum, streamed per-frame deltas, 15,000-frame cap) which adds clip-buffering latency. 3,000 RPM per team = 50 req/s, not the bottleneck. One concept per request; lists return zero matches; object identity is stable only within one response, so cross-request re-identification (e.g., IoU association or Muse Spark grounding box -> SAM box prompt) must be done client-side. Add metadata.include_confidence='true' for a c score.
- Hosting facts: Meta Model API serves muse-wonder-1.0, muse-spark-1.3/-contributor, 1.2/-contributor, 1.1, muse-image-1.0, muse-voice-transcribe-1.0, sam-3.1 and NOT Glimmer or Spark Open (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/models). Baseten library page (RSC payload saved by the researcher) lists Spark Open on Model APIs as technical name meta-models/muse-spark-open at https://inference.baseten.co/v1 (chat.completions) and Glimmer only as a dedicated deployment ('runs the BF16 model on a single H100 through an OpenAI-compatible vLLM API' with a 2.56B DFlash draft, model id meta-models/Muse-Glimmer-30B). The meta-hack guide transcription says the hackathon Baseten ID is meta-models/muse-spark-open-1026 (conflicts with the Basa team's relayed ruling). Fireworks: accounts/fireworks/models/muse-glimmer-30b is on-demand only (serverless deprecated 2026-09-25) (https://fireworks.ai/models/fireworks/muse-glimmer-30b).
- Model-license constraints relevant to a drone: Muse Spark Open uses the 'Muse Model License' requiring 'Built with Muse Spark Open' prominently displayed in the UI, and its Exhibit A policy prohibits 'Military, warfare, nuclear industries or applications, espionage' AND 'Operation of critical infrastructure, transportation technologies, or heavy machinery' (https://huggingface.co/meta-models/Muse-Spark-Open-816B-A42B) — a gray area if Spark Open sits in the flight-control loop (inference: keep Spark Open to intent parsing/narration, or use Glimmer, which is Apache-2.0 + Usage Policy with no attribution clause, released Aug 2026, dense 29.6B incl. 1.8B ViT-G/14 encoder, 131,072 ctx, text+image in, no audio: https://huggingface.co/meta-models/Muse-Glimmer-30B). SAM License 1.b.v bans ITAR activities, 'military or warfare purposes', espionage, guns/illegal weapons (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/facebookresearch/sam3/main/LICENSE).
- Running Glimmer on the 48 GB M5 Pro: dev.meta.ai/docs/muse-glimmer/llama-cpp gives Q4_K_M ~17 GB (+1.4 GB mmproj, +1.6 GB optional dflash draft) measured ~24.1 GiB resident at 16K ctx; bf16 is ~60 GB (does not fit); Ollama tag muse-glimmer:30b-mlx and RadixArk MLX quants (q4km-gs128, q4, q4k-dynamic) exist; SGLang's MLX path 'targets Apple silicon with 48 GB or more' — exactly the dev machine, so leave headroom for ffmpeg/SAM. Vision input works out of the box in llama.cpp (projector auto-downloads) and tool calling uses the embedded ATEM template. No Apple tokens/s benchmarks were found (web search budget exhausted before that query).
- Logistics/API details not in the summary: closing ceremony Mon Nov 9 12 PM ET announces top 10 (5/track) then live final judging and opens the community vote (winner Nov 16); Discord office hours weekdays 10-11 AM ET in #office-hours (guide.md). ASR (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/speech-to-text/): modes PUSH_TO_TALK (default), ENDPOINTING, DIARIZATION; raw PCM16 mono at 24 kHz or 16 kHz; file endpoint POST /v1/asr/transcribe (WAV only, <=10 min, <=32 MB); realtime session <=60 min; turn-level timestamps only; 'keywords' bias spelling; 25 languages with code-switching. Muse Spark grounding (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/image-understanding): point/box/count modes on a 0-1000 grid (clamp; values like 1001 occur), ~1,300-1,500 input tokens per ~1280 px image (~$0.002/frame at $1.25/M), 50 images max per request. Structured output (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/structured-output): response_format (Chat) or text.format (Responses), strict defaults to false; strict mode needs root object, additionalProperties:false, all properties required, no allOf/oneOf, no recursion.

VERIFIER NOTES: VERIFICATION METHOD. Fetched/greped 20+ distinct pages: dev.meta.ai (events/global-hackathon, docs/pricing-rate-limits, docs/quickstart, docs/sam/segmenting, docs/sam/overview, docs/models, docs/speech-to-text, docs/image-understanding, docs/structured-output, docs/muse-glimmer/llama-cpp), cerebralvalley.ai event page (curl + grep of the live HTML, which embeds the full Official Rules; the /waiver route returns the same bundle and shows only 'Loading...' to WebFetch), github.com (facebookresearch/sam3 README, RELEASE_SAM3p1.md, LICENSE raw; Tways-study/Basa PR 28 and JannetEkka/DSProjects issue 40 via gh api incl. comments; meta-ai-hackathon/meta-hack guide.md raw; seanudeke PR 45; nmheinrich/seeyable issue 1), huggingface.co (facebook/sam3, facebook/sam3.1, meta-models/Muse-Glimmer-30B, meta-models/Muse-Spark-Open-816B-A42B, transformers docs sam3/sam3_video/sam3_tracker, two SAM3.1 Core ML repos), fireworks.ai model page + docs cookbook, pypi meta-sam-parser, superpowerdaily.com (Oct 22 news), docs.baseten.co. Baseten library pages are client-rendered (WebFetch sees only the title); I used the researcher's saved RSC payloads (dl_rules/baseten_mso.html, baseten_glimmer.html) for Baseten specifics. The session's WebSearch budget ran out after 5 searches, so Apple-silicon Glimmer/SAM benchmarks and Modal's Spark Open endpoint were not searched. The researcher's three extracted text files (waiver.txt/details.txt/submit.txt) are byte-identical copies of the event-page bundle — fine for the Rules, but it means the participant guide itself was never read directly; all guide facts rest on two participant transcriptions that agree with each other.

HEADLINE RESULTS. 11 of 15 claims confirmed on primary sources; 4 partially correct. No fabricated numbers found: every price, rate limit, prize, date, percentage and version number checked out. Errors are (a) quote/source attribution: the 'immediate disqualification' phrase and the exact anti-project wording were attributed to the wrong transcription and quoted as verbatim when the two copies differ ('war-related' vs 'about war'); the 'drones/ISR/defense' extrapolation is unsourced (not in DSProjects #40); (b) the Baseten 'organizer ruling' is one team's unquoted hearsay ('relayed from Josh at Meta') and conflicts with the guide transcription that says the hackathon Baseten model ID is meta-models/muse-spark-open-1026; (c) the most consequential technical slip: 'no transformers integration' is true for facebook/sam3.1 only — SAM 3 (facebook/sam3) has a complete transformers integration including a documented streaming per-frame video mode with persistent object IDs, which is the only plausible fully-local text-prompted tracker for the no-CUDA M5 Pro (unbenchmarked on MPS; I infer feasibility from it being device-agnostic PyTorch). Also Fireworks serverless Glimmer was deprecated Sep 25, 2026, so 'Fireworks-hosted Glimmer' means paid on-demand GPU.

PLANNER-RELEVANT CONFIRMATIONS. Deadline Nov 4 9:00 PM ET (about 23 hours from the Nov 3 22:00 local timestamp on the researcher's files, not 36). Pass/fail gate needs a theme fit plus genuine Meta Model API and/or open-weights use; SAM 3.1 via API clears the gate; the $10K open-weights bonus needs Glimmer or Spark Open on own hardware or partner inference. Rules allow building on Apache-2.0 turbodrone if licenses are followed and the submission 'enhances or builds upon' it; the guide's 'new work only' rule means the demo must show only hackathon work and the README should delineate reused code. No hardware/safety rules exist; the risk clause is virtual-only. Judging weights 30/25/20/25 with Demo 25% explicitly scoring 'whether the product holds up live' and 'the quality of the demo video' (3 minutes, public repo, deployed/live product, all contributors listed). Push the Frontier text literally invites 'control a physical system'. Position away from 'simple image analyzers' and anything war/political; SAM License and Muse Model License both bar military/espionage uses, and Spark Open's policy additionally bars 'transportation technologies, or heavy machinery' operation and requires a 'Built with Muse Spark Open' UI notice.

API SHAPE FOR THE CONTROL LOOP (verified numbers, inferred costs). SAM 3.1: POST https://api.meta.ai/v1/responses only; one noun phrase per request; images via URL/base64, video via video_url/file_id (MP4/MOV, H.264/H.265/MPEG-4, <=15,000 frames, 50-frame billing minimum, <=16 tracked objects per frame); boxes in source pixels + masks, c confidence optional; per-frame streamed deltas; ids stable within one response only; parsers @meta-sam/parser >=0.0.13 / meta-sam-parser >=0.0.6 (PyPI 0.1.0). 3,000 RPM per team. Image polling at 5 Hz costs ~$45/h (inference), so budget the $150 credit or use short video clips / local SAM 3 for the digital twin. Muse Spark 1.3: $1.25/$4.25 per M, 50 images/request, grounding on a 0-1000 grid, strict JSON schema via text.format/response_format. ASR: wss://api.meta.ai/v1/asr/realtime, PCM16 mono 16/24 kHz, PUSH_TO_TALK/ENDPOINTING/DIARIZATION, $0.18/h, 256 concurrent streams. Glimmer locally: llama.cpp b10353+, Q4_K_M 17 GB + 1.4 GB mmproj, ~24 GiB resident at 16K ctx on a 48 GB machine; Ollama muse-glimmer:30b-mlx exists. Credit Terms PDF link on the CV page is an expired signed fbcdn URL (403); reload the event page in a browser to get a fresh one.

RECOMMENDATIONS:
- Before any coding: confirm acceptance status, log into https://cerebralvalley.ai/e/meta-ai-developer-hackathon/details to read the participant guide verbatim (submission form fields, Discord, credit-claim form, Credit Terms PDF), claim the $150 Meta Model API credits (card pre-auth, set a spend limit on dev.meta.ai/billing) and request Baseten/Modal credits immediately — all of this expires Nov 4, 9 PM ET.
- Treat Nov 4, 9:00 PM ET as a hard scope boundary (~36 h): build and demo in the digital twin first (deployable web app = the 'live product' judges can touch), then capture one real X69 flight clip for the 3-minute video; do not gate submission on the fine-tune or the real drone.
- Enter the Indie track under 'Push the Frontier' and frame the project as a consumer follow-me camera drone with natural-language subject selection ('follow the person in the blue shirt'); avoid 'target', 'surveillance', 'ISR', or identity recognition in copy and code (attribute-based descriptions only), and show a geofence/kill-switch/hover-on-loss safety layer — this keeps clear of the 'anything involving war' anti-project rule, the SAM License military prohibition, and reviewer bias.
- Maximize the 25% 'Use of Meta AI Tooling' score by chaining multiple Meta models in the live loop: Muse Voice Transcribe realtime WebSocket (ENDPOINTING mode, keyword bias on color/object words) -> Muse Spark 1.3 with strict json_schema structured output to emit {target_noun_phrase, attributes, action, standoff_distance, safety_flags} -> SAM 3.1 concept prompt on the frame -> if several matches, Muse Spark image input (perception grounding boxes on the 0–1000 grid) as a disambiguator -> PID/visual-servo loop -> turbodrone x69_lg RC. Document each model's role in README and the video.
- Design the perception loop around API economics and unknown API latency: call SAM 3.1 at 1–3 Hz on downscaled JPEGs (3,000 RPM is ample; $2.50/1k images means ~$9–27/hour) and bridge frames with a local lightweight tracker (OpenCV CSRT/KCF or mask-centroid prediction) so the 5–10 Hz control loop never blocks on the network; measure p50/p95 SAM API latency on day one and keep the clip/video endpoint (50-frame minimum) for offline regression tests in the twin.
- Satisfy the open-weights requirement (and the $10K bonus) with Muse Glimmer 30B: run it locally on the M5 Pro via llama.cpp b10353+ (Q4_K_M + mmproj, OpenAI-compatible server on :8080) as the offline intent parser/fallback and optionally mirror it on Baseten/Fireworks; if 'train an open Meta model' is non-negotiable, do a small LoRA SFT on Fireworks (free through Nov 30) using synthetic command->intent JSON pairs generated by Muse Spark, but confirm first whether the LoRA can be exported for local serving, since Fireworks LoRA serving needs a paid on-demand GPU.
- Repo hygiene for the new-work and IP rules: fork turbodrone with its Apache-2.0 LICENSE and NOTICE intact, state in the README exactly which modules are reused vs. built during the event (commit history from Oct 26 onward), include setup instructions, list all contributors, and keep the repo public at submission time.
- Prepare submission assets explicitly: a 3-minute video (voice command -> twin -> real flight, honest about what is sim), a public demo URL for the twin, the 'models used' list (muse-voice-transcribe-1.0, muse-spark-1.3, sam-3.1, Muse Glimmer 30B [+ Baseten/Fireworks]), and an X post link on the form if chasing the Most Viral bonus.
- If Muse Spark Open is used anywhere (e.g., Baseten meta-models/muse-spark-open), add the 'Built with Muse Spark Open' attribution required by the Muse Model License; Glimmer (Apache-2.0) needs only its usage policy respected.
- Make the digital twin faithful to the X69 constraints turbodrone exposes: 1280x720 HEVC RTSP with ffmpeg decode latency, discrete RC command cadence, camera tilt, brushed-motor drift — inject latency/noise so the same control loop runs unchanged against sim and hardware; this is the 'Technical Depth' story.

RISKS:
- Hard deadline: Nov 4, 9:00 PM ET (~36 h from Nov 3); late entries are not considered, and if the user was never accepted there is no way to enter now (applications closed Oct 25).
- New-work rule vs. building on turbodrone: the guide says the demo must show only work built during the event and (per one participant copy) only startups may build on an existing codebase, while the waiver allows building on open-source products; ambiguity resolves only through clear README disclosure and a visible delta — otherwise 'unclear contribution = immediate disqualification'.
- Positioning risk: a drone that tracks people can be read as surveillance/defense; the guide bans 'anything involving war', one participant explicitly extrapolated 'drones, target recognition, ISR' as banned in spirit, and the SAM License forbids military/weapons uses. Consumer follow-me framing and attribute-only (no identity) selection are essential.
- Credits: the $150 is 'valid for a 10-day development window' (likely expiring at event end — Credit Terms PDF not retrievable), requires a card pre-auth, and partner credits are capped/discretionary; Fireworks amount conflicts ($100 official page vs $25 guide copies).
- SAM 3.1 API latency is unpublished; the open checkpoints are CUDA-only (no MPS), so on the Mac the live loop depends on network round-trips or a rented GPU; at 5 Hz the image endpoint costs ~$45/hour, which can exhaust the $150 quickly during tuning.
- Per-team rate limits and the contributor tier (150 RPM, data used for training) can throttle a multi-model loop; use standard tier for anything latency-sensitive.
- Judges score 'whether the product holds up live' — hardware cannot be tested by remote judges, so a flaky or missing deployed twin directly costs points in the 25% Demo criterion.
- Fine-tuning scope creep: Fireworks free SFT ends Nov 30 but serving a LoRA requires paid on-demand GPUs, and LoRA export for local llama.cpp serving is unverified; MLX QLoRA of a 30B on 48 GB is unverified. A failed training detour can sink the deadline.
- Submission-form specifics (3-minute video, public repo, deployed product, X post field) come from participant transcriptions of a login-walled guide, not from a page I could read; verify in the portal before recording.
- Bonus prize 'Best Build on Open Weight Models' counts only Glimmer/Spark Open (per guide copies); SAM 3.1 via API or self-hosted does not qualify for that bonus.
- X69 video pipeline latency (RTSP HEVC via ffmpeg) and brushed-motor control noise are not characterized in the README; an untested real-flight demo on the last day may fail — the video should lead with the twin and include hardware only if it works.

OPEN QUESTIONS FOR USER:
- Were you accepted into the hackathon before Oct 26 (applications closed Oct 25), in which track (Indie or Startup), and have you accepted the Official Rules/waiver in the Cerebral Valley portal? If not accepted, submission is impossible and the plan becomes a portfolio/next-event build.
- Is the goal to submit by Nov 4, 9:00 PM ET (about 36 hours away), or is the hackathon framing for a longer build? This decides whether the real-drone flight and the model fine-tune are in or out of scope.
- Have you already claimed the $150 Meta Model API credits (card on file, spend limit set) and requested Baseten/Modal/Fireworks credits? Do you have a working dev.meta.ai API key today?
- What do you mean by 'train an open meta model' — a LoRA fine-tune of Muse Glimmer (e.g., command->intent JSON), or is prompt-only acceptable? Do you have any training data, and is the $10K open-weights bonus the motivation?
- Are you solo or up to 4 people, who is the Representative, and did any teammate write code before Oct 26 (new-work rule)? Is it acceptable to publish the repo as a public fork of turbodrone with disclosure of reused modules?
- Can the digital twin be deployed publicly (judges may use the 'live product'), and do you want to pursue the Most Viral bonus (requires an X post link on the submission form)?
- Is the X69 charged, flyable indoors/outdoors safely, and connectable from the Mac today (Wi-Fi 172.16.11.1 RTSP works)? Any constraint on recording a flight clip before the deadline?
- What cloud budget beyond credits is acceptable for Modal/Fireworks on-demand GPUs (self-hosted SAM 3.1 or LoRA serving) if the API path proves too slow or costly?



### Turbodrone codebase deep-dive and extension points  [verifier reliability: high]

SUMMARY:
Repo state (verified via local clone + GitHub API, https://github.com/marshallrichards/turbodrone): master = 2c09cf8 (2026-06-22 "README small update"), 580 stars, 79 forks, Apache-2.0, 6 open issues/PRs, no issue mentions X69. Activity is single-maintainer bursts (Apr–Jun 2026: cooingdv, wifi_cam, x69_lg h265 then RTSP) and nothing since; treat as dormant. PR #35 (open) documents Linux firewalls blocking inbound UDP video (ports x69_lg h265=1234, jpeg=7070). Issue #22: a macOS user saw choppy video and 2–5 s control dropouts via FlightController (maintainer only tested Linux/Windows). Issue #21: maintainer says no API docs; web_server.py is the seam; camera is the only sensor. All line refs below are at 2c09cf8 (https://github.com/marshallrichards/turbodrone/blob/2c09cf8/...).

Startup: backend/web_server.py lifespan L244 picks DRONE_TYPE from env/.env (dotenv in utils/logging_config.py:32). x69_lg branch L364-425: X69LgRcModel + X69LgRcProtocolAdapter(172.16.11.1:23458), CONTROL_RATE default 25 Hz, video class by X69_LG_VIDEO_MODE (default rtsp). Order: VideoReceiverService L445, sleep 1 s, FlightController L468-469, PluginManager only if PLUGINS_ENABLED=true (L97, L474-486), FramePump thread L491-497. main.py is the curses CLI with identical wiring (L142-176).

RC path: services/flight_controller.py daemon thread (L32-34); loop L94-122: model.update(dt, axes in [-1,1]) -> protocol.build_control_packet(model) -> send -> sleep(1/rate); all send errors swallowed L112-119. Input API set_axes_from(source,t,y,p,r) clamps to +/-1 (L63-69). control/strategies.py: IncrementalStrategy ramps via BaseRCModel.update_axes (models/base_rc.py L108-170); DirectStrategy maps absolute via _scale_normalised (L90-97). models/x69_lg_rc.py: StickRange(0,128,255) L12, presets L14-18, speed_index default 1 L35, one-shot flags L24-28, camera_tilt_state 0/1/2 L31. protocols/x69_lg_rc_protocol_adapter.py: 20-byte payload `66 14 RR PP TT YY F1 F2 00x10 XOR 99` (L67-80, XOR over bytes 2..17), wrapped `ca 47 d5 00 14 00 00 00`+payload = 28 bytes (L119-120); 1 Hz keepalive `ca 47 d1 00 00 00 00 00` thread L122-126; byte6 flags takeoff 0x01, estop 0x02, calibrate 0x04, flip 0x08, land 0x80; byte7 tilt up 0x08/down 0x10 (L27-34); speed scale {0:0.55,1:0.75,2:1.0} on roll/pitch only (L57-61,157-161); one-shots clear after ONE packet (L150-155), i.e. 40 ms on the wire. Adapter never recv()s: zero telemetry. docs/research/x69_lg.md L219-228 says only estop 0x02 and tilt bits were confirmed from the decompile; takeoff/land/flip bits came from obfuscated handlers and "packet captures should be used" (README nonetheless lists X69 as Tested).

Video: services/video_receiver.py thread instantiates the adapter, loops get_frame(timeout=1), recreates it after errors with 5 s backoff (L79-141). VideoFrame = frame_id, data (JPEG bytes), format, timestamp (models/video_frame.py). All X69 modes yield JPEG: rtsp (protocols/x69_lg_rtsp_video_protocol.py) cv2.VideoCapture("rtsp://172.16.11.1/live/ch00_1", CAP_FFMPEG, opts `rtsp_transport;tcp|fflags;nobuffer|flags;low_delay` L45-54) -> imencode q=85 (L198-199), queue maxsize 2, reconnect 2 s x10; h265 (protocols/x69_lg_video_protocol.py) UDP 1234, 32-byte `c6 6c a5 5a` header reassembly L396-445, open/close/iframe cmds to 23459 (L229-231), ffmpeg `-f hevc -i pipe:0 -vf scale=640:-2,fps=15 -c:v mjpeg -q:v 12` (L95-123); jpeg = legacy 7070/7080. web_server FramePump L698-757 drains RAW_Q (DroppingQueue maxsize 2, L530), publishes frame.data to FrameHub for GET /mjpeg (multipart, L759-782) and put_nowait(VideoFrame) into PLUGIN_FRAME_Q (DroppingQueue maxsize 100 -> up to ~6 s backlog if a plugin is slower than 15 fps). Frontend is `<img src="http://localhost:8000/mjpeg">` (App.tsx:27); localhost:8000 is hard-coded in 7 places.

Plugins: plugins/base.py Plugin(name, flight_controller, frame_source, overlay_queue); start/_on_start L31-37, idempotent stop L39-47, send_overlay(list) L56-61. plugins/manager.py discovers every Plugin subclass in the plugins package by class name (L122-135), gives each a generator polling the shared queue every 0.2 s (L61-73); REST GET /plugins, POST /plugins/{name}/start|stop (web_server L551-601), one plugin at a time (L572-577). Plugins own their thread; no framework rate. While a plugin runs, /ws axes are ignored (L632-636) but takeoff/land/estop still execute (L666-683). Overlays: JSON list of {type:"rect", coords:[x1,y1,x2,y2] normalized, color} on /ws/overlays (L784-824; useOverlays.ts:5-9); DrawingOverlay.tsx renders only rect (L20-33).

FollowPlugin (plugins/follow/follow_plugin.py): ultralytics YOLO("yolov10n.pt") L54 (weights gitignored, auto-download), classes=[0] persons, imgsz 320, conf 0.65, FOLLOW_FPS 20 (L40-42, L124-131); largest box L146; follow_controller.py bang-bang: yaw=+/-20 if |cx-0.5|>0.15, pitch=+20 if width<0.28, -20 if >0.82 (L40-55); sent as yaw/100, pitch/100 under DirectStrategy, expo 0 (L72-74, L165) -> raw 128+/-25 (x0.75 pitch at speed 1). Throttle/roll always 0; lost target -> set_axes(0,0,0,0)+clear overlay (L139-143), no search/timeout. test_yolo.py is a webcam demo.

Debug seam: DRONE_TYPE=debug -> DebugRcModel (no-op update, no strategy/emergency_stop) + DebugRcProtocolAdapter lacking build_control_packet (debug_rc_protocol_adapter.py L5-12; FlightController AttributeError swallowed every tick) + webcam DebugVideoProtocolAdapter. It is a smoke stub, not a simulator. NoVideoProtocolAdapter = RC-only placeholder. Real seams: (a) new BaseRCModel + adapter (build_control_packet/send_control_packet/toggle_debug) + video adapter (start/stop/is_running/get_frame/get_packets); (b) a network emulator on 172.16.11.1 (UDP 23458 parser, 23459 cmds, RTSP /live/ch00_1 HEVC 1280x720) so the untouched x69_lg stack runs against it.

Frontend WS (hooks/useControls.ts): {type:"axes", mode:"inc"|"abs"|"mouse", camera_tilt_direction:-1|0|1, throttle,yaw,pitch,roll} at 30 Hz (L329-345); one-shots takeoff/land/estop/set_speed_index{speed_index} (L376-381); backend also accepts set_profile{name}, set_camera_tilt, emergency_stop (web_server L627-688). lib/ws.ts is unused.

Deps/tests/license: requirements.txt unpinned (numpy, opencv-python, fastapi, uvicorn[standard], python-multipart, starlette, python-dotenv, ultralytics). uv pip compile on this Mac resolves for Python 3.13 and 3.15: torch 2.15.0, torchvision 0.29.2, ultralytics 8.4.202 (AGPL-3.0, https://pypi.org/project/ultralytics/), opencv-python 5.0.0.93, numpy 2.5.4, fastapi 0.162.0, starlette 1.10.0, uvicorn 0.54.0; torch 2.15.0 ships macosx_14_0_arm64 wheels for 3.11–3.15 (https://pypi.org/project/torch/). Tests are stdlib unittest run from backend/ (python -m unittest tests.test_x69_lg_protocol ...); no pytest config; RC test bypasses __init__ via __new__, video test injects FakeDecoder (the sandbox denied me executing them). License Apache-2.0 + NOTICE whose block "must remain" in derivatives.

SAM facts shaping extension point 2: hosted sam-3.1 (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/sam/segmenting, https://dev.meta.ai/docs/pricing-rate-limits): POST https://api.meta.ai/v1/responses, OpenAI SDK compatible, image via input_image data URL, video only as whole MP4/MOV clips (<=15,000 frames, billed min 50), $2.50/1k images, $0.20/1k frames, 3,000 RPM; output = pixel boxes + base85 masks + integer IDs stable within one response (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/sam/reading-segmentation); no live-frame tracking API. Local SAM 3 officially needs CUDA 12.6 (https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam3); MPS/CPU PR #627 still open; sam3-mac gives ~3 s/image on CPU (M4 Pro) and no video tracking (https://github.com/benreichman/sam3-mac); Ultralytics has sam3.pt (gated, 3.45 GB, 2921 ms/img on RTX PRO 6000; SAM 3.1 image-only) (https://docs.ultralytics.com/models/sam-3/). Conclusion: SAM provides low-rate semantic (re)acquisition; a local lightweight tracker must close the 10–15 Hz loop.

KEY CLAIMS:
- [high] X69 RC wire format: 28-byte UDP datagram to 172.16.11.1:23458 = `ca 47 d5 00` + LE len(20) + 20-byte payload `66 14 roll pitch throttle yaw flags1 flags2 00x10 XOR(bytes2..17) 99`; sticks 0..255 centered 128; flags1 takeoff 0x01/estop 0x02/calibrate 0x04/flip 0x08/land 0x80; flags2 tilt up 0x08/down 0x10; separate 1 Hz keepalive `ca 47 d1 00 00 00 00 00`; one-shot flags are cleared after a single packet. (https://github.com/marshallrichards/turbodrone/blob/2c09cf8/backend/protocols/x69_lg_rc_protocol_adapter.py)
- [high] FlightController sends one packet per tick from a daemon thread at CONTROL_RATE (25 Hz default for x69_lg), consuming normalized axes in [-1,1] via set_axes_from(source,...); send exceptions are swallowed; the X69 adapter never receives data, so there is no battery/altitude telemetry. (https://github.com/marshallrichards/turbodrone/blob/2c09cf8/backend/services/flight_controller.py)
- [high] Plugin contract: subclass plugins.base.Plugin, implement _on_start (spawn own thread), read VideoFrame JPEGs from the frame_source iterator (shared DroppingQueue maxsize 100, 0.2 s poll), command via self.fc.set_axes_from, overlay via send_overlay(list of {type:'rect',coords,color}); PluginManager auto-discovers subclasses by class name and allows one running plugin; while a plugin runs frontend axes are ignored but takeoff/land/estop WS commands still execute. (https://github.com/marshallrichards/turbodrone/blob/2c09cf8/backend/plugins/manager.py)
- [high] Default X69 video mode is RTSP: OpenCV opens rtsp://172.16.11.1/live/ch00_1 over TCP (HEVC 1280x720) and re-encodes every frame to JPEG q=85 into a maxsize-2 queue; h265 mode instead reassembles UDP 1234 (32-byte c6 6c a5 5a header) and pipes through ffmpeg to 640px/15fps MJPEG; all modes deliver JPEG bytes to /mjpeg and plugins. (https://github.com/marshallrichards/turbodrone/blob/2c09cf8/backend/protocols/x69_lg_rtsp_video_protocol.py)
- [high] FollowPlugin = ultralytics YOLOv10n (person class only, imgsz 320, conf 0.65, 20 fps cap) + bang-bang FollowController: yaw +/-20% when |center_x-0.5|>0.15, pitch +20% when box width<0.28, -20% when >0.82, throttle/roll always 0, outputs divided by 100 under DirectStrategy with expo 0; lost target -> zero axes (hover), no timeout or search. (https://github.com/marshallrichards/turbodrone/blob/2c09cf8/backend/plugins/follow/follow_plugin.py)
- [high] The 'debug' drone type is not a simulator: DebugRcProtocolAdapter only defines send_control_data (no build_control_packet/send_control_packet), DebugRcModel.update is a no-op, and video comes from the local webcam; a digital twin needs either a new model+adapter pair or a network emulator that speaks the X69 UDP/RTSP protocol. (https://github.com/marshallrichards/turbodrone/blob/2c09cf8/backend/protocols/debug_rc_protocol_adapter.py)
- [high] Frontend WS schema: {type:'axes', mode:'inc'|'abs'|'mouse', camera_tilt_direction:-1|0|1, throttle,yaw,pitch,roll} sent at 30 Hz to ws://localhost:8000/ws; one-shots {type:'takeoff'|'land'|'estop'|'set_speed_index'}; backend also accepts set_profile, set_camera_tilt, emergency_stop; overlays arrive on /ws/overlays as a JSON array of rects; backend URL hard-coded to localhost:8000 in 7 files. (https://github.com/marshallrichards/turbodrone/blob/2c09cf8/frontend/src/hooks/useControls.ts)
- [high] Only the estop (0x02) and camera-tilt bits were confirmed from the decompiled com.lg.drone app; takeoff/land/calibrate/flip bits were recovered from heavily duplicated/obfuscated handlers and the doc explicitly recommends packet captures before trusting them. (https://github.com/marshallrichards/turbodrone/blob/2c09cf8/docs/research/x69_lg.md)
- [high] Repo is Apache-2.0 with a NOTICE file that must be retained in derivatives; last commit 2026-06-22 (2c09cf8), 580 stars, 6 open issues/PRs, none about X69; maintainer states (issue #21) there are no API docs, web_server.py is the integration point, and the camera is the only sensor. (https://github.com/marshallrichards/turbodrone/issues/21)
- [high] uv resolves backend/requirements.txt on Apple Silicon for both Python 3.13 and 3.15 to torch 2.15.0, torchvision 0.29.2, ultralytics 8.4.202, opencv-python 5.0.0.93, numpy 2.5.4, fastapi 0.162.0, uvicorn 0.54.0; ultralytics is licensed AGPL-3.0, so keeping FollowPlugin's import pulls AGPL into the derived project. (https://pypi.org/project/ultralytics/)
- [high] Hosted SAM 3.1 on the Meta Model API: model 'sam-3.1' at https://api.meta.ai/v1/responses (OpenAI-SDK compatible), images as URL/base64 data URL, video only as whole MP4/MOV clips up to 15,000 frames with a 50-frame billing minimum, $2.50 per 1,000 images, $0.20 per 1,000 video frames, 3,000 RPM; there is no incremental/live frame-tracking endpoint. (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/sam/segmenting)
- [high] SAM 3.1 API output is special-token text: boxes in source pixels `<|box;x1;y1;x2;y2;w;h[;c]|>`, per-object masks as base85 `<|mask;...;data=H,W,~payload|>`, integer object IDs stable within one response (may break on occlusion/exit), one `response.output_text.delta` per frame. (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/sam/reading-segmentation)
- [high] Local SAM 3 officially requires Python>=3.12, PyTorch>=2.7 and a CUDA 12.6 GPU; the MPS/CPU PR #627 is still open (not merged as of 2026-10-25); the community sam3-mac shim runs text-prompted image inference at ~3 s/image on CPU (M4 Pro, MPS slower) and does not support video tracking. (https://github.com/benreichman/sam3-mac)
- [high] Meta Model API pricing for the voice/LLM side: muse-spark-1.3 $1.25/M input, $4.25/M output tokens (3,000 RPM / 4M TPM); muse-voice-transcribe-1.0 $0.18 per audio hour with 256 concurrent streams; Muse Glimmer is not listed on the pricing page. (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/pricing-rate-limits)
- [medium] The X69's companion iOS app 'X69 DRONE' (developer 秋菊 莫 / 东驰智创, v1.0.1 Feb 2025) connects to the drone's own Wi-Fi AP; reviewers report connection difficulties; the Wi-Fi band and battery capacity are not published on any page I could reach (Amazon/manual/FCC pages blocked), third-party listings claim a 2K camera, ~26 min total with two batteries, under 250 g. (https://apps.apple.com/us/app/x69-drone/id6738834352)

VERIFIER CORRECTIONS (non-confirmed claims):
- [partially_correct] 3. Plugin contract: subclass plugins.base.Plugin, implement _on_start, read VideoFrame JPEGs from frame_source iterator (shared DroppingQueue maxsize 100, 0.2 s poll), command via self.fc.set_axes_from, overlay via send_overlay(list of rects); PluginManager auto-discovers subclasses by class name and allows one running plugin; while a plugin runs frontend axes are ignored but takeoff/land/estop still execute -> All behaviors confirmed, but the single-running-plugin rule is NOT in PluginManager: manager.start() only rejects a duplicate of the same name (L50-53) and its own comment says 'Current architecture can technically run multiple plugins'. The 409 'Another plugin is already running' guard lives in web_server.py start_plugin (L572-577), so code that calls plugin_manager.start() directly can run several plugins competing for the same queue. The '0.2 s poll' is a blocking Queue.get(timeout=0.2) (manager.py L68-73), i.e. frames are yielded immediately on arrival, not every 200 ms. Confirmed: discovery via pkgutil.walk_packages + issubclass registry keyed by obj.__name__ (L122-135); PLUGIN_FRAME_Q = DroppingQueue(maxsize=100) (web_server.py L475); /ws 'axes' ignored when plugin_manager.running() is truthy (L632-636) while takeoff/land/estop/set_profile/set_speed_index/camera tilt still dispatch (L656-688); PLUGINS_ENABLED defaults to false (L97) so /plugins returns 404 unless set. (https://github.com/marshallrichards/turbodrone/blob/2c09cf8/backend/plugins/manager.py)
- [partially_correct] 7. Frontend WS schema {type:'axes', mode:'inc'|'abs'|'mouse', camera_tilt_direction:-1|0|1, throttle,yaw,pitch,roll} at 30 Hz to ws://localhost:8000/ws; one-shots takeoff/land/estop/set_speed_index; backend also accepts set_profile, set_camera_tilt, emergency_stop; overlays on /ws/overlays as JSON array of rects; backend URL hard-coded to localhost:8000 in 7 files -> Schema, 30 Hz interval (L329-345, suppressed while a plugin is running), one-shots (L376-381) and backend handlers (web_server.py L627-688, which also accept aliases 'speed_index', 'camera_tilt') are confirmed. The count is 7 occurrences in 6 files, not 7 files: App.tsx:27, useControls.ts:28 and :44, useOverlays.ts:3, usePlugins.ts:4, lib/ws.ts:3 (unused module), pages/TestPage.tsx:14. useOverlays.ts parses any JSON array; DrawingOverlay.tsx renders only type==='rect' in a 0..1 viewBox. (https://github.com/marshallrichards/turbodrone/blob/2c09cf8/frontend/src/hooks/useControls.ts)
- [partially_correct] 12. SAM 3.1 output is special-token text: boxes in source pixels <|box;x1;y1;x2;y2;w;h[;c]|>, masks as base85 <|mask;...;data=H,W,~payload|>, integer IDs stable within one response (may break on occlusion/exit), one response.output_text.delta per frame -> Fields are key=value, not positional, and the docs say to parse by key: example '<0f>0<|box;x1=211;y1=228;x2=270;y2=254;w=320;h=334|><|mask;x=0;y=0;data=27,60,~...|>,1<|box;...|><|mask;...|>'. w/h are the SOURCE FRAME size (not box width/height); c (0-1) appears only when include_confidence='true'. Each line starts with a zero-based frame marker <Nf>, records are comma-separated 'id<box><mask>', frames with no matches emit no line and indices may skip. Mask token is data=<H>,<W>,<enc>payload with '~' lossless or '!' one_bit, payload base85. IDs are bare integers, sparse/non-dense, stable within a response but not guaranteed across entry/exit, full occlusion or very long clips. Official decoders: pip meta-sam-parser (decode_mask_to_raster/_rle/_svg_path), npm @meta-sam/parser. (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/sam/reading-segmentation)
- [partially_correct] 15. iOS app 'X69 DRONE' (developer 秋菊 莫 / 东驰智创, v1.0.1 Feb 2025) connects to the drone's own Wi-Fi AP; reviewers report connection difficulties; Wi-Fi band and battery capacity not published on any reachable page; third-party listings claim 2K camera, ~26 min with two batteries, under 250 g -> App Store confirms: 'X69 DRONE', seller 秋菊 莫, v1.0.1 dated 02/18/2025, 31 ratings avg 3.2, 'By connecting to the toy WiFi, the image and flight control data returned by the UAV are obtained', UDP control, reviews citing many restarts to connect. 东驰智创 is only the copyright line ('© CopyRight @2024 东驰智创'), not the developer. The Android com.lg.drone app is by 杨亚林, v1.0.15 dated Aug 9, 2026 (APKPure) - newer than iOS and the version the turbodrone decompile targets. Battery capacity IS published by the vendor: loileydrone.com sells a '3.7V 1000mAh LiPo Battery for X69'. Wi-Fi band remains unconfirmed: only a single Amazon review surfaced in search says 2.4 GHz (no 5 GHz claim found); Amazon returned a 'Fallback Detail Page' without specs and manuals.plus returned 403. 2K camera / ~26 min with two batteries / <250 g match listing and manual snippets (marketing claims; reviews report 10-12 min per battery). (https://apps.apple.com/us/app/x69-drone/id6738834352)

VERIFIER MISSED FINDINGS:
- Network topology blocker (inferred from App Store 'connect to the toy WiFi' + adapter IP 172.16.11.1): controlling the X69 means the MacBook joins the drone's own AP with its single Wi-Fi radio, so cloud calls to api.meta.ai (sam-3.1, muse-spark-1.3, ASR) need a second uplink - USB-C Ethernet, iPhone USB tethering, or a USB Wi-Fi dongle - plus macOS service-order/route care so 172.16.11.0/24 stays on the drone interface. The digital-twin path avoids this, but the live demo does not. Also no issue/PR in the repo has ever been filed about the X69; the only field report of control dropouts on macOS (issue #22) is for a different drone.
- Meta realtime ASR is a raw WebSocket, not an SDK call: wss://api.meta.ai/v1/asr/realtime, first JSON text frame within 10 s carries authorization.accessToken (the HTTP Authorization header is ignored), model muse-voice-transcribe-1.0, audio as raw binary frames of 16-bit LE mono PCM at 24 kHz (PCM_24KHZ, preferred) or 16 kHz, paced near real time (~80 ms chunks; below-real-time ingress or backlog closes with code 1008, 1013 = rate limited), modes PUSH_TO_TALK (default) / ENDPOINTING / DIARIZATION fixed per session, 'transcript' events carry a final flag, partialMode CUMULATIVE|DELTA, keywords list for vocabulary biasing, {"type":"endStream"} to finish, sessions <=60 min, 25 languages. Batch alternative: POST https://api.meta.ai/v1/asr/transcribe multipart WAV 16/24 kHz mono, <=32 MB, <=10 min. Both share the 256-concurrent / 16,000-per-hour limit at $0.18 per processed hour (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/speech-to-text, /docs/api-reference/voice/realtime).
- Model-family facts for the LLM stage (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/models, /docs/muse-glimmer): Muse Glimmer is a 30B dense multimodal (text+image in, text out) OPEN-WEIGHT model under Apache-2.0, 128K context, served via vLLM/SGLang/llama.cpp/Ollama/LM Studio - bf16 weights are ~80 GB, so on a 48 GB M5 Pro only a <=4-bit llama.cpp/Ollama quant is realistic, and it has no hosted model id. Hosted muse-spark-1.3 is multimodal (text, image, video, audio, PDF in) with a 1,048,576-token context and supports reasoning effort levels, so it can double as a coarse scene-describer/intent parser from a JPEG frame; muse-wonder-1.0 is preview-only. The API exposes three protocols at https://api.meta.ai/v1: Responses, Chat Completions, and Anthropic-style Messages, plus tool calling and structured output pages.
- Official SAM output tooling and knobs the plan should use rather than hand-rolling a parser: pip install meta-sam-parser (Python >=3.10, zero deps: decode_mask_to_raster / decode_mask_to_rle / decode_mask_to_svg_path, build_object_prompt) and npm @meta-sam/parser (+ @meta-sam/graphics SegmentationRenderer, @meta-sam/video, @meta-sam/react; ESM-only, Node ^20.17 or >=22.9). Request knobs: metadata.mask_encoding='one_bit' for smaller payloads, include_confidence='true', metadata.propagation_direction for box/point prompts, box/point prompts let the client choose object ids (useful for re-acquiring a tracked target from the local tracker's last bbox), video capped at 16 tracked objects per frame, clips reusable via POST /v1/files (purpose user_data). Cost math from the published prices: a 1 Hz single-image re-acquisition loop is ~$9/hour, 2 Hz ~$18/hour; 3,000 RPM is not the constraint, unpublished per-request latency is (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/sam/client-libraries).
- Control-loop safety/UX gotchas in turbodrone that a voice-follow plugin inherits: (a) no deadman - FlightController re-sends the last axes forever, so a plugin thread that dies after commanding pitch +20 leaves the drone flying forward (follow_plugin.py has no finally/zeroing; manager.stop() does not zero axes either); (b) the frontend's maybeStopPluginOnUserInput (useControls.ts L356-374) POSTs /plugins/{name}/stop for ALL running plugins on any WASD/arrow/PageUp/gamepad/mouse input, and suppresses axes TX while a plugin runs - stray keypresses will kill an autonomous plugin; (c) PLUGIN_FRAME_Q is a DroppingQueue(100) fed by put_nowait (drops oldest), so a consumer slower than the ~15-25 fps feed reads frames up to ~100 deep (~4-7 s stale) unless it drains to the newest frame itself; (d) FramePump publishes None after a 3 s stall, closing /mjpeg clients, and VideoReceiverService waits 5 s before recreating a failed adapter; (e) estop in the X69 protocol is a one-shot 0x02 flag for a single 40 ms packet (adapter clears it after one build), and web_server's 'estop' handler catches AttributeError, so a twin model must implement emergency_stop/set_speed_index/set_camera_tilt_state/strategy to behave like X69LgRcModel.
- Local-SAM-on-Mac status is worse than 'one open PR': facebookresearch/sam3 has at least six unmerged MPS/CPU PRs (#173, #258, #264, #400, #482, #627; last activity 2026-10-25) and three closed-unmerged ones, the SAM 3.1 checkpoints (RELEASE_SAM3p1.md, March 27, 2026, 'Object Multiplex') need the latest repo code, Ultralytics' sam3.1_multiplex.pt is image-only and all SAM weights are HF-gated, and sam3-mac pins SAM 3 (not 3.1) at ~3 s/image CPU with numpy<2 (conflicts with turbodrone's resolved numpy 2.5.4 - needs a separate venv/process). Dependency note for the Mac toolchain: torch >=2.12 only ships macosx_14_0_arm64 wheels (fine on this macOS 26 host) and torch 2.15.0 is the first release with cp315 wheels; Python 3.15 is also the Homebrew default python3 on this machine, while ultralytics 8.4.202 (AGPL-3.0, published 2026-11-03) declares no torch upper bound. Repo unit tests are stdlib unittest (python -m unittest tests.test_x69_lg_protocol ...) with no pytest config; I was also blocked from executing them (permission classifier), so they remain unrun.

VERIFIER NOTES: Method: every codebase claim was checked against the read-only clone at scratchpad/ref/turbodrone (HEAD 2c09cf8, identical to GitHub master via gh api) by reading the cited files and line ranges; repo metadata, issues #21/#22, PR #35 and facebookresearch/sam3 PR #627 were pulled through the GitHub API; web claims were checked on dev.meta.ai (sam/segmenting, sam/overview, sam/reading-segmentation, sam/client-libraries, pricing-rate-limits, models, muse-glimmer, muse-glimmer/hardware-platforms, speech-to-text, api-reference/voice/realtime, media-segmentation, docs index), github.com/facebookresearch/sam3 (+ RELEASE_SAM3p1.md), github.com/benreichman/sam3-mac, docs.ultralytics.com/models/sam-3, pypi.org (ultralytics, torch incl. per-version wheel tags via JSON API), apps.apple.com, apkpure.com, loileydrone.com, and amazon.com (returned a 'Fallback Detail Page' with no specs; manuals.plus returned 403). The uv resolution was re-run locally with uv 0.13.1. Result: 11 claims confirmed, 4 partially correct (3, 7, 12, 15) with only precision-level corrections, 0 refuted; no hallucinated model names, hosts, prices or endpoints were found. The most consequential corrections for a planner are the SAM box-token semantics (key=value fields; w/h are the source frame size, not box size), that single-plugin enforcement lives in web_server.py rather than PluginManager, and that Muse Glimmer is self-host-only open weights. Limits: the session's WebSearch budget was exhausted after four searches, so X69 Wi-Fi band (2.4 GHz per one review) and real-world battery life rest on secondary snippets; I could not execute the repo's unit tests (sandbox denied running external code) and did not reach the Amazon spec table or an FCC filing. All dev.meta.ai pages loaded without login (an identity-confirmation modal overlays but does not block content).

RECOMMENDATIONS:
- Fork turbodrone at 2c09cf8 as a vendored dependency (keep LICENSE + NOTICE block verbatim, add your own NOTICE lines), run the backend with uv on Python 3.13 (not 3.15) and pin the resolved set (torch 2.15.0, opencv-python 5.0.0.93, fastapi 0.162.0, uvicorn 0.54.0); make ultralytics an optional extra and do not import it in your new plugins to avoid AGPL entanglement.
- Digital twin, two layers: (1) in-process `DRONE_TYPE=x69_sim` = SimRcModel (reuse X69LgRcModel) + SimRcProtocolAdapter whose send_control_packet decodes the exact 28-byte X69 packet (byte parsing from x69_lg_rc_protocol_adapter.py L67-80) and feeds a kinematic plant, plus SimVideoProtocolAdapter that renders the camera view into JPEG VideoFrames (fast iteration, no HEVC); (2) network emulator `sim/x69_emulator.py` bound to a loopback alias 172.16.11.1 (UDP 23458 control parser + keepalive, UDP 23459 a8 8a open/close/iframe acks, RTSP /live/ch00_1 served via mediamtx or ffmpeg libx265 from the renderer) so the unmodified x69_lg stack and web UI run end-to-end. Plant model: stick deflection -> body velocity/yaw-rate with first-order lag, position hold at center stick (optical flow), takeoff climbs to ~1.2 m, land descends, estop 0x02 cuts motors; calibrate lag/gain from real flight video later.
- Scene for the twin: render a person in a blue shirt / a green car as textured billboards or simple 3D meshes (OpenCV/pyrender/pygame, or Blender-exported sprites) at 1280x720 so SAM 3.1 text prompts genuinely detect them; include moving targets, occlusion, and target exit to exercise lost-target logic.
- SAM tracking plugin: new `plugins/sam_follow/SamFollowPlugin(Plugin)` mirroring FollowPlugin's lifecycle (DirectStrategy, expo 0, restore on stop) but two-rate: (a) semantic acquisition via hosted sam-3.1 image requests (input_text = LLM-extracted noun phrase, input_image = base64 JPEG downscaled to 640 px, metadata.include_confidence='true', parse <|box|> tokens) at 1-2 Hz; (b) local tracker between detections (OpenCV CSRT/KCF or template/optical-flow seeded from the SAM box/mask) at 10-15 Hz; re-acquire with SAM when tracker confidence drops or every N s. Replace bang-bang with proportional control: yaw = Kp_yaw*(cx-0.5), throttle = Kp_thr*(0.45-cy), pitch from box width vs setpoint band, each clamped to |axis| <= 0.35 with slew limiting and deadbands; keep roll 0. Drain the frame iterator to the newest frame (or set the plugin queue maxsize to 1 in your fork) to avoid the 100-frame backlog.
- Extend overlays for masks: add overlay types {type:'polygon', points:[...]} or {type:'mask_png', data:base64} in OverlayBroadcaster consumers and DrawingOverlay.tsx; add a `/state` JSON endpoint (last_control_source, plugin running, target phrase, lock status, time-in-flight) so the LLM/voice layer and UI can introspect.
- LLM/voice layer as a separate `agent/` process: mic -> muse-voice-transcribe-1.0 (streaming) -> muse-spark (or your fine-tuned open Muse model) with a strict JSON tool schema {action: follow|stop|hover|land|takeoff|tilt, target_phrase, speed}; it drives turbodrone through existing REST (POST /plugins/SamFollowPlugin/start|stop, GET /plugins) plus a new WS message type `set_target {phrase}` (or POST /plugins/{name}/config). Keep a local keyword kill-phrase ('stop'/'land') that bypasses the LLM round-trip.
- Safety interlocks at the FlightController choke point: wrap set_axes_from with a SafetyGovernor that (1) clamps per-source axis limits (plugins <= 0.35, voice-issued <= 0.5), (2) rate-limits axis slew, (3) zeroes axes if the active plugin has not called set_axes_from within 500 ms (heartbeat watchdog), (4) hovers on video stall > 1 s and lands on > 3 s, (5) enforces a flight timer with auto-land (X69 ~10-13 min per battery; no telemetry available) and a session geofence-by-time per command, (6) repeats one-shot takeoff/land flags for 3-5 packets instead of one, and (7) reserves estop (0x02 = motor cut, drone falls) for an explicit human action, never an LLM decision; map voice 'stop' to hover and 'land' to 0x80.
- Hardware verification before autonomy: Wireshark the stock X69 app for takeoff/land/flip bits (docs/research/x69_lg.md says only estop and tilt bits are confirmed), measure RTSP glass-to-glass latency and SAM 3.1 API round trip from the Mac, and confirm flight on macOS (issue #22 reports macOS control dropouts on another drone family). Use RTSP (TCP) mode to avoid macOS firewall/inbound-UDP problems.
- Networking: the X69 AP has no internet, so cloud Meta calls while flying need a second interface (USB Wi-Fi dongle such as ALFA AWUS036ACM, Ethernet, or iPhone USB tethering) and a routing rule that keeps 172.16.11.0/24 on the drone link; design the twin to exercise the same dual-network path.
- Run the upstream unit tests under Python 3.13 from backend/ (python -m unittest tests.test_x69_lg_protocol tests.test_x69_lg_video_protocol tests.test_x69_lg_video_mode tests.test_x69_lg_jpeg_video_protocol) and add emulator round-trip tests (packet -> emulator -> decoded sticks) in the same unittest style.

RISKS:
- Single Wi-Fi radio: connecting the Mac to the X69 AP drops internet, breaking cloud SAM 3.1 / Muse calls unless a second network interface or tethering is used (README recommends an ALFA dongle).
- Latency budget: RTSP HEVC decode + JPEG re-encode + cloud SAM round-trip cannot sustain a >=5-10 Hz loop by itself; without a local inter-frame tracker the follow loop will oscillate or lag.
- No telemetry from the X69 (adapter never receives); battery and altitude interlocks must be time/vision based, and the stated battery life (~10-13 min per pack per listings/reviews, unverified) bounds every autonomous session.
- Estop bit 0x02 cuts motors mid-air (fall), and the 'Land' path (0x80) plus takeoff bits are not capture-verified per docs/research/x69_lg.md; wrong bits could trigger unintended behavior.
- One-shot flags are transmitted for exactly one 40 ms packet; a single lost UDP datagram silently drops a takeoff/land command.
- Plugin frame queue is 100 deep; a slow consumer accumulates up to ~6 s of stale frames, so a naive SAM plugin would steer on old imagery.
- Upstream is effectively dormant since June 2026 (no X69 issues, no macOS testing by the maintainer; issue #22 reports macOS dropouts), so bugs must be fixed in the fork.
- Hosted SAM 3.1 video endpoint is clip-batch only (min 50 billed frames); IDs are stable only within one response; costs scale with call rate (~$9/h at 1 Hz, ~$45/h at 5 Hz image calls).
- Local SAM 3/3.1 is CUDA-only officially; Mac paths are community shims at ~3 s/image CPU with no video tracking; gated HF weights need Meta approval lead time; SAM License forbids military/weapons use and requires deleting weights on termination.
- ultralytics is AGPL-3.0; shipping FollowPlugin as-is in a networked product carries AGPL obligations.
- macOS/Linux firewalls can block inbound UDP video (PR #35); only the RTSP/TCP mode is immune.
- Dependency drift: requirements.txt is unpinned; today it resolves to opencv-python 5.0.0.93 and torch 2.15.0, which the upstream code was never tested against.

OPEN QUESTIONS FOR USER:
- Do you have (or can you get) a second network path for the Mac while it is joined to the X69's Wi-Fi (USB Wi-Fi dongle, Ethernet, iPhone tethering)? Without it, cloud Meta calls during flight are impossible. Also, what SSID/band does the X69 broadcast (check the label/box: 2.4 GHz vs 5 GHz)?
- Is hosted Meta API inference for SAM 3.1 acceptable (cost roughly $9-45/hour depending on call rate), or must SAM run locally on the Mac (CPU shim, ~3 s/image, no video tracking)? Do you already have a dev.meta.ai API key or hackathon credits?
- Which open Meta model do you intend to fine-tune and for what (voice-intent parsing vs something else), and on what hardware/budget? That decides whether fine-tuning fits the hackathon timeline or should be prompt-only.
- Have you already flown the X69 with stock turbodrone on this Mac (takeoff, land, estop verified)? If not, the plan must budget a hardware bring-up day plus a Wireshark capture of the stock app.
- Flight environment and demo format: indoors (small space, person target) or outdoors (green car), and is the hackathon demo a live flight or recorded video from the twin plus real drone? What is the submission deadline?
- Autonomy envelope: may the autopilot command throttle (altitude) and roll, or only yaw/pitch like the existing FollowPlugin? What maximum stick deflection and maximum autonomous session length are you comfortable with?
- Digital twin fidelity target: is a lightweight Python/OpenCV renderer with textured targets enough for SAM-detectable scenes, or do you want a Blender/3D-engine render pipeline (slower to build, higher visual realism)?
- Do you want the voice loop to run fully local for safety-critical words (local keyword spotting for 'stop'/'land') while routing everything else through muse-voice-transcribe + muse-spark?



### Vision-based target following control design for a latency-heavy toy quad  [verifier reliability: high]

SUMMARY:
BASELINE (verified in the cloned repo, /private/tmp/.../scratchpad/ref/turbodrone). turbodrone's follow plugin (backend/plugins/follow/follow_controller.py, https://github.com/marshallrichards/turbodrone/blob/main/backend/plugins/follow/follow_controller.py) is bang-bang: yaw = +/-20% when |cx-0.5| > 0.15, pitch = +/-20% when box width < 0.30 or > 0.80 (+/-0.02 band), throttle/roll = 0, largest YOLOv10n person, zeros on no detection, DirectStrategy with expo 0. FlightController runs an 80 Hz loop taking normalized -1..1 axes (set_axes_from(source,...)); X69 sticks are 0..255 centred at 128; the X69 adapter scales ONLY roll/pitch by speed index (0.55/0.75/1.0, default 0.75), yaw/throttle unscaled; one-shot flags takeoff 0x01, emergency-stop 0x02 (motor cut), calibrate 0x04, flip 0x08, land 0x80; camera tilt is momentary (byte 7, 0x08 up / 0x10 down). Video: RTSP rtsp://172.16.11.1/live/ch00_1 HEVC 1280x720 via OpenCV with `rtsp_transport;tcp|fflags;nobuffer|flags;low_delay`, CAP_PROP_BUFFERSIZE=1, 2-deep queue, re-encoded to JPEG q85; docs/research/x69_lg.md records that the UDP H.265 path needed normal buffering (`-fflags +genpts`) because nobuffer broke decoding (extra latency is structural). Latency trap: plugins read a 100-deep DroppingQueue (drop-oldest) FIFO (web_server.py:475, utils/dropping_queue.py) so a consumer slower than ~15-20 fps reads frames up to ~100 frames (~6 s) stale; the controller must consume a latest-only mailbox. Plugin control surface: POST /plugins/{name}/start|stop (one plugin at a time), overlays on /ws/overlays as normalized rects; DebugVideoProtocolAdapter (webcam) shows the drop-in adapter seam a digital twin can reuse.

PRIOR ART. Tello projects converge on the same mapping: horizontal centroid error -> yaw, vertical error -> throttle, apparent size -> pitch. allureking/follow-me-drone: PID kp 0.2/ki 0.04/kd 0.005 on pixel error (960x720) at 10 Hz, yaw clamp 100, vertical clamp 40 x 0.5, forward/back +/-20 by face-area band 14000-15000 px^2, target y = 0.25 of height, lost after 15 frames -> rotate at 40 toward last-seen side (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/allureking/follow-me-drone/main/config/default.yaml). geaxgx/tello-openpose: yaw P 0.25 on x-offset px (+/-100), throttle P 0.4 (-80..100), pitch PID 0.5/0.04/0.3 on (shoulder_width - keep_distance) px (+/-50), hold last command 1 s then search-rotate at 50 in last rotation direction; README warns a PID tuned at 20 fps oscillates at 10 fps (https://github.com/geaxgx/tello-openpose). PX4 Follow-Me filters target position/velocity with a 2nd-order model (wn=1.0 rad/s, zeta=0.7071), resets after 1.5 s without updates, no yaw control under 1 m, 1 m minimum safety altitude, FLW_TGT_RS 0.1, FLW_TGT_MAX_VEL 5 m/s, FLW_TGT_DST min 1 m (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/PX4/PX4-Autopilot/main/src/modules/flight_mode_manager/tasks/AutoFollowTarget/FlightTaskAutoFollowTarget.hpp). ArduPilot AP_Follow dead-reckons the target forward: delta_pos = vel*dt + 0.5*accel*dt^2, accel/jerk-shaped (5 m/s^2, 10 m/s^3), FOLL_POS_P 0.1, holds position after FOLL_TIMEOUT 3 s or beyond FOLL_DIST_MAX 100 m (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ArduPilot/ardupilot/master/libraries/AP_Follow/AP_Follow.cpp). DJI ActiveTrack exposes the lifecycle to copy: CannotConfirm (UnstableTarget, TargetTooFar/TooClose, AircraftTooHigh/Low, BlockedByObstacle), WaitingForConfirmation (aircraft hovers while camera tracks, follows after confirmation), TrackingWithHighConfidence, TrackingWithLowConfidence (confirmation may be re-requested or lock lost); Spotlight = yaw-only (https://developer.dji.com/api-reference/ios-api/Components/Missions/DJIActiveTrackTrackingState.html). A 2025 camera-latency benchmark for IBVS quadrotor tracking uses 160 ms delay at 30 Hz and compensates with delayed-KF "rewind to exposure, correct, replay" rather than a Smith predictor; metrics are settling time, final error, min separation, RMSE (https://github.com/abbassnasrallah089-sketch/quadrotor-camera-latency-benchmark).

THEORY THAT SETS THE GAINS. IBVS point interaction matrix: x_dot = -(f/Z)vx + (x/Z)vz + (xy/f)wx - ((f^2+x^2)/f)wy + y*wz, y_dot symmetric (https://dellaert.github.io/19F-4476/Slides/Hutchinson-VisualServoing.pdf): yaw and pitch rates move the feature by ~f px per rad (f ~ 830 px for 720p/75 deg HFOV: a 10 deg forward pitch shifts the target ~145 px = 20% of frame height), and translation sensitivity scales with 1/Z. Consequence for an IMU-less toy: vertical error is contaminated by the pitch needed to move forward, size is the cleanest range cue, and gains must scale with distance. SIMC (https://skoge.folk.ntnu.no/vgprosessregulering/papers-pensum/PIDbook-chapter5.pdf): for an integrating-plus-delay plant (bearing error integrates yaw rate) with tau_c = theta, Kc*k' = 0.5/theta, tau_I = 8*theta, gain crossover wc*theta = 0.5, GM 2.96, PM 46.9 deg, tolerates +59% delay error; add tau_D ~ theta/3 at most; measurement filter tau_F <= theta/2. My simulation (scratchpad/sim/delay_loop2.py; 300 ms video + 75 ms command + 50 ms half-period + 250 ms attitude lag = theta_eff 0.675 s, 10 Hz ZOH) confirms: Kp*K = 0.5/theta_eff = 0.74 rad/s per rad gives 4% overshoot, 4.1 s settle; 1.0/theta_eff gives 39% overshoot; a Smith-style own-motion predictor allowed Kp*K 1.2-1.6 with <=3% overshoot and ~2.2 s settle, and stayed stable with a 30% delay-model error (13% overshoot). P-only steady lag for a target bearing rate r is r/(Kp*K): 0.3 rad/s -> 0.4 rad at 0.74, i.e., a walking person at 3 m will leave a 75 deg FOV without velocity feed-forward.

PERCEPTION BUDGET. Meta Model API `sam-3.1` is request/response (POST https://api.meta.ai/v1/responses, OpenAI-compatible): images by URL/base64, video by URL/base64/file upload, <=15,000 frames per request, 50-frame billing minimum, <=16 box/point-prompted objects, metadata.propagation_direction, include_confidence "true", mask_encoding one_bit; $2.50/1k images, $0.20/1k frames, 3,000 RPM; no latency figures and no persistent streaming session, and ids are not guaranteed across exit/full occlusion (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/sam/segmenting, https://dev.meta.ai/docs/sam/reading-segmentation, https://dev.meta.ai/docs/pricing-rate-limits). So hosted SAM 3.1 is an acquisition/re-ID oracle, not the 10-20 Hz tracker. Meta reports 30 ms/image on H200 and SAM 3.1 at 32 fps on H100 for medium object counts (https://ai.meta.com/blog/segment-anything-model-3/). Locally, HF transformers has a true streaming API (model(inference_session, frame=pixel_values[0])) with defaults recondition_every_nth_frame 16, max_trk_keep_alive 30, score 0.5/new-det 0.7, 1008 px input, but streaming disables hotstart de-duplication (https://huggingface.co/docs/transformers/en/model_doc/sam3_video); official facebookresearch/sam3 requires CUDA 12.6 and the Apple-MPS path is an unmerged PR reporting ~1.3 fps at 720p and ~7 GB on an M5 (https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam3/pull/627). Fast in-loop trackers that run on the Mac: OpenCV VitTrack at 1.46 ms/frame on Apple M2 (4 threads, ONNX Runtime) with a tracking score that collapses on loss (default threshold 0.20) (https://github.com/opencv/opencv_zoo/tree/main/models/object_tracking_vittrack), and Meta-authored EfficientTAM (Apache-2.0, MPS supported since Jan 2025, >10 fps on iPhone 15, SAM2-style predictor API) (https://github.com/yformer/EfficientTAM). Meta also sells muse-voice-transcribe-1.0 ($0.18/h, streaming) and muse-spark-1.3 ($1.25/$4.25 per 1M tokens, 3,000 RPM) for the voice/intent stages. Hardware anecdotes (unverified on the X69 itself): 2.4 GHz app link flaky, video dropouts at ~20 yards, optical-flow drift over featureless floors, 10-13 min per battery; the Amazon listing confirms altitude hold, speed adjustment, headless mode, 2 batteries, 2K camera (https://www.amazon.com/dp/B0F8QTBGNP).

KEY CLAIMS:
- [high] turbodrone's shipped follow controller is bang-bang (yaw +/-20% outside a 0.15 centre deadzone; pitch +/-20% when normalized box width < 0.30 or > 0.80), sends zeros on no detection, and uses DirectStrategy with expo 0 at FOLLOW_FPS=20; it has no latency compensation, filtering, or lost-target logic. (https://github.com/marshallrichards/turbodrone/blob/main/backend/plugins/follow/follow_controller.py)
- [high] X69 control path: FlightController loop at 80 Hz takes normalized -1..1 axes; X69 sticks are 0..255 centred at 128; the adapter scales only roll/pitch by speed index (0.55/0.75/1.0, default index 1 = 0.75) while yaw/throttle are unscaled; flags: takeoff 0x01, emergency stop 0x02, land 0x80; camera tilt is momentary bits 0x08/0x10 in byte 7; packets go to UDP 172.16.11.1:23458 with a 1 Hz D1 keepalive. (https://github.com/marshallrichards/turbodrone/blob/main/backend/protocols/x69_lg_rc_protocol_adapter.py)
- [high] Plugins consume frames from a 100-deep drop-oldest DroppingQueue in FIFO order (PLUGIN_FRAME_Q = DroppingQueue(maxsize=100)), so any consumer slower than the camera reads frames up to ~100 frames (~5-7 s at 15-20 fps) stale; a latest-only mailbox is required for closed-loop control. (https://github.com/marshallrichards/turbodrone/blob/main/backend/web_server.py)
- [high] Hosted SAM 3.1 (model id sam-3.1) is a request/response Responses API at https://api.meta.ai/v1: image or video input (<=15,000 frames per request, 50-frame billing minimum), <=16 box/point-prompted objects, metadata.propagation_direction forward/backward/both, include_confidence 'true' adds c=0..1, no latency figures published and no persistent streaming session. (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/sam/segmenting)
- [high] SAM 3.1 pricing is $2.50 per 1,000 images and $0.20 per 1,000 video frames with a 3,000 RPM per-team limit; muse-voice-transcribe-1.0 costs $0.18/hour (streaming, 256 concurrent streams) and muse-spark-1.3 costs $1.25 input / $4.25 output per 1M tokens at 3,000 RPM / 4M TPM. (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/pricing-rate-limits)
- [high] Hosted SAM 3.1 object ids are stable only while an object stays in frame; the docs say not to rely on them across an object's entry, exit, or full occlusion, and frames with no visible object emit no line (frame indices can skip). (https://dev.meta.ai/docs/sam/reading-segmentation)
- [high] HF transformers Sam3VideoModel supports frame-by-frame streaming with a text prompt (model(inference_session=..., frame=pixel_values[0])), but streaming disables the hotstart heuristics that remove unmatched/duplicate objects; defaults: score_threshold_detection 0.5, new_det_thresh 0.7, recondition_every_nth_frame 16, max_trk_keep_alive 30, 1008 px input (560 px custom degrades accuracy). (https://huggingface.co/docs/transformers/en/model_doc/sam3_video)
- [medium] Official facebookresearch/sam3 (848M params) requires a CUDA 12.6 GPU; Apple-Silicon support is an unmerged community PR whose author measured SAM 3.1 tracking 720p video at ~1.3 fps with ~7 GB peak on an M5 (16 GB), with CPU ~50x slower. (https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam3/pull/627)
- [high] Meta reports SAM 3 at 30 ms per image on an H200 with 100+ objects, near-real-time video for ~5 concurrent objects, and SAM 3.1 (released 2026-03-27) raising video throughput from 16 to 32 fps on a single H100 for medium object counts. (https://ai.meta.com/blog/segment-anything-model-3/)
- [high] OpenCV VitTrack (opencv_zoo object_tracking_vittrack, Apache-2.0) runs at 1.46 ms/frame on an Apple M2 with 4 threads (ONNX Runtime; nanotrack 4.49 ms) and exposes a tracking score that 'drops significantly' when the target is lost; cv2.TrackerVit.create takes tracking_score_threshold (default 0.20) and getTrackingScore(). (https://github.com/opencv/opencv_zoo/tree/main/models/object_tracking_vittrack)
- [high] EfficientTAM (Meta-authored 'Efficient Track Anything', Apache-2.0, repo yformer/EfficientTAM) runs >10 fps on an iPhone 15, is ~2x faster than SAM 2 on A100 with ~2.4x fewer parameters, added Apple MPS support on Jan 5 2025, and exposes a SAM2-style build_efficienttam_video_predictor API usable as the fast local mask tracker between SAM 3.1 re-detections. (https://github.com/yformer/EfficientTAM)
- [high] SIMC tuning for an integrating-plus-delay loop with tau_c = theta gives Kc*k' = 0.5/theta, tau_I = 8*theta, gain crossover wc*theta = 0.5, GM 2.96, PM 46.9 deg, Ms 1.70 and tolerates a +59% time-delay error; for first-order-plus-delay Kc = (1/k)*tau1/(tau_c+theta), tau_I = min(tau1, 4(tau_c+theta)). (https://skoge.folk.ntnu.no/vgprosessregulering/papers-pensum/PIDbook-chapter5.pdf)
- [high] IBVS point-feature interaction matrix: x_dot = -(f/Z)vx + (x/Z)vz + (xy/f)wx - ((f^2+x^2)/f)wy + y*wz (and symmetric y_dot), so yaw/pitch rates move the feature by ~f pixels per radian and translation sensitivity scales with 1/Z; depth Z (or an estimate) is required. (https://dellaert.github.io/19F-4476/Slides/Hutchinson-VisualServoing.pdf)
- [high] PX4 Follow-Me filters target position/velocity with a 2nd-order model (wn = 1.0 rad/s, zeta = 0.7071), resets the estimator after 1.5 s without updates, does no yaw control within 1 m, enforces a 1 m minimum safety altitude, freezes orientation tracking below 1 m/s target speed, and defaults FLW_TGT_RS 0.1 / FLW_TGT_MAX_VEL 5 m/s / FLW_TGT_DST 8 m (min 1 m); PX4's manual-control-loss timeout COM_RC_LOSS_T defaults to 0.5 s. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/PX4/PX4-Autopilot/main/src/modules/flight_mode_manager/tasks/AutoFollowTarget/FlightTaskAutoFollowTarget.hpp)
- [high] Tello follow projects use centroid-x -> yaw, centroid-y -> throttle, size -> pitch with low P gains on pixel error: tello-openpose uses yaw P 0.25, throttle P 0.4, pitch PID 0.5/0.04/0.3 on shoulder width, holds the last command 1 s after losing the body then search-rotates at speed 50 toward the last rotation direction, and warns a PID tuned at 20 fps oscillates at 10 fps; allureking/follow-me-drone uses kp 0.2/ki 0.04/kd 0.005 at 10 Hz with a 14000-15000 px^2 face-area band and rotates at 40 after 15 lost frames. (https://github.com/geaxgx/tello-openpose)

VERIFIER CORRECTIONS (non-confirmed claims):
- [partially_correct] 2. X69 path: FlightController loop at 80 Hz with normalized -1..1 axes; sticks 0..255 centred 128; adapter scales only roll/pitch by speed index 0.55/0.75/1.0 (default 0.75); flags takeoff 0x01, estop 0x02, land 0x80; tilt bits 0x08/0x10 in byte 7; UDP 172.16.11.1:23458; 1 Hz D1 keepalive -> Everything matches the code EXCEPT the loop rate: 80 Hz is only the FlightController class default and the s2x/wifi_uav/debug branches. For drone_type x69_lg, web_server.py:364-368 sets default_control_rate = 25.0, overridable by env CONTROL_RATE (web_server.py:464). So the stock X69 RC packet rate is 25 Hz (40 ms command quantization) unless CONTROL_RATE is set. Other verified details: StickRange(0,128,255); _scale_axis applies to packet[2] roll and packet[3] pitch only, fallback scale 0.5 for unknown speed_index; FLAG_CALIBRATE 0x04, FLAG_FLIP 0x08 in byte 6; byte 7 = 0x08 while camera_tilt_state==2 (up), 0x10 while ==1 (down) and is re-sent every packet until the client sends direction 0, so 'momentary' depends on the frontend; one-shot flags are cleared after a single packet; XOR checksum over bytes 2..17, wrapper magic ca 47 d5 00 + LE length; D1 keepalive b'\xca\x47\xd1\x00\x00\x00\x00\x00' every 1.0 s to the same port. (https://github.com/marshallrichards/turbodrone/blob/main/backend/protocols/x69_lg_rc_protocol_adapter.py)
- [partially_correct] 12. SIMC integrating-plus-delay with tau_c = theta: Kc*k' = 0.5/theta, tau_I = 8*theta, wc*theta = 0.5, GM 2.96, PM 46.9 deg, Ms 1.70, tolerates +59% time-delay error; FOPDT: Kc = (1/k)*tau1/(tau_c+theta), tau_I = min(tau1, 4(tau_c+theta)) -> Table 5.1/5.2 confirm Kc = (1/k')*1/(tau_c+theta), tau_I = 4(tau_c+theta) -> 0.5/theta and 8*theta at tau_c = theta; GM 2.96, PM 46.9 deg, Ms 1.70, Mt 1.30, wc*theta = 0.51 (0.50 is the first-order column); FOPDT rule as stated. The delay tolerance is misread: the chapter says the 'maximum allowed time delay error is Delta-theta = 1.59 theta' (Delta-theta/theta = PM[rad]/(wc*theta) = 0.819/0.51), i.e. +159%, instability when the delay grows to ~2.59 theta (first-order column: 2.14, unstable at 3.14 theta). Also, the summary's 'tau_D ~ theta/3' and 'tau_F <= theta/2' are not in this chapter: theta/3 there is the improved-SIMC integral-time correction (replace tau1 by tau1 + theta/3); the chapter sets tau_D = tau2 (none for first-order/integrating) and says nothing about a measurement filter. (https://skoge.folk.ntnu.no/vgprosessregulering/papers-pensum/PIDbook-chapter5.pdf)

VERIFIER MISSED FINDINGS:
- X69 RC packet rate in turbodrone defaults to 25 Hz, not 80 Hz (web_server.py:368 default_control_rate = 25.0 in the x69_lg branch; env CONTROL_RATE overrides). That adds up to 40 ms of command quantization to the delay budget and means any 'command at 80 Hz' assumption must set CONTROL_RATE explicitly; nothing in the repo documents whether the X69 firmware tolerates faster rates.
- SAM 3.1 has no local/HF streaming path on the Mac today: huggingface.co/facebook/sam3.1 says 'there is no Hugging Face Transformers integration' (checkpoint-only, gated); transformers PR #46128 'Add SAM 3.1 to Transformers' (opened 2026-05-20) is still open with stale pings 2026-10-21/24. The documented Sam3VideoModel streaming API runs facebook/sam3 (SAM 3). Running real SAM 3.1 weights locally means facebookresearch/sam3 (CUDA 12.6 official) plus unmerged PR #627 on MPS at ~1.3 fps 720p. If the hackathon requires SAM 3.1 specifically, the hosted api.meta.ai endpoint is the only SAM 3.1 that fits the 5-10 Hz budget, and only as an acquisition/re-ID oracle.
- Hosted API practicalities for a live loop: 'stream': true gives SSE with one response.output_text.delta per frame (useful for consuming a clip's results before the whole clip is done); video input must be MP4/MOV containing H.264/H.265/MPEG-4 via URL, base64 or Files API (POST /v1/files, purpose user_data), so a live feed must be muxed into short HEVC clips; each video request bills at least 50 frames ($0.01) while an image request is $0.0025, so periodic single-image re-detection at 5 Hz costs ~$0.75/min; 3,000 RPM (50 RPS) is shared per team; confidence parsing needs @meta-sam/parser >= 0.0.13 or meta-sam-parser >= 0.0.6; boxes are source pixels with w,h, masks are base85 at sub-frame resolution.
- Control-authority details in turbodrone the planner should reuse: while any plugin runs, /ws 'axes' messages are dropped entirely, but takeoff/land/estop/set_speed_index messages are still processed, so the UI kill switch keeps working under autonomy; the follow plugin's no-detection branch uses fc.set_axes() (source 'unknown') rather than set_axes_from('follow'); with default speed_index 1 a normalized pitch of 0.20 becomes 128 + 0.20*127*0.75 = ~147 counts while yaw 0.20 becomes ~153 (unscaled); _scale_axis silently falls back to 0.5 for an unknown speed_index; one-shot flags (takeoff/land/estop/flip/calibrate) are emitted in exactly one packet and auto-cleared.
- The 2025 camera-latency benchmark cited in the summary (abbassnasrallah089-sketch/quadrotor-camera-latency-benchmark) is MATLAB/Simulink (R2024a required, cannot run on this Mac without MATLAB), 0 stars, 4 commits, companion code for a review article 'submitted to Annual Reviews in Control' (not published). It compares a delayed Kalman filter, delayed Luenberger observer, LSTM residual predictor and a neural Luenberger observer; a Smith predictor is never mentioned, so 'rather than a Smith predictor' is the researcher's framing, not the repo's. Delay 160 ms at a 35 ms camera period (nominal 30 Hz).
- SIMC delay margin is larger than the summary implies: with tau_c = theta on an integrating plant the loop stays stable until the true delay reaches ~2.59 theta (+159%), not +59%. Conversely, the chapter gives no derivative-time or measurement-filter rule for integrating/first-order plants (tau_D is 'none' there; theta/3 is an integral-time correction), so the summary's tau_D ~ theta/3 and tau_F <= theta/2 should be treated as unsourced design choices to validate in the simulator, not SIMC guidance.
